# Shared documentation drift

Find accumulated documentation problems across a repository, then turn verified proposals into one reviewable change. The shared operation has audit and resolve modes.

For example, an old guide links to a moved source file. The local scanner flags the link; the shared workflow reads the guide and current source before proposing the correction. A stale audit PR gets checked again before its change is reused.

| Shared in vh-adlc | Retained in the adopting repository |
| --- | --- |
| Audit and resolution procedure, finding dispositions and concise reports | Scanner implementation, roots, detector exceptions and thresholds |
| Recheck proposed edits against current source and preserve human decisions | Documentation styles, generated indexes, site and visual checks |
| Distinguish a wrong detector from a wrong interpretation | Suppressions, corrections history, schedules and publication permissions |

The scope excludes claim-by-claim docs-truth sweeps and their rolling ledger. Existing docs impact continues to handle the documentation affected by one selected code change.

## Context

### Owner summary

This feature moves reusable whole-corpus documentation auditing and proposal resolution into vh-adlc. Stackweaver supplies its scanner, documentation rules, generators, feedback and delivery policy. Audits account for the declared corpus and ground findings in source evidence. Selected corrections need the applicable permission and independent review of the exact candidate and publication payload. Software defects follow the ordinary repair workflow.

Recovery supports a proven fully applied delivery that someone later edited. Exact approval permits acknowledgement while preserving those edits. The operation records a distinct non-success result, stops business replay and releases its original owned lease after every effect and request is settled. Partial delivery or missing proof leaves recovery pending. Local native/collector fixtures exercise publication, interruption, resume, acknowledgement and cleanup.

Approved execution uses retained component snapshots, exact native clients and isolated profiles. Host upgrades and updater signals do not select a new runtime. Compatible updates need independent full-diff/dependency review, regressions and installed checks. A contract-restoring fix includes an old-fail/new-pass regression. Changes to execution, clients, permissions, decision or publication rules, and uncertain changes, require the affected trials before selecting a successor. Historical records and pins remain available through rollback.

Committed trial setup binds the candidate configuration, worker/callers, expected scenarios, namespace and a portable Git fixture bundle. Setup checks and signals provide no execution authority. Candidate execution, original-artifact verification and actual model/hosted observations remain separate requirements.

Migration starts with the legacy-compatible scanner accounting correction. Actual marketplace installs, model resolve/closure/issue trials, Claude forwarding, hosted advisory/history checks and paired consumer review precede cutover. Cutover drains publishers and records the approved component and policy. Rollback quiesces shared publication before restoring the reviewed legacy routes.

The owner accepted spec R-57 at `b9342ea`. Scan, preparation, runtime/upgrade and original candidate-verification helpers are implemented. Exact checkpoint `1b7e8bc` passed 607 tests and every repository partition. Copilot component review 69 returned two nits, addressed locally. The new publication-binding entry rechecks original source/candidate proof, current native proposal inventory, decision bytes and complete selected resolve diffs before deriving the review target. These bindings grant no effects. Advisory controls pass, and resolve controls include a passing source-bound record and refusals for changed decisions/originals or missing refs. One initial candidate-verification failure remains unexplained after 20 passing isolated repetitions; fuller failure diagnostics are retained for the combined checks. Normal effect execution, recovery/acknowledgement and post-merge closure assembly, current non-Git and generation observations, original-artifact operator verification and actual installed/model/hosted adoption remain open. Shipment and live model/provider/consumer operations require their separate approvals.


### Terms used here

| Term | Meaning | Existing criterion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Docs-drift audit | Account for the declared documentation corpus, report candidates and distinguish observed verification from unknowns. | AC-01, AC-02 |
| Prepare | Make only the selected eligible corrections in an owned candidate under the current permission. | AC-03, AC-05 |
| Resolve | Recheck selected proposals and feedback against current source, then classify every selected change. | AC-04 |
| Advisory | The exact independently reviewed PR handed off for the project's normal review and merge process. | AC-05 |
| New attention | A new or materially changed unresolved decision or verification item requiring owner input. | AC-05 |
| Carried attention | An unresolved item whose complete current binding matches eligible earlier evidence. | AC-05 |
| Carried delivery | A freshly supported correction already present in the exact open reviewed proposal. | AC-05 |
| Delivery acknowledgement | Explicitly approved non-success recovery disposition for a proven applied delivery whose current payload differs. | AC-05 |
| Adoption | The recorded, verified switch to the shared execution route under the consumer's scoped authority. | AC-06 |
| Trial | A separately authorized synthetic observation of the required installed and hosted execution paths. | AC-06, AC-07 |

## Scope

Recovery includes a separately approved delivery acknowledgement that preserves a changed remote resource and permits exact-owned lease cleanup after proof of original ownership and settled execution.

Include one harness-agnostic docs-drift operation with audit and resolve modes, a consumer configuration/scan-result contract, reports, bounded verification helpers, examples and Stackweaver migration tests.

Audit consumes a local scan and feedback history, verifies candidates against current source and prepares a concise report. An explicitly authorized prepare run may also produce bounded safe edits for a consumer-owned advisory workflow. Resolve rechecks selected old proposals, deduplicates them and prepares their verified subset plus a ranked decision list.

Keep the scanner and detector semantics local. Preserve Stackweaver's current command, advisory scheduling, source/index/visual rules, historical exemptions and feedback files through thin forwarding instructions. Preserve schedule cadence, the scanning provider/model, credential ownership and the existing notification mechanism/settings. Apply the owner-selected scheduled status policy below: unchanged previously surfaced attention can finish green with its report; new attention and actual failures remain non-successful. The explicit migration changes are the bounded scanner-completeness correction and local regression, preserving detector rules and legacy output; consumer reusable-worker, dispatch-only advisory-trial and read-only history-check workflow files; protected report-history settings and explicit Actions read permission; the configured `.vh-adlc/docs-drift/resolutions/` root for committed records created by separately selected authorized resolve runs; explicitly authorized retained metadata-only receipt refs for every publisher; the independent candidate-reviewer policy and owner-selected new-attention versus carried-attention status distinction; consumer `scripts/docs-drift-entry.ts` launcher and `runtime_loader`; separately owned per-host source/client caches and runtime profiles with normal credential provisioning; the Claude command's move to dispatching tested bound external worker/reviewer roles; exact provider-client build selection; reusable-worker secret-name mappings; mandatory CI signal consistency and updater configuration with automerge false; and authorized pause/drain/switch of the existing scheduled route. These are intentional execution/setup differences, not a claim that installing a skill alone migrates every host. Those changes do not authorize credential rotation, a different scanning model or an additional notification mechanism.

Excluded: docs-truth/sharding, a new universal scanner, detector-rule changes, product code repair, unrestricted autonomous publication, a replacement for feature acceptance/review gates, and migration of unrelated agents or active drift PRs. Live backlog resolution requires a separate selected run after adoption.

## Design

### Owner decisions

The owner selected the bounded scanner-completeness correction, independent review before publication and staged scheduled-worker cutover. These choices are part of the Design and Test Plan, not implementation evidence. The scanner correction changes collection accounting and error reporting only; detector rules, thresholds and project exceptions stay local. The legacy scheduled worker remains active until a real unattended trial of the installed shared worker succeeds. Interactive adoption may precede that trial, but full scheduled adoption and this feature's migration criterion remain incomplete until the trial and switch are verified. For later upgrades, the owner selected independent review and installed checks for compatible changes. The accepted contract-based compatible boundary and complete trial-trigger list in Upgrades below govern this decision; that list includes protected execution, identity, secret mapping, lease and supersession/closure behavior. Local detector rules, ordinary scan scope/options and feedback use normal assessment freshness when they preserve that contract. The owner also selected closure of superseded proposals only after the consolidated replacement merges. On 2026-11-06, the owner selected green scheduled runs for unchanged unresolved items already surfaced in an open PR or earlier report, while new attention and actual failures stay red. Green reports still list those unresolved items. This report/status contract follows the same compatible-versus-contract-changing upgrade rules below. This does not waive the first-adoption trial or authorize paid runs.

### Entry and consumer contract

Add `vh-adlc-docs-drift` as a content operation with explicit audit and resolve intent. Read local instructions and resolve the existing pinned conventions. Keep the canonical procedure independent of model, shell-session persistence and harness-specific tool names. Missing prerequisites produce a setup handoff, never reconstruction of the retired agent.

Use `.vh-adlc/docs-drift.yaml` and the verified binary's strict YAML reader exported by `scripts/settings-file.ts`. For committed assessment or current-default policy, obtain the exact Git blob bytes and pass them to `readYAML`/`createYAMLReader`; hash those same bytes. Do not call the working-tree `settingsFile` reader to stand in for a remote/default blob. In an owned clean checkout whose bytes match its bound commit, `settingsFile` may enforce local naming/suffix conventions. No dirty or candidate policy substitutes for the default-branch gate. A docs-drift validator owns its schema/version and meanings; no second YAML parser or alternative file suffix is added. PR discovery reuses the GET-only paginated `scripts/github-read.ts` reader and preserves its completeness/error observations. Incomplete selected PR collection is an assessment gap and cannot authorize resolution or superseded-PR closure.

Factor the hardened Git environment and byte-read runner from `scripts/docs-impact/collection.ts:26-31,87-92` into neutral `scripts/git-read.ts`, used by docs impact and every docs-drift read/snapshot/fetch. Remove inherited repository/index/object/alternate/shallow/namespace and injected-config variables, disable replace objects, promisor lazy fetching and optional locks, disable fsmonitor, and force literal pathspecs. Preserve raw bytes and NUL path inventories; only validated scalar IDs may be decoded/trimmed. The scanner subprocess and its Git children receive those same trusted controls, including fixed configuration controls where flags cannot be passed through; inherited config injection is never retained. Fetch is an explicit operation only in the recorded owned store, with no caller refs/FETCH_HEAD or automatic lazy fetch/submodule/maintenance side effects. Missing required Git capability/objects/history produces an incomplete assessment without network repair or caller mutation. Hash raw bytes with Node's standard SHA-256 primitive in the protocol utility; do not import audit's Git wrappers or its settings/inventory graph. Preserve docs-impact's public behavior while extracting the common hardened primitive. Protocol digests use the byte-ordered canonicalizer defined below, not audit's locale-dependent `digest` ordering. Add that small protocol utility within `scripts/docs-drift/`; it contains no YAML parser or detector logic. Do not call its audit-specific `captureSnapshot`, which requires a persistent finding ledger and code-audit modes. The new `scripts/docs-drift/settings.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `scan.ts`, `report.ts`, `adoption.ts`, `bootstrap.ts` and `cli.ts` provide docs-drift validation, input binding, scan checks, report formatting and installed adoption consistency. Add `publish.ts`, `transport.ts`, `journal.ts` and `recovery.ts` for the single effect path described below. Add `report-history.ts` for prior-report provenance and bounded artifact/local-history reads; keep GitHub JSON metadata in the shared GET-only reader and binary archives on its separate fixed download path. They do not implement structural detectors. Do not import maintenance or audit `adoptionPreflight` directly: those helpers hard-code their own operation name, helper stages and CLI paths, while audit also binds its ledger policy and mode effects. The new adoption helper has separate operator-verification and runtime-gate modes. Operator verification follows their artifact-hash/payload/consumer/native-install consistency pattern with docs-drift evidence. Runtime checks use the portable committed attestation contract below, without a private operator artifact root. Both retain the operator-attestation limitation; neither grants permission or enforced truth proof.

Add a lifecycle route for whole-corpus documentation drift and stale documentation-audit proposals. “Scan all docs for accumulated drift” selects audit; “recheck and consolidate these docs-audit PRs” selects resolve. “Finish closing the audit proposals consolidated in merged PR #N” selects resolve's explicit post-merge sub-mode, not another advisory scan or an automatic replay of edits. “Which docs does this patch affect?” stays docs impact, a plain doc edit stays artifact editing, and persistent code-audit intent stays `vh-adlc-audit`. A request to verify claims exhaustively stays the selected local truth workflow or gets an unsupported-operation handoff. Ambiguous “audit” or “audit the docs” without enough context gets one focused question before any scan or write. The presence of settings never chooses the operation.

Ordinary locked-binary setup precedes strict settings and knowledge; this docs-drift content operation does not use persistent code audit's special offline bootstrap. Required pages are `conventions/feature-documents.md`, `conventions/documentation.md`, `conventions/documentation-impact.md`, `conventions/delivery.md` and `guides/humanize-prose.md`, plus applicable consumer language/project pages. Missing required knowledge stops with the existing missing-page/pin handoff. The loop's unpinned-content-operation rule adds no onboarding files: use the installed plugin revision, report the missing repository pin and leave onboarding to a separate authorized setup. Missing required consumer settings also gets setup instructions; do not create policy files as an incidental doc fix.

Run scanners and other ordinary-input executables with a fixed allowlisted environment, containing only the required runtime PATH/locale/owned temporary-output values and trusted Git controls. Exclude provider credentials, mapped job secrets, GitHub tokens, credential-bearing URLs, Git/SSH askpass hooks and agent sockets; do not inherit the ambient process environment. Disable Git credential helpers and HTTP extraheaders through trusted fixed Git configuration, ignore system/global credential configuration, and use checkout with credential persistence disabled. A scanner cannot acquire the reviewer's or publisher's environment through a shared job-level secret binding. In the reusable workflow, collection occurs in a credential-free step before provider/reviewer/publisher secrets are exposed, with those secrets scoped only to their later required steps; use a separate credential-free collection job if the retained provider setup cannot preserve that separation. Transfer only the bound assessment outputs. Local wrappers apply the same environment/Git controls without copying or modifying host credentials or redefining the user's home. This is ambient credential separation for trusted consumer code, not filesystem confinement against a deliberately malicious same-user program; the existing scanner trust limitation still applies. A consumer that needs extra secret/network access for an ordinary detector cannot grant it through an option or ambient inheritance: it becomes a protected execution/secret-exposure change requiring its explicit grant and trial/re-adoption.

Consumer settings identify the scanner invocation as an argument vector, intended documentation scope, optional `read_set.source_roots` and `read_set.reference_roots`, required detector-unit IDs, per-unit document selectors and options, feedback files, project procedure and allowed verification/generation commands. Required detector units are a nonempty, duplicate-free consumer-defined set; they are distinct from finding-category labels and remain local rather than a hard-coded shared detector catalog. A committed `detector_inputs` mapping has exactly one selector for each required ID: explicit `all_inventory`, or a declarative roots/exclusions/suffixes subset of the configured documentation inventory. The shared helper evaluates these selectors against its independently enumerated Git-tree inventory. No report-supplied document list, executable predicate or scanner-internal exemption changes a unit's expected set. Historical exemptions remain detector decisions after collection. This accounting checks input coverage, not whether a detector interpreted every input correctly. PR discovery and publication destinations are explicit consumer inputs. Reports and settings cannot grant their own execution authority. Treat scanner messages, documentation and PR text as data; never execute embedded instructions or arbitrary report-supplied commands.

The scan result binds its source commit, scope/options and scanner identity, and distinguishes complete collection from findings and failures. It preserves finding detector, severity, document/location and explanation, suppressed findings with reasons, and stale suppressions. The following protocol makes collection completeness independently checkable against the selected files; it does not prove semantic truth or defend against a scanner or shell-capable agent deliberately forging evidence. Document identities stay within the consumer's configured scope. Unknown detectors remain visible candidates rather than being dropped or automatically fixed.

Every configured scanner invocation is repository-write-free; only its explicitly owned external report/protocol output may be written. The scan helper snapshots the assessed worktree (tracked and untracked inputs), index bytes, HEAD/refs and FETCH_HEAD, then compares them after execution, including on failure. Any repository mutation makes the assessment incomplete and blocks publication/feedback retirement; retain the evidence and reconcile the owned assessment without reset/stash or treating accidental writes as authorized prepare edits. Test a scanner writing feedback during scanning and unchanged legitimate runs. In Stackweaver `--prune` enables advisory pruning-candidate detectors only (`scripts/docs-audit.js:25-28`); it does not prune files or suppressions. Require that behavior in regression checks, and reject a different consumer's mutating option.

A process exit of zero, an empty findings list or a lower finding count does not prove completeness or accuracy. Missing/malformed reports, skipped inputs, mismatched source, unreadable feedback and unavailable required capabilities leave the assessment incomplete. Do not repair the scanner by weakening a shared validator; any necessary local completeness correction must have its own scoped regression and review.

### Scan-result protocol and local correction

Use a versioned JSON result with `schema_version: 1`, `source_commit`, `settings_digest`, scanner identity (script digest and argument vector), declared roots/exclusions/detector options, an examined-document inventory, collection failures, per-detector execution outcomes, findings, suppressed findings and stale suppressions. Required fields and types are validated; duplicate document identities, unknown protocol versions, malformed entries or a changed binding are incomplete. The settings contract defines the documentation suffixes and scope; the initial example uses tracked Markdown under its configured roots. It never substitutes settings or scope supplied by the report itself.

The shared scan helper independently enumerates the expected documents from the exact Git tree and the committed configuration's scope/exclusions. Each in-scope document must have exactly one inventory entry binding its normalized relative path and content digest, with a read-success or failure outcome. Unexpected paths, omissions, duplicate entries, escapes, symlinks, changed content or explicit read/parse failures prevent a complete result. Legitimate configured exclusions remain explicit in the bound scope; an unexpected read failure cannot be relabelled an exclusion. Detector-specific historical exemptions remain detector policy after collection, not a way to hide unexamined inputs. Missing required detector execution is a gap even if all document reads succeeded; its explicit v1 accounting is defined below.

For each configured required detector unit, the v1 result includes exactly one execution entry with its ID, examined document path/digest inventory, bound input digest and `complete` or `failed` outcome, plus an error on failure. The helper independently derives the applicable set from committed `detector_inputs` and requires exact equality with the unit's examined inventory; a complete unit over a strict subset is incomplete even if every document was read globally. A successful unit with no applicable files is complete with an explicit zero-input count; it is not silently omitted. Missing, duplicate, skipped, failed or differently bound required units make the assessment incomplete. Additional unknown finding categories remain visible as candidates; they do not replace missing required execution. The bounded Stackweaver correction emits this execution accounting as well as document accounting, without changing detector rules. Tests include all documents read but a required detector absent or throwing, and require incomplete despite a lower finding count.

Protocol v1 uses lowercase SHA-256 hex of raw content bytes, not a Git blob OID or decoded/normalized prose. Successful document entries must match the bytes of their blob at `source_commit`; failed reads record a failure and a null digest when no bytes were obtained. Paths use repository-relative Git-tree identities with `/` separators, preserving case, Unicode and literal filename characters; reject invalid UTF-8, absolute paths and `.`/`..` traversal rather than replacing or aliasing names. Never reinterpret a literal backslash as a separator. Sort applicable document entries by their UTF-8 path bytes. A detector input digest is SHA-256 of canonical UTF-8 JSON with exactly these keys: `source_commit`, `settings_sha256`, `scope_sha256`, `feedback_sha256`, `detector_id`, `options` and `documents`. Settings and each feedback-file digest are SHA-256 of raw Git blob bytes, never a parsed YAML value. `scope_sha256` hashes the canonical object with exactly `roots`, `exclusions`, `suffixes`, `required_detectors`, `detector_inputs` and `read_set` (sets and per-selector path/suffix sets sorted by UTF-8 bytes); `read_set` is exactly `{source_roots, reference_roots}`, each a byte-sorted relative-path prefix set or null when unspecified. Settings bind these roots and the same object joins the scan scope digest. An omitted/incomplete source/reference bound defaults the assessment read set to the whole tracked repository; negative/absence searches and always-bound settings/worker/feedback/generator inputs cannot be excluded. `feedback_sha256` hashes the sorted `[path, raw_blob_sha256]` array; `documents` is the sorted applicable `[path, content_sha256]` array independently derived from the committed unit selector, and must equal that unit's examined inventory. Canonical JSON has no insignificant whitespace, sorts every object key recursively by UTF-8 bytes without locale comparison, preserves array order except the specified set/path sorts, and uses JSON string/finite-number serialization. Reject invalid encodings, unpaired surrogates and unsupported/non-finite values. The scanner/adapter must read/hash Git blob bytes, or refuse a checkout whose filters/EOL conversion changed them. Cross-implementation golden fixtures cover mixed-case and non-ASCII option keys and filtered checkouts. Required Git-history or other bound inputs must be available; an unavailable input makes that detector failed/incomplete, not a zero-findings success.

The helper supplies the scanner's metadata through one owned canonical JSON file, not YAML parsing in Node or duplicated settings constants. After strict verified-reader settings resolution and exact Git snapshot collection, `scan.ts` writes the canonical source/settings/scope/feedback bindings, detector selectors/options and independently computed expected unit input digests to an external owner-only file. It contains no self-digest. A shared protocol run passes `--protocol-bindings <owned-bindings>` with `--protocol-json <owned-output>`; the scanner reads JSON using Node built-ins, computes/echoes that file's raw SHA-256 and expected binding fields, and separately records its actual examined inventories, applied options, outcomes and failures. It cannot change its fixed roots or execute commands from this metadata. The helper rehashes the file, checks actual source/options/inventories against its independent snapshot and recomputes every bound digest before accepting v1; an echoed digest alone never establishes collection. Missing/malformed/changed bindings, unknown IDs or applied-option disagreement is incomplete. The scanner need not implement the helper's canonicalizer or read YAML. Golden tests cover helper canonical binding generation, scanner echo/raw-byte accounting and helper recomputation, including malicious/mismatched inputs. Legacy invocations without the new protocol flags retain their existing output and semantics.

An initial assessment uses a clean committed source. After authorized edits, the verification scan binds a new authorized local checkpoint commit; it includes any added/deleted documentation and regenerated outputs. Before that checkpoint, reports may describe a provisional diff, but cannot establish publication readiness. This avoids claiming committed-source verification over unrelated dirty or unrecorded document overlays.

Include the minimal Stackweaver correction in this migration: `scripts/docs-audit.js` records read/parse outcomes and document inventory instead of silently continuing on read failure, and emits the bound v1 protocol only through a separate explicit output/adapter invocation, such as paired `--protocol-bindings <owned-bindings>` and `--protocol-json <owned-output>`. Preserve the existing default and `--json` legacy report fields/types/meaning and advisory/strict exits for the unchanged legacy scheduled/interactive readers. Do not replace the legacy report with v1 or add fields a strict legacy reader rejects. The new accounting may be internal and exported separately; detector/suppression results stay unchanged. Its existing advisory/strict exit semantics and detector findings remain unchanged; consumers inspect protocol completeness separately. Before trial/interactive shared adoption, deliver this narrowly reviewed scanner compatibility prerequisite under separate consumer publication authority while both legacy routes remain active. Test the unchanged scheduled auditor, interactive resolver and frozen rollback target against the corrected scanner's legacy output as well as the new v1 output; rollback must not need to revert the correction. Add meaningful local regressions in `scripts/docs-audit.test.js` and migration contract tests in `tests/agent-docs-drift/`: inject an unreadable/skipped document, observe the old omission, then require an explicit failure/inventory mismatch and an incomplete shared result. Compare ordinary findings and suppression behavior before/after to detect unintended rule changes. Independently review the exact bounded correction candidate before its delivery, which precedes the trial and interactive shared adoption; migration approval does not authorize applying its real findings.

### Audit and preparation

Use one exact owned assessment tree for the scan, source inspection and proposed changes. Recheck the source and selected inputs before acting; preserve unrelated dirty work. Read relevant prior corrections and suppressions before adjudicating a finding.

For every candidate, inspect the document and supporting code and record confirmed drift, still accurate or unknown, with evidence and a recommendation. Recency, an existing audit diff and a detector category do not establish truth. Keep intended contracts, historical records, deliberately incomplete matrices and other-system references intact. A product bug is a separate finding, not permission to rewrite the documented intended contract.

Standard feature-document protection is explicit: prepare/resolve never edits Scope, Design, Acceptance Criteria, Test Plan or normative `covers` in a standard feature document. It also never edits Review Log rows/subsections, criterion Evidence/Residual blocks, Task evidence/historical completion records, or the recorded provenance in Context/Sources of standard feature documents. Treat Context/Sources conservatively as report-only when a current statement cannot be separated unambiguously from dated evidence; if protected ranges cannot be identified safely, preserve the entire document. Stale paths in returned FINDING/CRITERION records or old command evidence remain byte-identical. Route proposed corrections/annotations to the owner or claiming feature writer; selecting a docs correction does not grant authority to rewrite historical proof. It never edits any part of a `branch`-claimed feature document on its own drift branch. Report the proposed correction and route normative revisions to `vh-adlc-spec` or the existing claiming writer with the required handoff; do not change status to bypass that route. Use `resolveRoots` from `scripts/docs-impact/collection.ts` on the exact owned committed assessment for `feature_root`/`local_format`. Trust those values only when HEAD is present, diagnostics are empty and outcome is `declared` or `absent`. Missing HEAD, an incomplete outcome or any repository/policy/reader/roots/root/symlink/filename diagnostic makes the whole assessment incomplete and permits no prepare/resolve edits anywhere in the corpus or publication. Never use its default/partly parsed feature root after an error. This deliberately also blocks on unrelated declared docs-impact-root defects until setup is corrected; report that reason rather than silently repairing another operation's policy. A repository with no docs-impact roots declaration may use a clean `absent` result and the resolved feature classification. Factor/export its existing strict frontmatter reader as `readDocumentMetadata` so docs impact and docs drift read the same bytes with the existing YAML reader, returning the strictly parsed mapping or absent frontmatter without branch/status validation; do not add a parser. Docs impact keeps its existing covers-only validation and absent-frontmatter behavior; branch/status validation belongs only to the docs-drift protection layer. A regression must preserve docs-impact candidates/gaps for metadata it previously accepted. Reuse `compileCoverage` from `scripts/docs-impact/matching.ts`. Add a feature dialect to that same engine preserving the gate's `internal/doc/covers.go:MatchCovers` semantics: literal directory prefixes and path.Match-style valid patterns, with unsupported double-star/malformed standard declarations reported as gaps. Keep the existing documentation dialect/default unchanged. Shared JSON fixtures in `tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/` exercise the feature dialect alongside Go `MatchCovers` in `internal/doc/covers_test.go`, including literal wildcard characters in directory prefixes, escapes and Unicode. Use the binary's existing `fresh` result for actual review staleness; matching alone never grants feature ownership or replaces its gate. Local-format consumers keep their project governance, documented matching dialect, plan rules and ownership gates. Source presence alone still cannot satisfy runtime acceptance checks. In a local-format repository, prepare/resolve may edit a plan, review or evidence document only within ranges the retained project procedure explicitly identifies as editable. If that procedure does not classify the document or range, report the correction without editing it; unknown applicability cannot authorize a history rewrite. Tests include a planned standard spec with stale Design, stale normative coverage and a claimed in-progress document, all byte-preserved with the correct handoff.

Also inventory ordinary document paths covered by other features. Report every affected feature and the resulting implementation-review staleness; changing an ordinary covered doc does not silently preserve a fresh implementation review or reopen a terminal feature. If a path belongs to an in-progress/other active claimed feature or another current writer, do not edit it on the drift branch without the claiming writer's handoff or the owner's explicit coordinated selection of that overlap. Otherwise retain it as a proposal to that writer. Closed historical feature coverage is reported as historical staleness, not treated as a permanent reservation of every covered document; selected new work still preserves the old feature's scope/status and follows its own review. Test a guide covered by an in-progress claimed feature, an explicitly coordinated case and a document covered only by a closed feature, alongside the protected feature-document cases above.

Report mode leaves consumer docs, feedback, indexes, Git index and refs unchanged, saving a report only at an explicitly selected output location. An authorized prepare run edits only confirmed, bounded corrections within its owned scope. Moves, deletions, merges, pruning, ambiguous fixes and substantive plan/status decisions require explicit selection. Feature documents retain their own review/gate rules; source presence alone does not establish runtime criteria or authorize ticking them.

The owner-facing report starts with a short explanation, proposed/applied fixes and decisions with recommendations. Keep the full per-finding evidence and unknowns available below that summary. Never claim all findings were verified when only a subset was inspected. Where a harness cannot delegate independent nontrivial checks, record the missing verification and retain the affected recommendation as provisional.

### Resolution and feedback

Resolve discovers only the selected consumer/PR scope, reads complete proposal bodies and diffs, and takes a fresh scan from the current owned base. It classifies every selected hunk as apply, apply-modified, already fixed, wrong or defer. Deduplicate identical proposals; conflicting suggestions require current evidence, not a newest-wins rule. Preserve uncertain proposals in the decision report.

Complete proposal input means more than the paginated PR list. Obtain repository/PR identity, full body, exact head SHA and observed base SHA through the GET reader. In a recorded owned assessment object store, read-only-fetch the selected target repository's exact PR head and base objects, verify the head matches the observed metadata, derive their merge base and compute the full local Git diff including the complete file inventory, additions/deletions, renames and any binary blob identities. Do not use optional/truncated REST `patch` fields as authoritative hunks. Fetch objects only into that owned store (without changing caller refs/index or checking out its files), from the verified consumer origin and its fixed PR ref; never follow a report/body-supplied clone URL. Bind the proposal to repository, PR ID, body digest, head/base/merge-base SHAs and diff digest, and recheck them before effects.

Missing objects/history, a changed head, unreadable or truncated diff, omitted file/hunk or exceeded processing bound makes that proposal incomplete. It contributes no applied hunks and cannot be closed. Other proposals can still be inspected, but the selected run cannot claim complete readiness by silently dropping an incomplete member; get complete inputs or an explicit scope reselection. Binary changes require a concrete disposition and the consumer's actual asset checks rather than pretending a missing textual patch is no change. Test large diffs, omitted REST patches, unavailable objects, changed PR heads and incomplete file inventories with caller refs/index preserved.

Rebuild the verified subset on a fresh owned branch instead of replaying entire stale audit commits. Report which source proposals contribute which exact changes. Approval for one decision does not approve the rest. Revalidate source, proposals and approved decisions after material changes.

A selected proposal is superseded only when every hunk has a reviewed disposition: applied/apply-modified in the merged consolidated PR, already fixed with current evidence, wrong with the appropriate feedback delivered, or explicitly carried as a deferral/owner decision in that merged PR's decision list. Uncarried deferred or undecided hunks keep the original proposal open. Superseded closure requires separate actual closure authority and happens only after the consolidated PR is observed merged and a fresh full-diff closure review verifies its actual merged head against the immutable resolution record, including selected hunks, feedback and carry-forward. This uses the reproducible closure basis below rather than claiming equality with an unavailable historical review target. Merely opening/publishing the replacement is insufficient. Post an authorized explanation linking that merged PR and the per-hunk dispositions before closing. An abandoned or closed-unmerged replacement leaves every original proposal open. Publication/merge failure, missing feedback or incomplete carry-forward denies closure; this workflow never grants merge authority by proposing supersession. For closure, first load and freshly review the durable resolve record through the post-merge entry described below. GET-observe `merged: true`, the consolidation's actual merged head SHA bound by this fresh closure review, its merge-result commit, and the selected original proposal heads/bodies. Support every merge method allowed by the consumer, including squash and rebase; do not require the reviewed head to be an ancestor of default. Bind each delivered correction/feedback file to its expected postimage raw-blob SHA, including expected absence for a deletion, and carry-forward to the full consolidation-body digest freshly reviewed for this closure batch, rather than assuming an old mutable PR body is still approved. Verify these postimages at the observed merge-result tree. Then read current default, require the merge-result commit to be reachable there and compare the full delta from the resolution record's assessed/publication base through current default against the same bounded assessment read set used for publication. This covers default advances before publication, while the PR is open, and after merge, for squash, rebase and merge commits alike. Treat only the consolidation's declared correction/feedback paths with exact recorded postimages/absences, plus its unchanged resolve-record blob, as the expected transition. Do not subtract the whole merge result or all default-side changes merely because the merge succeeded. Recheck required history inputs over that full interval too. Any other bound-input change invalidates the old closure evidence and takes the limited fresh closure-only reassessment; unavailable ancestry/delta/history cannot pass. Disjoint changes may proceed. In particular, reverify every already-fixed disposition and the source evidence behind applied hunks when an intervening default commit changes those inputs, even if no declared touched-file postimage changed. A later default-branch bound-input change, unavailable later-delta comparison or consolidation-body change invalidates the current closure batch and requires a fresh closure-only assessment/review at current default. That reassessment may proceed only once comparison is complete, recorded postimages/dispositions still verify and the current consolidation body preserves every recorded carry-forward; obtain fresh approval for a materially changed explanation. It does not require inventing another docs PR. In contrast, changed original proposal head/body bindings or an actual merged diff with unlisted paths or mismatched postimages deny closure under this record and leave the affected originals open with a recovery handoff for a separately selected resolution. Closure-only reassessment cannot rewrite the record, substitute new original inputs or approve a changed merged postimage. A changed historical consolidation head SHA alone is not proof of changed content: fresh full-diff/record verification decides; any head/body change after that fresh review still invalidates its batch. A later revert or removed carry-forward keeps affected originals open. The delivered consolidation delta itself is the expected transition, not a reason to invalidate its own review. Under the lease immediately before each authorized explanation/closure, recheck the observed default, proposal identities/heads/bodies and consolidation bindings; a changed value stops the effect sequence. Report-only runs merely propose this sequence.

If every selected hunk is already fixed, or is wrong with the required feedback already delivered, resolve still needs a merged consolidation before superseded closure. Under the actual prepare/publication grant, build a candidate containing only the versioned resolve record, with no documentation/feedback correction; the reviewed PR body carries all per-hunk dispositions and current evidence. Its declared changed-path set is empty apart from the separately permitted record path. Review and publish that exact record-only candidate through the same gates, and leave every original open until it merges and the fresh closure checks pass. This record is part of the pre-review candidate, never a post-review evidence commit. Report-only or missing write/PR authority leaves a local proposal/handoff and the originals open; it does not silently close them or create a PR. Apply the same rule when all remaining hunks are explicitly carried decisions rather than edits; incomplete or uncarried undecided inputs still cannot establish readiness. This is resolve's zero-correction outcome; advisory scans keep their separate no-change report behavior.

Before the publication checkpoint, an authorized resolve preparation creates one versioned record at the configured `.vh-adlc/docs-drift/resolutions/<id>.yaml` path in that same candidate tree. It contains repository identity, assessed base, selected original PR IDs/head/body digests, every hunk's disposition and evidence, touched-file postimage hashes/absences (excluding this record itself), carried decisions, bounded read-set/history inputs and selected verification and the exact declared changed-path set. It contains neither its own future checkpoint ID nor the future consolidation PR ID/body digest, native review result or a self-digest. Its raw Git-blob digest is therefore stable before checkpoint review. Include it in the owned diff and independent candidate review; the published PR links its path/digest as a locator after the checkpoint is known. No record-only commit is added after that review.

A fresh post-merge session can start with only the consolidation PR ID and the actual current request. Obtain complete GET identity/head/body/merge inputs and the exact head Git objects; identify the added resolve record through that head's full diff within the configured record root, then verify its blob digest, repository and all bindings against that tree and selected proposals. A PR-body locator is an untrusted hint, not the authority or source of truth; absent, ambiguous, malformed or differently bound records produce a recovery handoff with no closure. A missing private historical artifact does not silently pass. Run a fresh independent closure review using the immutable published checkpoint/resolve record and observed current default, bind the current full PR body, selected dispositions and exact proposed explanation/closure payload, and require the actual current/adopted closure authority. This review can verify the old checkpoint now without claiming to recover or prove its original pre-publication review. That review compares the complete Git diff from the recorded publication base to the actual merged head against the record's exact changed-path set plus the resolve-record path itself, and checks every changed file's postimage/absence. Unlisted paths, extra hunks that change a declared postimage, changed original proposal head/body bindings, or an incomplete diff deny closure under this record and produce a recovery handoff. A fresh closure-only assessment cannot override those mismatches. Later default deltas or the current consolidation body may be reassessed only under the limited retry rule above, with immutable record/original inputs and merged postimages still matching. It does not claim that this actual head equals an unavailable historical pre-publication review target; fresh full-diff review is the closure basis. A later commit added to the consolidation after publication cannot pass merely because it carries the old record. A current head/body/default change after this fresh review invalidates its closure batch. Save its raw records and current evidence in this run's selected report location; no historical private cache is required. The committed record supplies reproducible inputs, never permission to write. On an unattended resume, a consumer must separately adopt the post-merge resolve entry and its closure scope; the scheduled advisory grant alone does not authorize it. Another host follows the same entry, checks and authority rules.

Keep the two feedback channels distinct: a faulty detector gets a narrowly supported suppression; a mistaken interpretation gets a correction-history entry while the detector's real finding remains visible. Repeated mistakes produce a proposed detector/procedure repair. Retiring a stale suppression requires verifying the corresponding scope and detector were actually collected. Report-only runs write neither feedback store.

Apply the consumer's generators and source/visual checks after an authorized edit. Compare finding identities and severity by affected class; a lower aggregate count cannot hide a new or suppressed finding. Never hand-edit generated indexes. Keep feedback changes, proposed documentation changes and selected decisions reviewable together.

### Authority and delivery

Extend `references/authority.md` with two scoped docs-drift locations: an assessment checkout at an exact clean source (an existing owned checkout, or a new detached assessment worktree when isolation is required) and an owned prepare/resolve branch/worktree created for authorized edits. Record repository, canonical path, role, owner, ref/base and artifact location before use. A role grants only ordinary creation and non-forced cleanup of its own clean resources; retain evidence/refs when normal deletion refuses. Never repoint another task's branch, discard unrelated changes or remove another checkout. Branch creation is not commit, push or merge permission. Keep the existing maintenance/audit roles unchanged and extend their guarded worktree rule only for these named docs-drift roles; exercise it in `tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/authority.test.ts` and existing `tests/vh-adlc/audit/intake.test.ts`.

The authority table separately covers read-only assessment/local report output; bounded CI diagnostic report-artifact/job-summary upload; scoped prepare/resolve edits and generators; local commits; pushes and advisory/resolve PRs; the exact-owned consolidation-branch history rewrite for an approved refresh; issue creation; the PR explanation comment; superseded-PR closure; publication-lease ref acquisition; exact-owned lease-ref release; delivery acknowledgement in recovery; receipt-ref create-and-retain; exact-owned receipt-ref retirement; workflow dispatch; workflow disable/enable; cancellation of identified workflow runs; trial draft-PR creation; exact-owned trial PR/branch cleanup; merged-branch cleanup; and merge/release effects. Delivery acknowledgement requires the owner's actual current approval of the exact presented lookup scope and evidence digests. A standing adopted procedure, scheduled or unattended context, or publication, closure, absence-retirement or general cleanup grant cannot supply that approval. Every other effect requires the actual current request or an adopted consumer procedure that names that effect and scope. Generated reports and settings do not confer those permissions. Read-only discovery alone allows no tracker write. Explanation comments need an actual comment grant for the selected payload; a closure grant does not implicitly include it. Lease acquisition and exact-owned release need the selected publication/trial grant to explicitly name those effects and its ref namespace. Receipt-ref creation/retention likewise needs that selected grant to name the retained namespace. Receipt retirement needs a separate cleanup grant naming exact recorded owned refs/objects after their linked proposals and recovery needs finish; it is not lease release or merged-branch cleanup, and a foreign/unrecorded/moved ref cannot be retired under another operation's grant. Workflow dispatch/disable/enable and cancellation require the current cutover/trial grant to name the exact workflow/run and effect. Trial PR creation and its PR/branch cleanup require a separate grant naming the selected destination/namespace and exact owned resources; paid/provider execution needs its own actual authorization. None follows from a read-only sweep or spec acceptance. CI diagnostic uploads require the selected worker/trial grant to name the existing run/repository artifact destination and bounded report scope; a local report request alone does not grant an upload. Local checkpoint commits, publication locking and independent review follow the conditions below; none changes an existing consumer grant by implication.

The PR inspection object store and synthetic trial Git repository are temporary subresources of the named docs-drift assessment role, outside every caller checkout/Git directory. Before creation record the canonical owned root, resource kind (`pr-object-store` or `trial-scratch`), owner/run, selected repository/base/object identities and evidence location. Use a newly owned bare store for PR fetches, with no caller refs or FETCH_HEAD writes (including `--no-write-fetch-head`); never fetch into an existing caller `.git`. The fixture repository likewise uses its own recorded temporary root and the separately authorized trial scope. Only these exact owned resources may be created or normally cleaned under the role; no foreign/unrecorded/symlink-escaped resource or ambiguous nonempty content is removed. Retain failed/ambiguous resources and evidence for reconciliation rather than forcing cleanup. Tests preserve caller objects/index/refs/FETCH_HEAD and reject overlapping or foreign paths.

Shared defaults permit assessment and proposals. Local edits need their selected run's authority; commits, pushes, advisory PRs, issue creation, superseded-PR closure and branch cleanup each retain their actual consumer/current permissions. Advisory publication never authorizes merge or downstream deployment. Do not import maintenance report-push grants or triage's enforced-proof controller into this workflow.

Issue creation is a selected publication mode, never a side effect of discovery. Audit/resolve may propose an issue for a selected unresolved documentation problem or separately identified product defect, with an exact repository/title/body/labels payload. Its body states the work briefly and links a committed/published repository analysis or feature document holding the source finding/PR scope, evidence, dispositions and unknowns, following the pinned documentation convention. Bind the evidence document's repository/path/commit/content digest and review its relevance/freshness with the short payload. Do not use the issue as the evidence database. Before creating it, independently review that exact payload bound to the complete committed assessment, using the adopted reviewer identity and actual issue grant. An issue-only run may reuse an already published current evidence document and target its existing assessed commit without an unrequested local checkpoint. If that document does not exist, prepare a local report/document draft and a publication handoff; do not create the issue until the separately authorized, reviewed document publication is observed at its bound permalink. An issue grant implies no report commit/push/merge authority. Keep runtime payload/review artifacts external and digest-bound; private local artifacts alone cannot be the issue's evidence link. Put this publication under the same current-default staged/adopted gate, source freshness and publication lease as PR publication. Pre-cutover shared real-corpus issues are denied even with a generic issue request, and no scanner/PR text grants issue authority. The ordinary advisory grant does not grant issue creation. Recheck the payload/source/selected scope under the lease; missing/stale review or any changed binding denies creation. This rule concerns docs-drift findings, not lifecycle tracking issues for implementing this feature.

### Common publisher and effect recovery

All hosted and interactive publication uses `scripts/docs-drift/publish.ts` through `cli.ts publish --manifest <owned-manifest>`; post-merge resolve prepares its closure manifest for that same entry. `cli.ts resume-effects --operation <id>` inspects/reconciles an interrupted operation before any authorized remaining action. `transport.ts` owns GitHub/Git observation and effects. The production CLI always constructs its native transport and accepts no report/settings/environment switch to a collector. The separately invoked installed `scripts/docs-drift/trial-entry.ts` test driver may inject a collector into the same publisher API, with an explicit synthetic context and `transport: collector` receipts. Such receipts cannot stand in for live effects or the actual hosted advisory trial; they can satisfy only the designated collector test cases. Both interactive entries and the hosted wrapper invoke the same implementation, never hand-roll a push/API sequence.

Follow the audit journal's write-ahead intent/receipt and `recoverEffect` observe-before-replay pattern (`scripts/audit/journal.ts:10-18,288-298` for effects and `100-110` for header registration, `scripts/audit/recovery.ts:5-25`). Do not directly import its audit-specific settings, finding-ledger/common-dir registry or resource-cleanup schema into an ephemeral hosted job. The docs-drift journal stores a hash-linked local mirror at the selected owned external artifact root. Before any selected push, PR, issue, explanation or closure effect, its complete immutable operation plan is also durable in the owned publication-lease Git object: operation/effect IDs, repository and actor, source/approval/review digests, ordered effects, target/expected head and exact payload hashes, including every byte of the unchanged reviewed PR/issue/comment body. Recovery compares those submitted payload fields; server-generated metadata is not part of the submitted payload hash. Do not store credentials, host paths or private report bodies there. This lease object survives runner loss; adding/changing an effect requires a new reviewed/authorized plan rather than mutating the acquired plan. IDs/hidden correlation markers are included in prepared PR/issue/comment payloads before review; no self-hash or native returned review record is appended to the reviewed payload afterward. Native review evidence stays in external/run artifacts. Each local intent precedes execution and each receipt records actual observed outcome. Lease creation/release themselves have local write-ahead intents and exact-owned remote reconciliation.

Recovery re-observes actual state with complete GET/ref lookups before replay: an exact pushed head is done; a PR/issue/comment with the operation marker, expected actor/target and exact payload is done; terminal closure is recorded as observed without claiming who performed it. A lost response leaves intent pending. Retry only after definitive complete absence, unchanged scope/source/model/approval and current authority, using the same effect ID/payload; changed/multiple/unavailable matches stop for reconciliation. A comment recorded/observed before interruption is never posted twice on an unchanged resume; closing still requires its current explanation and all post-merge preconditions. The next scheduled run cannot take an interrupted foreign/pending lease. If the local mirror/artifact is lost, use the remote immutable intent plan to observe outcomes, but do not replay without the exact payload and original proof/authority being available and verified. Unknown state remains pending and non-success. Release only after all effects are observed terminal or separately authorized reconciliation has accounted for the remaining effects through verified known-absence retirement or delivery acknowledgement under the conditions below. Preserve that handoff and its evidence, including the non-success disposition. Source/actor/payload changes need a new review/authorization and new operation, not reuse of an old comment or grant. Atomic lease release/force-with-lease rechecks remain in force. Test lost PR/issue responses, interruption after explanation before closure, changed/duplicated markers, lost local artifacts, stale scope, and absent replay authority with no duplicate or unintended action.


### Delivery acknowledgement during recovery

The owner approved this addition on 2026-11-07 after the recovery-policy question. The current implementation has distinct observed-outcome and absence-retirement records (`scripts/docs-drift/journal.ts:24-25`). Reconciliation already refuses observation-only mirrors with live leases and requires original ownership before mutations (`scripts/docs-drift/recovery.ts:291,321`). These ownership requirements apply to acknowledgement cleanup.

An acknowledgement records positive evidence that the selected effect applied, together with the resource's current payload mismatch. The owner approved creation effects, PR updates and PR closures on 2026-11-07. Every eligible effect requires the expected repository, original mutating actor, exact resource target and applicable original head/branch bindings. The original mutating actor can differ from the author of a legacy PR. Current matching outcomes continue through ordinary recovery.

| Effect kind | Acknowledgement eligibility and required proof |
| --- | --- |
| `pr-create` | Eligible. One unique native PR with the operation/effect marker, expected creator, exact target and original head/branch bindings. The bound pre-dispatch inventory proves that this marker did not predate the effect. Direct native application evidence binds the created resource to that effect and attempt, proving creation with its full approved payload including every requested label. |
| `issue-create` | Eligible. One unique native issue with the operation/effect marker and expected creator/target. The bound pre-dispatch inventory excludes a pre-existing marker. Direct native application evidence binds the created resource to that effect and attempt. Prove terminal application of the full approved payload, including title, body and every requested label. |
| `comment-create` | Eligible. One unique native comment on the exact parent with the operation/effect marker and expected creator. The bound pre-dispatch inventory excludes a pre-existing marker. Direct native application evidence binds the created resource to that effect and attempt. |
| `pr-update` | Eligible. Direct native mutation evidence identifies the original update, PR ID/number, mutating actor, attempt and applied field values. Bind the original source-field digests, intended transition and expected head/base/branch. Prove terminal application of the full approved payload, including title/body, labels and any requested draft-state change. A current GET showing a different payload alone cannot establish that the selected update applied. A pre-existing marker supplies no update proof. |
| `pr-close` | Eligible. Direct native closure evidence identifies this PR, original mutating actor, selected attempt and original expected head. Verify the separately proven explanation under the existing closure rules. The closure record must establish the selected mutation despite the current reopened state. An explanation marker alone supplies no closure proof. |
| `push` | Ineligible. Preserve the existing exact-ref outcome and reconciliation rules. |
| `branch-refresh` | Ineligible. Preserve the existing exact-head, ownership and reconciliation rules. |
| `lease-acquire` | Ineligible. Preserve the existing original-run acquisition and ownership rules. |
| `receipt-retain` | Ineligible. Preserve the existing exact immutable receipt observation and retention rules. |
| `lease-release` | Ineligible. Preserve the existing exact-owned atomic release and lost-response recovery rules. |

Eligibility requires terminal application evidence covering the entire approved payload of the selected effect. Account for every native request and required component, including every requested PR or issue label and any draft-state change. A settled but partly applied effect remains pending and is ineligible for acknowledgement. Evidence that a request was not dispatched or was rejected proves settlement only. It cannot prove application of that component or authorize abandoning it. A fully applied effect whose payload was subsequently edited remains eligible under the proof table. The owner approved this full-application requirement on 2026-11-07.

Bind native evidence to the original request through the retained bounded native response projection or an independently retrievable native mutation record that establishes the listed resource, actor, applied transition and attempt bindings. A local caller assertion, ambiguous timeline event or current resource snapshot alone cannot prove an update or closure. If the native service supplies no such proof, leave that effect pending. Missing/removed creation markers, duplicate matches, changed target/head/branch/actor, malformed responses and unavailable evidence keep acknowledgement pending. This rule preserves later payload edits while requiring the original target and head bindings to remain valid.

The owner approved bounded private success-evidence retention on 2026-11-07. Extend the native executor and `PublicationTransport.execute` result contract to return or link one integrity-bound artifact for every owned native mutation command. The durable journal links each artifact to its original run, plan/payload digest, effect, attempt, command sequence and exact request digest. Capture HTTP/CLI result status, native resource IDs and applicable target/head bindings, the verified authenticated mutating actor, applied-field digests, label identities and draft state where the native response establishes them. Preserve the existing rejection evidence and settlement distinctions. A success exit code alone establishes no applied-field proof. A later GET cannot be retroactively attributed to the earlier mutation response.

Persist a strict allowlisted response projection with explicit byte/count limits, content digest and owner-only permissions in the operation's external private artifact directory. Body contents are represented only by digests. Exclude raw response bodies, credentials, headers containing secrets and unrestricted stdout/stderr from retained artifacts, reports and remote intent metadata. For mutation capture, extend `scripts/docs-drift/owned-process.ts` to drain stdout/stderr through bounded in-memory pipes and parse the native response transiently. Never redirect these raw streams to retained files or temporary disk paths. Enforce the bounds while draining, retain owned process-group cancellation/reaping and classify interrupted or oversized responses conservatively. A crash between command completion and projection persistence can lose proof but leaves no new raw body or secret-bearing header file. Persist only the allowlisted projection. Extend `scripts/docs-drift/rejections.ts` with versioned projected HTTP rejection evidence and projected Git porcelain ref-status evidence derived from the same bounded transient capture. Preserve definitive-rejection requirements, request/command linkage and integrity checks. Keep the existing reader for already-serialized legacy rejection proofs and their original owned files, without rewriting them or treating them as new success evidence. Their historical file-backed format remains verifiable. New HTTP and Git captures use their projected formats and create no raw-response fallback. For Git push, drain bounded porcelain stdout/stderr through memory too. A definitive Git rejection projection records the complete parsed per-ref rejection statuses and requested ref/object bindings, with command/run/effect/attempt linkage, integrity digests and explicit schema/count/byte bounds. Require complete terminal status for every selected ref under the existing definitive-rejection rules. Unknown, successful, mixed, missing, malformed or truncated statuses cannot become all-rejected proof. Link the projection through the same hash-linked journal evidence path used for HTTP, and revalidate its linkage and digest on recovery. Preserve legacy file-backed Git proof reading alongside legacy HTTP proof reading. Existing files remain verifiable in their original format. New Git captures never use a raw-output file fallback. If capture cannot establish the necessary success or rejection facts, leave the attempt pending. Reject malformed, oversized, missing, tampered, unlinked or unsupported evidence instead of treating it as successful proof. Link evidence through a hash-linked journal record before recovery can use it. Preserve evidence across crashes and retries without allowing another attempt's result to replace it. Losing capture or its durable link after remote success leaves the attempt unproved and pending.

A multi-command effect requires evidence for every command and the complete approved payload. Where a CLI component cannot return sufficient applied-state evidence, use a native mutation response that can establish it or keep that component unprovable and the effect pending. Existing journals remain readable. Pre-capture attempts cannot gain inferred success artifacts from process exit, present-day GETs or old markers. They remain pending unless independently retrievable native records establish every required original request/attempt binding. These artifacts are local integrity-bound attestations under the existing same-user trust model. Their hashes establish retained-byte integrity and linkage, without introducing a stronger execution boundary.

Production acknowledgement requires native evidence. For the already required isolated synthetic trial, the collector's own `execute` produces a transport-labelled success projection from its owned fixture objects. Use the same bounded schema, plan/request/effect/attempt/command bindings, fixture actor identity, applied-field digests, integrity checks, journal links and private permissions. Every success projection explicitly records its transport and context. Collector evidence is admissible only for a sealed plan with `context: trial` and `transport: collector`, through the existing guarded installed synthetic entry. Apply the proof table to its fixture-native records and exact fixture bindings only within that trial. Reject collector evidence in every native or production plan, even if resource IDs and digests otherwise match. Native/production records cannot be relabelled as collector records or vice versa. Synthetic evidence proves fixture procedure coverage and supplies no claim of actual native delivery. The collector does not contact a real endpoint or mutate a real repository.

Collect a new bounded native lookup for the proposed acknowledgement. Present its selected effect/resource IDs, original plan and payload digests, current observed field digests and differences, original run identity, exact lease ref/object, executor/request settlement evidence and the acknowledgement/release effects to the owner. Bind the approval to that complete immutable scope and its evidence digests. Re-read those bindings immediately before recording acknowledgement and again before lease release. Any changed binding requires a new lookup and approval. Permission for publication, closure, absence retirement or a general cleanup request grants none of this new acknowledgement scope by implication. An adopted standing procedure or unattended controller cannot replace the owner's actual current approval of the exact presented lookup and evidence digests.

Require the original verified journal/run ownership proof and current native actor/repository binding. An inferred same-plan lease in a restored inspection supplies read-only observations. An observation-only mirror remains pending while a live lease lacks original ownership proof. Acknowledgement never transfers ownership or takes a foreign lease. The original executor must be quiescent and every owned mutation request must be accounted for through native evidence of non-dispatch, definitive rejection or terminal application. Bind this evidence to the actual run/effect/attempt and owned command. Process exit/reaping, elapsed time, a cancelled workflow, a marker for another effect or a caller-supplied quiescent flag alone cannot settle an unknown server request. Unknown or still-live attempts keep the lease pending. The owner's approval supplies permission; the retained native evidence supplies these facts.

Add a distinct hash-linked local journal event for acknowledgement, with the approved scope and native evidence digests. Its meaning is the observed applied delivery and current mismatch. It supplies no exact-completion or non-application verdict. The original operation permanently stops business replay, even after the resource is restored to its old payload. Account for every remaining effect through current terminal observations, separately approved definitive absence retirement or eligible acknowledgement. Then release only the original run's still-owned exact lease object by the existing atomic comparison. Persist release intent, observe-before-replay recovery and the actual release result. A lost acknowledgement or release response must resume without a duplicate acknowledgement, business replay or deletion of a replacement lease. Preserve the immutable retained operation ref.

The operation and its report remain non-successful after this cleanup. The report names the acknowledged differences, ownership/settlement proof, remaining decisions and observed lease result. Reporting uses its existing separately authorized local/artifact/summary channels. Later work requires a fresh reviewed/authorized operation and explanation under the current rules. Acknowledgement supplies no carried-delivery, corrected-documentation, resolved-hunk or original-proposal-closure proof. Existing assessment and eligible-history rules decide any later carried attention.


### Independent candidate review and publication

Require an independent review of every prepared advisory or consolidated resolve change before publication. Add `references/docs-drift-review.md` using the existing model-identity/isolation/returned-record protocol. The reviewer receives repository, exact candidate commit, assessment/report identity, selected finding/PR/decision scope, consumer policy and verification inputs. It inspects the entire proposed diff including feedback and generated files, checks source-grounded dispositions and authority, and runs or inspects the required checks. Per-claim verification is additional evidence, not a substitute for this candidate review.

A local checkpoint commit precedes review only when the run actually authorizes that commit; a permission to edit does not imply permission to commit. The scheduled prepare worker emits its bounded edit manifest and body without staging. Its authorized local wrapper stages only that owned manifest, makes the checkpoint, runs the read-only independent reviewer and publishes only after a clean matching result. No broad `git add -A` or partial/failed model return becomes success. Record native model identity, exact target and unmodified returned findings with their provenance outside the checkpoint tree, and retain those native records only in selected external/run artifacts through the authorized wrapper. The advisory/resolve PR title, complete body, labels, draft state and target are finalized before independent review and published byte-for-byte unchanged as that prepared payload. A stable pre-review run/report locator may be included, but never append the returned review or a new evidence section afterward. Both production and trial publish the exact independently reviewed candidate commit and tree. No record-only commit is added afterward. Review records and native evidence remain only in separately retained external/run artifacts; any source-tree evidence file must already be present in the reviewed checkpoint. A changed head requires fresh candidate review, even if its only change is described as evidence.

Changed source/diff, settings, scanner/options/scope, feedback, selected PR heads/bodies or owner decision payloads invalidate the review and require revalidation/review. A missing reviewer, model mismatch or blocking/should-fix finding prevents publication. Report/local preparation without publishing effects follows the existing default-review protocol: a current owner-requested model must match; without one the host default may report its identity honestly, including unattested `unknown`. Every adopted real-corpus publication (PR or issue), explanation and closure effect, interactive or unattended, instead requires a committed nonempty `candidate_reviewer.requested_model`; this adopted consumer policy supplies the requested model, selected without fallback. The fresh post-merge closure review uses that same expected identity. Bind it in the trial definition and adoption record. Compare native returned `MODEL:` using the protocol's family/full-ID rule; different or `unknown` identities deny all those remote effects. A current request for a different reviewer can support reports or an authorized re-adoption trial, not override the current real-corpus gate. This field records the owner-selected local reviewer; extraction does not change provider/model selection or invent a shared default. Missing model policy is a setup handoff before the trial, not an instruction to spend or alter credentials. Reports remain available with their limits. This is an operator-reviewed workflow, not a new enforced-proof controller or an implicit permission to make remote changes.

Shared consolidation has an explicit consumer constraint: no default-branch update merge is supported between its recorded publication base and merged head. Require `git rev-list --merges <recorded-base>..<head>` to be empty and the recorded base to be an ancestor; missing history cannot pass. GitHub Update branch/merge-from-default after candidate review therefore needs a fresh clean preparation against the new default, updated resolve record, complete assessment and independent review, followed only by an authorized PR update. If the consumer requires an up-to-date branch, perform that refresh before merge; do not bypass branch protection or silently rebase/amend a reviewed checkpoint. A disjoint default advance may still permit publication of the unchanged candidate when consumer policy permits it. If an unsupported update merge is already in the merged consolidation, post-merge closure stops with that reason and a recovery handoff; it does not subtract paths heuristically or auto-close old proposals. This preserves the full declared-diff check, while making the limitation explicit. Test this separately from squash/rebase merge methods, which remain supported for an unchanged/re-reviewed clean consolidation head.

Delivery of a refreshed clean candidate to an existing consolidation PR uses only an explicitly authorized exact-owned non-fast-forward branch update. The consumer/current grant must name the history rewrite, target repository/PR/branch, expected replaced head and new reviewed candidate; ordinary edit/push permission is insufficient. Under the publication lease recheck origin, PR identity/ownership, still-open state and remote branch head against the previously recorded published head. Push the exact newly reviewed checkpoint with `--force-with-lease=refs/heads/<owned-branch>:<expected-old-head>`; never use bare force or an implicit tracking-ref lease. A foreign/new head or changed owner/state refuses before effects, and the atomic expected-head check also catches a race after observation. Preserve the old head and review artifacts. This updates the same PR; it neither creates/closes a replacement consolidation nor closes originals early. Without this precise rewrite grant, leave the refresh local and report the handoff. Test success, no grant, stale/replaced branch head, foreign ownership and interrupted lease with no unintended writes.
