A compatible pin bump adds the new immutable component pin and changes only the active caller's declared `component_pin` selection plus the adoption-record selection, and refreshes the non-executing upgrade signal to the verified new source/release. This mechanical pin selection is the explicit caller-contract exception for compatible re-adoption; pin bytes/new component identity are reviewed and tested separately. Pin-only upgrades keep the reusable-worker blob and every non-pin caller field unchanged. The compatible refactor/fix exception covers the shared docs-drift component payload, including the skill entry, audit/resolve and reviewer procedures, routing/settings/review references and helper code, when independent review, regressions and installed checks establish the unchanged accepted contract. This does not exempt a changed protected execution binding. It never covers the consumer reusable GitHub-workflow blob or protected caller/execution fields. That workflow blob and all non-pin install/runner/environment/permission/secret/worker-reviewer-publisher invocation/step fields must remain exact; any change to them takes the live trial route even when described as behavior-preserving. The only caller exception is the mechanical component-pin selection already defined. The compatible successor binds new component-internal hashes and their independently reviewed old-to-new contract mapping, without pretending the old trial ran those bytes. Changes to the accepted decision rules/contracts/effect authority, or uncertainty, take the full trial route. A candidate trial and its prospective scheduled caller select the same candidate pin, so their comparison does not ignore arbitrary extra inputs.

For a compatible upgrade, obtain independent review and actual fresh/upgrade installed-payload checks for the new exact version, plus affected regressions and consumer command/worker contract checks. Prepare source candidate C with the new component pin and a distinct successor-record path while the approved default stays unchanged. Obtain paired source/compatibility acceptance at C, then use the same record-only child D assembly and separately authorized delivery as first adoption. The successor record binds the previous immutable adoption record and its passing trial, exact old/new components, compatibility classification, installed-test artifacts and fresh C review. The gate accepts this explicit compatible-re-adoption chain only when invariant behavior/policy bindings match, changed component-internal hashes have the independently approved compatibility mapping and installed/regression evidence, unchanged hosted-workflow/non-pin execution bindings match the predecessor, and the current component equals the new reviewed/tested value. It does not pretend the old trial executed the new bytes, or require those bytes to equal the old trial's component. A missing/stale compatibility review or installed check denies the upgrade. Keep prior records; update the selected record path rather than overwriting evidence.

Every shared change that fails the compatible-upgrade definition requires the affected-surface trials defined above, followed by C review, record-only D assembly and post-update observations. A shared change to hosted-worker rules/helpers that fails the compatible definition requires another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version. An interactive-only protected-client or entry change follows its actual installed-entry trial route; a hosted trial cannot substitute for it. This includes changes to accepted assessment, resolve-classification/supersession, scan-contract, execution, provider/reviewer, permission/secret, lease or publication/closure rules/behavior, rather than restoration of an unchanged accepted contract; uncertainty also takes this trial route. No known non-compatible change can fall between the two routes. Local detector rules, ordinary scan scope/options and feedback remain normal assessment inputs as listed above. To run that upgrade trial while production remains approved, separately authorize a default-branch setup that adds a distinct dormant candidate reusable-worker file and a dispatch-only caller. The current-default trial definition binds their exact blobs, proposed component/provider/permissions, allowed synthetic namespace and the intended prospective scheduled-caller contract. The active scheduled caller still references the old approved worker and record; it does not share the candidate file yet. The bounded synthetic trial may load only that explicitly named candidate, even though it differs from the active production binding, and cannot publish real-corpus findings. During this interval compare the trial caller to the prospective scheduled-caller contract, not the still-active old caller. After the trial, source candidate C switches the scheduled caller to that exact tested candidate worker; mechanically require its normalized caller fields and all protected candidate bindings to match the passing trial. D adds only the new immutable record. An unexpected candidate ref/blob, permission, invocation or caller difference requires re-review/retrial. For this feature's upgrade-procedure proof, hermetically simulate a permission- or execution-changing candidate trial with synthetic Git repositories/pins/callers and authenticated-as-test receipts; verify the old approved worker gate remains usable. This is not a live successor trial claim. The same conservative path applies when compatibility is uncertain. A failed/unavailable upgrade trial leaves the approved default pin/record active. Neither classification grants automatic merge, publication, paid execution or secret transfer. Every real adopted upgrade route refreshes the non-executing upgrade signal to the verified new source/release in C. After an authorized upgrade, recheck installed routing and gate behavior at the observed default. Rollback selects the prior approved pin, record, its corresponding source/release signal and matching migration-owned inputs together; preserve unrelated newer work and refuse mismatched combinations. A reverted signal may produce another intake proposal for the rolled-back release; that proposal cannot auto-merge or execute and needs fresh selection/verification. Do not rewrite historical pins or silently add updater-ignore policy during rollback.

An assessment-rule, scan-contract, scheduled-status, attention-projection/carry or report-history/provenance trial-route upgrade must bind a changed-rule coverage map in its committed trial definition. For each changed contract, name a bounded fixture scenario, exact fixture bytes and old/new contract references, with discriminatory expected dispositions or protocol outcomes under those two rules. Independently review that mapping; an unrelated unchanged first-adoption fixture cannot prove the new rule. The actual candidate trial must observe every mapped scenario and satisfy the new expected outcome through its real assessment/scan path. Missing coverage, a skipped case or a generic passing job without those observations denies upgrade verification. The old expectation can be grounded in the old accepted contract and hermetic old-component regression where applicable; this adds no implicit paid old-provider rerun. Hermetic upgrade-procedure tests use matching explicitly test-only observations, retaining their production rejection. Actual adopted successors require the corresponding observed candidate-trial evidence. Status/attention/history changes, protected report settings and history permission changes also require a candidate hosted read check: retrieve an actual earlier eligible trial artifact under the proposed Actions permission and report policy, then exercise the reviewed old/new projection/eligibility rules against unchanged-carried and changed/new-attention fixture cases. The helper check executes the candidate status/history code against bound assessment fixtures without a second paid provider call; retain that synthetic-assessment limitation. An advisory-only run or a binary download without those case observations cannot verify this surface. Candidate policies/workflows live only in the separately authorized default-branch trial setup while the old production settings/pin stay active; C selects exactly the tested prospective policy, and D records the observed check.

First adoption and the upgrade verification suite must exercise both supported surfaces. Before first cutover, and again when the accepted resolve-classification/supersession, PR/issue publication or closure contract changes, run a bounded actual model resolve trial using the exact installed candidate and synthetic proposal/diff/feedback/merge histories through a hermetic remote collector. It must observe consolidation publication, denial before merge, verified post-merge explanation/closure, abandonment/revert refusal, and exact scoped issue creation with missing/stale-review, wrong-model, absent-grant and staged-gate refusals; assert the exact attempted effects and that no real backlog IDs can enter the namespace. The actual model executes the installed resolve procedure; the tracker transport and merge event are fixtures, so report this as a model/collector trial rather than a real GitHub merge/closure. Require individually passing resolve, closure and issue collector cases in the first-adoption attestation before D; an advisory-only or generic collector-pass flag cannot substitute. Issue-affecting trial-route upgrades must pass that issue case again at the new exact payload. For a change affecting the hosted contract, this supplements its separately authorized unattended advisory trial; advisory success alone cannot verify a changed resolver. An interactive-only client change follows the affected-entry mapping above. Lease/advisory changes must likewise exercise the corresponding contention/publication path. A missing affected-surface trial leaves the upgrade unverified even when its other trial passes.

The AC-06 proofs for later upgrade/re-adoption/rollback machinery are hermetic helper/runtime-gate scenarios using synthetic version pins, workflow definitions, Git defaults, predecessor/successor records and clearly test-only trial/install receipts. They need no second published release, second paid hosted trial or candidate setup on Stackweaver default during first adoption. When a real later upgrade is selected, its actual new-version installed checks and applicable live trials are required by the operational procedure above; fixture receipts cannot satisfy them. The actual first-release marketplace fresh installs/upgrades and first-adoption advisory/resolve/closure/issue trials remain mandatory and are not replaced by these hermetic upgrade tests.

### Migration and rollback

Before retiring either local route, inventory every caller, command, scheduled worker, scanner option, feedback reader and generated-output dependency. Record old-to-new behavior and explicit intentional differences. Keep the scanner, suppressions, correction history, docs-truth workflow and local project policy in Stackweaver; preserve their bytes except the owner-selected bounded collection-accounting correction and the reviewed routing/configuration migration. Detector rules and existing feedback contents remain unchanged.

Publish the shared feature only under separate authority, then verify actual native Codex, Claude and Copilot marketplace fresh installs/upgrades, discovery and installed execution. Copilot uses the existing repository-root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` catalog; Codex uses `.agents/plugins/marketplace.json`. Verify that source choice during native installation rather than adding an unverified parallel catalog. For Copilot, register the published `michielvha/vh-adlc` marketplace with `copilot plugin marketplace add`, install `vh-adlc@vhco-tools` using `copilot plugin install` in an isolated `COPILOT_HOME`, and observe native plugin listing plus discovery/loading of the named `vh-adlc-docs-drift` skill. Record the catalog source/revision, front-door plugin version/payload, native discovery artifact and actual invocation. Upgrade a prior published install in a separate isolated profile and check the resulting payload/discovery again. Local copies or `--plugin-dir` alone do not satisfy this front-door marketplace proof; `--plugin-dir` remains the explicit retained approved-runtime mechanism. Document the commands in the installation/marketplace guides and keep the Copilot publishing entry unclaimed and cutover denied if that actual source/install/discovery fails. Local `copilot plugin`, `plugin install`, `plugin marketplace add` and `plugin list --help` confirm these native command forms; this is command-availability evidence, not a successful install. Use bounded synthetic source/docs/PR/feedback scenarios for actual model trials; do not resolve real drift PRs or publish real findings during acceptance. GUI/phone checks remain unclaimed without observed coverage or a separately approved visible deferral. The real unattended worker trial is required before scheduled cutover under the staged choice above; a generic deferral cannot silently replace that requirement.

Fresh paired review binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate. Complete corpus/legacy behavior checks and conflict-safe rollback/reapply must pass before consumer merge. Rollback restores migration-owned routing/configuration including the publication gate to staged mode before the legacy publisher resumes, and preserves scanner/feedback/customer documents, other workflows and newer unrelated changes. After merge, recheck installed routing and the local scheduled-worker contract at the observed revision. Do not label static YAML validation as a live scheduled provider run.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-01: An adopting repository can assess whole-corpus documentation drift through one discoverable harness-agnostic workflow while retaining its scanner, detector policy and existing change-scoped docs-impact behavior.
- [ ] AC-02: Assessments expose incomplete collection, unknown findings and stale inputs without claiming a clean or fully verified corpus, and ground proposed fixes in the selected source and document evidence. Collection is complete only when the scanner's reported document and per-unit inventories and digests match the expected sets/digests the shared helper independently derives from the bound Git tree and committed selectors, with successful required-unit outcomes and no failed, skipped or partial required collection. A scanner report or echoed digest without that match cannot establish completeness. This cross-checks reported input coverage; it does not prove detector interpretation or resist deliberate evidence forgery.
- [ ] AC-03: Authorized preparation and resolution preserve unrelated work, intended contracts, historical documents and local feature-review rules while producing only the verified selected corrections and decisions.
- [ ] AC-04: Rejected proposals produce the correct detector-versus-interpretation feedback, and duplicate or conflicting stale proposals cannot silently reintroduce rejected or unsupported changes. An original proposal closes only after its merged consolidation is freshly verified to carry every hunk or its terminal disposition; otherwise the original stays open.
- [ ] AC-05: Reports let an owner understand the result and decisions without reading the full audit, and independent review precedes publication, and all local and remote effects retain their declared scope and permission boundaries. Every real-corpus PR publishes the exact independently reviewed candidate commit/tree. Each real-corpus PR, issue, explanation and closure effect requires current independent review of its exact payload and bound source with the adopted reviewer identity; changed candidates or payloads cannot reuse the old review. Interrupted effects reconcile against owned intents and exact remote observations without duplicate PRs, issues, comments or closures; ambiguous or foreign state remains pending instead of being replayed. A complete scheduled run with only unchanged previously surfaced attention succeeds while retaining all unresolved items in its report; new attention and actual incomplete, review, gate, publication or recovery failures remain non-successful. A complete scheduled run whose confirmed corrections are all unchanged carried deliveries in verified open owned PRs succeeds without a duplicate PR or head rewrite and still lists those pending PRs. An explicitly approved delivery acknowledgement requires unique direct application evidence, original run ownership, exact current target bindings and settled mutation requests. It preserves the changed resource, durably stops original business replay and permits only exact-owned cleanup after all effects are accounted for, retaining a non-success result.
- [ ] AC-06: Stackweaver uses the published installed workflow with verified legacy command compatibility and staged scheduled-worker cutover only after an observed passing unattended shared trial, with the legacy scheduled worker retained until that success, retained local scanner/feedback/site rules and a conflict-safe rollback path. After adoption, its approved pin and record remain active until a successor is approved through independent review and installed checks for compatible changes, or the required affected-surface trials for other changes; an update proposal cannot switch runtime execution. Before any trial or shared interactive adoption, Stackweaver has the delivered scanner-completeness correction: its separate protocol reports skipped/failed reads and required-unit execution while legacy report fields, detector results and exits remain unchanged.
- [ ] AC-07: Required repository and portable-host checks pass at recorded revisions. Actual published-marketplace fresh installs/upgrades and named-skill discovery pass for Codex, Claude and Copilot; actual bounded Copilot model operations/reviews and Claude daily-command forwarding through bound roles pass through their declared synthetic transports. Paired Stackweaver migration and rollback checks pass at the exact shared/consumer payloads before cutover. Unperformed model, GUI, phone or other surfaces remain explicitly unclaimed; installer/helper results alone cannot establish operation support.

## Test Plan

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| AC-01 | Install/discover the content operation and run it in a second synthetic adopter with different roots, statuses, scanner and generated files. Assert the documented request examples choose audit/resolve/docs-impact/plain-doc/persistent-audit/truth routes correctly; ambiguous wording asks once. Test missing settings, unpinned setup, missing knowledge and unchanged existing operations. Load every shipped docs-drift example through the strict settings validator and exercise its scan contract with the matching fixture, including `detector_inputs`, publication/trial policy and required reviewer identity; example/interface drift fails the check. |
| AC-02 | Exercise complete empty scans, findings, missing/malformed/stale reports, skipped or unreadable inputs, unknown detectors, suppressed findings and stale suppressions. Assert each incomplete source remains visible and prevents clean/ready claims. Independently enumerate the exact configured Git-tree corpus and compare every read/digest/failure entry; test omissions, duplicates, escapes, symlinks, all-docs-read/missing-detector and throwing-detector cases against the configured required unit set. Derive each unit's expected document set from committed selectors; deny a complete unit that examines only a strict subset, and test legitimate all-inventory, subset and zero-input selectors. Check the exact v1 preimage/canonical ordering in helper-generated binding JSON, scanner echo/actual accounting and helper recomputation, plus independent raw-blob digest agreement, including mixed-case/non-ASCII option keys, literal backslashes, invalid encodings and checkout filters. Test inherited GIT_DIR/GIT_INDEX_FILE/object/config overrides, replace refs on document blobs and missing promisor objects: return unaltered bound bytes or incomplete, with no lazy fetch, wrong-index read or caller-state mutation. Cover the scanner child environment and owned fetch path as well as direct helper reads. Test optional source/reference roots in scope hashing, whole-repository fallback and negative searches; scanner feedback/tree/index/ref/FETCH_HEAD mutation must make the assessment incomplete. Bind scans, evidence and rechecks to exact source/options; include source changes during assessment and malicious report/doc instructions. Run the scanner with canary provider/GitHub/mapped secrets in the parent and an ambient Git credential helper/extraheader: the child sees no tokens, helper or agent socket, and a credentialed push attempt cannot produce an authenticated effect. Verify no persisted checkout credentials and no secret-bearing job environment reach collection; inaccessible required inputs yield incomplete assessment. These tests prove ambient separation, not malicious same-user filesystem confinement. |
| AC-03 | Verify report-only preservation of files/index/refs; owned prepare/resolve edits; unrelated staged/unstaged work; historical/other-system/intended-contract and deliberately incomplete matrix cases; code-bug routing; uncertain/runtime acceptance boxes and local-format plan gates. Require explicit selection for destructive/structural and plan/status decisions. Exercise committed `resolveRoots`, shared strict metadata reading and the existing matcher's feature dialect against Go MatchCovers shared fixtures, preserving docs-impact default behavior and testing malformed/local-format cases; actual freshness uses the binary. Standard Review Log text, Evidence/Residual blocks, Task proof/completion history and dated Context/Sources provenance must remain byte-identical, including a stale link inside a stored review finding and inside an Evidence line; propose corrections through the feature owner instead. Update the docs-impact isolated-plugin copy list in `tests/vh-adlc/docs-impact/cli.test.ts` for `scripts/git-read.ts` and verify unchanged candidates/gaps for metadata it previously accepted. Standard normative sections/covers and every part of claimed feature documents must remain byte-identical and route to the spec/claiming-writer handoff. A guide covered by an active claimed feature is proposal-only without an actual handoff/coordinated owner selection; report affected features and freshness changes for ordinary covered documents including historical completed coverage. Missing independent verification remains provisional. Verify recorded assessment-role PR object stores and trial scratch repositories remain outside caller Git directories and can never change caller objects/refs/FETCH_HEAD/index or clean a foreign resource. With local feature-document format, put a stale path inside a recorded review/evidence range that the retained procedure does not classify. Preparation/resolution must report the correction and preserve the plan byte-for-byte; only explicitly editable local ranges may change. A custom-root AGENTS.md that is unreadable/malformed, or a missing HEAD, must leave every corpus document untouched and assessment incomplete; no default/partly parsed classification is used. A malformed declared docs-impact root also blocks deliberately, with its original setup diagnostic visible; clean absent roots remain supported. |
| AC-04 | Resolve synthetic overlapping PRs with identical, contradictory, already-fixed and stale hunks. Verify every disposition, exact selected subset, changed-approval invalidation and fresh-base reconstruction. Test full exact-head Git diffs for large proposals, absent REST patches, unreadable/truncated inputs, changed heads and binary file dispositions; incomplete proposals cannot be applied or closed and cannot disappear from the selected scope. Test positive superseded closure with every hunk accounted for and a linked merged consolidation, an uncarried deferred/undecided hunk staying open, explicit merged carry-forward, missing feedback, and closure attempts before publication, after publication but before merge, and after the consolidation closes unmerged; changed proposal/consolidation bindings stop the sequence. Exercise consumer-allowed squash and rebase merges using GET head equality and exact postimage blobs rather than reviewed-head ancestry; a later unrelated in-scope edit requires fresh closure-only assessment, and a later revert or removed carry-forward keeps the affected original open. Start post-merge closure in a fresh session/second host holding only the merged PR ID and current authority; load the reviewed-tree record, freshly review checkpoint/current default/body/effects, and complete only the eligible approved closures. Missing/ambiguous records or changed bindings deny; private historical artifacts are unnecessary and a PR-body approval claim grants nothing. A consolidation updated by merging advanced default is explicitly unsupported and denies closure; test that case and the supported fresh-clean-candidate/review route when branch protection requires an up-to-date head. A disjoint default advance without a head update remains supported under its normal read-set check. Add a commit to the consolidation after its publication review and merge it: unlisted paths or changed postimages deny fresh-session closure, rather than comparing the observed head to itself. Test an unattended follow-up without an adopted post-merge grant and require no write. Test narrow detector suppression versus interpretation correction, no feedback writes in report mode, repeated mistakes and incomplete-scan refusal to retire suppressions. Test closure-only retry after later default input or consolidation-body changes: it may pass only with complete comparison, unchanged original bindings, verified immutable merged postimages/dispositions and carried decisions plus fresh exact explanation approval. Changed original head/body or an unlisted/mismatched merged diff leaves originals open with recovery, and no closure-only record rewrite can make it pass. A differing historical head with the same exact permitted diff is checked afresh without claiming recovery of the old review; a change after fresh review invalidates the batch. Exercise an all-already-fixed selection and wrong-with-feedback-already-present selection: an authorized resolve produces only the pre-review record plus exact reviewed PR dispositions; no original closes before that record-only consolidation merges and passes fresh closure checks. Without publication authority, keep the proposal local and originals open. For squash, rebase and merge-commit histories, insert an in-scope default commit after publication but before merge that invalidates an already-fixed disposition or an applied hunk's source evidence without changing the consolidation postimages. Full recorded-base-to-current-default comparison must detect it and require fresh disposition verification, keeping the affected original open when no longer satisfied. Test an unrelated disjoint advance as a passing control, post-merge changes separately and unavailable comparison/history as refusal; only exact declared postimages and unchanged record count as the expected transition. |
| AC-05 | Test the single publisher through native CLI wiring and injected synthetic collector, denying production collector selection; observe-before-replay lost PR/issue responses, comment-before-close interruption, unavailable state, lost artifacts and unauthorized replay without duplicates. Inspect actual model reports for concise summary, complete hidden/detail evidence, recommendations, unknowns and precise next decisions. Run fake remote/command collectors and exact owned-role checks to prove no unauthorized creation/removal of worktrees, commit/push/PR/issue/closure/branch action; exercise the explicitly authorized exact-owned consolidation refresh with force-with-lease under the publication lease, refusing absent rewrite authority, changed remote head and foreign ownership; test an issue-only publication with a short reviewed work statement and bound committed/published evidence-document link, refusing a local-only/missing/stale report or evidence stuffed into the issue body; refusing pre-cutover requests, no review, wrong model, stale source/payload or absent issue/lease authority; exercise authorized scheduled preparation and publication failure without broad staging or clean-success fallback. An incomplete scheduled scan and should-fix review must fail with no partial PR/issue, a bounded run summary/report artifact and retained platform failure; test artifact-upload failure and the complete no-change success separately. Require exact-candidate independent review for advisory and resolve publication, unchanged raw records, exact reviewed/pushed commit and tree plus an unchanged independently reviewed full PR payload/body; returned native review evidence remains only in external/run artifacts, with at most a pre-review stable locator in the body, denied publication on missing/mismatched/stale/failed review or a post-review evidence commit, and invalidation after source/feedback/selection changes. Test requested aliases resolving to the adopted full role ID versus a substituted same-family ID, absent/unknown scanning-worker identities and changed reviewer IDs; test native provider fields plus matching worker/reviewer headers, conflicting/alias-only/missing native values, delegated/hosted roles and a self-performing host without native provenance; only exact adopted native role IDs may publish. Test unattended matching, different and `unknown` reviewer identities against committed expected-model policy, and report/local-prepare default behavior against the existing protocol. Test matching/different/unknown identities for interactive resolve publication and both interactive/unattended post-merge closure; all real-corpus effects require the adopted reviewer binding, while report-only unknown identity is honestly unattested. Test unrelated and in-scope default-branch advances using the declared read-set/history rule. After cutover race shared scheduled/local publishers through the same lease; deny a foreign or interrupted lease, and allow only atomic exact-owned release, denying a replaced lease. Verify operator-time original adoption artifacts, then pass a hosted runtime gate with no private artifact root using only its committed attestation/current blobs; corrupt recorded identities/verdicts/bindings deny, and runtime-only mode cannot satisfy operator verification. Read committed/current-default settings from exact Git blob bytes through the strict byte reader, including when that default is not checked out. Read the committed current-default publication gate before each selected remote effect; missing/staged state and stale adopted local configuration after rollback must deny shared publication. Before cutover deny shared real-corpus publication/closure even if a legacy run starts concurrently; prove the separately authorized synthetic trial cannot overlap legacy proposal IDs or file/branch scope. Exercise the installed synthetic trial entry through actual front-door/launcher dispatch with fixture tracker reads, owned local bare PR/base/merge objects and collector effects. Verify exact context/payload/client provenance and label these as synthetic procedure/API coverage. Production native transport rejects collector flags/contexts and ignores report/settings/environment attempts to select it; the trial driver rejects real repository IDs, network endpoints, foreign paths and out-of-namespace effects. Cover native production command construction separately and retain the hosted live advisory trial requirement. Test repeated complete scheduled runs with unchanged attention carried by an open owned PR and by a verified prior report: green with unresolved items retained, no duplicate advisory and no claim that the corpus is fixed. An unrelated commit/run timestamp alone must not re-alert. New entries, changed relevant evidence/rules/owner actions, missing or expired prior evidence and a just-created current report/PR must not suppress new-attention status. Incomplete scans, should-fix reviews, blocked effects, pending recovery and upload failure remain red even with matching old attention. Record successful reviewed effects separately if the same run still ends new-attention. Test an artifact planted by another workflow, a fork/PR run, unapproved source, wrong attempt/context, expired artifact, changed archive bytes and malformed/oversized ZIP; none may suppress attention. A complete prior new-attention report from a verified producer can carry on the next run despite its red conclusion, while prior incomplete/technical-failure reports cannot. An edited PR body or missing reviewed intent receipt must not match. Local roots require matching attestation envelopes and reject symlinks/cached-Actions relabelling; no history path grants writes. Test a new verified correction successfully published with no unresolved attention, and with only carried attention: both are green/published, with PR review awaiting the owner shown as delivery state. A simultaneous new unresolved decision instead makes the run red/new-attention while preserving the completed effect record. Run the packaged ZIP reader without node_modules/network on Linux/macOS, enforce declared archive limits, and reject a missing or stale vendor bundle. Exercise an open owned advisory with verified corrections and carried unknowns, then a complete later run confirming the same corrections: green/carried-delivery, linked old PR, no new branch/PR or head rewrite. New disjoint corrections publish only their subset; same-path overlap or changed/unverifiable PR contents yields a needs-resolution report. A hosted run must retrieve the retained immutable receipt for an interactively published consolidation after its ephemeral lease and local cache are gone, verifying its reviewed body/head and attention/correction entries. Ref creation needs exact grant; reject a substituted ref/object and reconcile lost create responses without duplicates or deletions. Test receipt create/retain with absent namespace grant, and retirement with absent cleanup grant, a foreign/unrecorded ref or a changed object: no such mutation occurs. Retire only exact recorded owned refs under the separately selected cleanup scope after linked needs finish. With an unchanged open advisory and valid old receipt, independently fix its hunk differently on default or make its old proposed correction wrong/unsupported, leaving no fresh same-path correction. The run must list the stale proposal as needs-resolution/new-attention, never clean no-change or replay; a matching subsequent complete report may carry that unresolved decision. An unverified current disposition stays explicit rather than being assumed supported.  Refuse acknowledgement authorized only by a standing adopted procedure, scheduled/unattended controller or generic cleanup/absence-retirement grant through the reconciliation authorize path. Record no acknowledgement or new replay-stop disposition and release no lease. Reject a collector success projection in a native or production plan, mismatched transport/context labels and relabelled native/collector evidence. The installed collector acknowledgement succeeds only with evidence captured by its own executor against owned fixture objects and remains a trial-only non-success disposition. Exercise a positive acknowledgement using only success evidence captured by the actual executor/journal path, with no caller-supplied success assertion. Refuse missing, oversized, tampered or unlinked artifacts and a pre-capture legacy attempt without complete independent native proof. Interrupt after successful dispatch but before artifact persistence or journal linking, leaving the unproved attempt pending. Check private artifact permissions, schema limits and exclusion of raw bodies, tokens and headers, including canary secrets. Crash between command completion and projection persistence and inspect the artifact root for raw body/header canaries. Require none. Verify both new projected HTTP rejection evidence and unchanged legacy file-backed rejection fixtures, with tampered, incomplete and wrong-attempt refusals. Exercise new projected Git rejection evidence, tampered/incomplete/wrong-attempt and mixed-status refusals, plus unchanged legacy file-backed Git proof compatibility. Preserve process-group timeout/cancellation/output-bound regressions. Exercise separate positive acknowledgement cases for `pr-create`, `issue-create` and `comment-create` after their delivered payload changes. Add a `pr-update` case with direct native mutation evidence of the original source-to-approved-field transition followed by a later edit, and a `pr-close` case with direct evidence of the selected closure followed by reopening. Include a legacy PR authored by a different actor from the selected mutation. All preserve original target/head bindings. Refuse acknowledgement independently for each of `push`, `branch-refresh`, `lease-acquire`, `receipt-retain` and `lease-release`, leaving unresolved effects and their lease pending. Refuse a PR update whose text PATCH applied but whose labels request was never dispatched or definitively rejected, including a later text edit. Refuse issue creation with incomplete or absent requested labels. Refuse PR creation with incomplete label application and an update with an unapplied requested draft-state change. Each partial case leaves the lease pending even when all requests have settled, records no acknowledgement and grants no abandonment of the unapplied portion. Refuse an update established only by a current GET or pre-existing marker and a closure established only by its explanation or an ambiguously attributed timeline event. Verify the effect-specific marker/baseline and direct application proof, exact repository/actor/target/head bindings, current field digests, original journal/run and lease nonce, and per-request native settlement. Preserve the edited resource and retained intent ref, record the separate non-success event and release only that owned lease. Refuse missing/pre-existing/duplicate markers, wrong actor/head/target, unproved closure, unavailable or tampered evidence, any live or unknown server request, inferred ownership, wrong scope, stale approval and approval-time changes. Interrupt before/after event publication and around release intent/dispatch/response, including concurrent approvals and a replacement holder. Resume without duplicate acknowledgement or business replay and keep non-success after payload restoration. An installed resolve collector case must prove the expected non-success acknowledgement plus exact cleanup and unchanged remote fixture; a generic failure cannot count as its passing result. |

## Tasks

Code tasks add and run the regression before the behavior change. Each task is one coherent commit with its observed checks recorded below it. Publication and live-operation tasks retain the explicit authority stated in their done conditions.

- [x] T1 `tests/vh-adlc/git-read.test.ts, plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/git-read.ts, plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-impact/collection.ts` - Add the failing cross-repository, injected-environment, raw-byte and caller-preservation regressions first. Extract and reuse the hardened Git primitive. Focused Git and existing docs-impact tests pass. (AC-02, AC-03)
      Evidence: `bun test tests/vh-adlc/git-read.test.ts` first failed because the neutral module was absent. After extraction, `bun test tests/vh-adlc/git-read.test.ts tests/vh-adlc/docs-impact` passed 132 tests and the docs-impact strict typecheck passed. The full Go test/vet/lint/module gate passed. The broad Bun run exposed the required package-version bump and two five-second audit CLI timeouts. Both manifests now select 0.11.0, and `bun test tests/vh-adlc/check.test.ts tests/vh-adlc/audit/cli.test.ts --timeout 30000` passed 66 tests. Registration checks passed 34 tests. The initial broad Bun run remains diagnostic, with its failures retained for final-gate reconciliation. Manual documentation-impact review found the extraction internal to collection, with the isolated-copy dependency list updated. Logs are retained under `.docs-drift-review-archive/t1-*` outside the published payload.

Its retry-wait finding was reproduced by an actual local HTTP 503 with a 120-second Retry-After header, which outlived the fixture's eight-second owned-process deadline. Each download now also limits its retry window to two seconds. Both archive routes use the same 25-second transfer limit, and the small metadata request uses ten seconds. The phase-specific fixture rejects missing, nonnumeric or changed budgets and retry counts, and checks their conservative combined 66-second network budget against the 120-second cold-test deadline. The native curl 8.15.0 on this host returned zero for the capped 503 without producing an archive. A separate first-failing case now takes the verified fallback when the primary leaves no nonempty file. A nonempty wrong digest still fails the checksum check. ### R-1

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: blocking
WHERE: Design, Audit and preparation / Resolution and feedback / Authority and delivery; frontmatter covers
EVIDENCE: The design needs "one exact owned assessment tree" (00081 line 9) and "a fresh owned branch" (line 21). It also lists superseded-PR closure and branch cleanup as effects (line 29). Shared authority currently says "Outside the maintenance and audit roles below, never create, remove, prune, reset or force-move a worktree" (references/authority.md:78). It defines scoped worktree/ref roles only for maintenance (lines 92-117) and audit (lines 119-139). Its action table (lines 10-32) has no docs-drift row and no superseded-PR closure action. references/authority.md is not in covers (00001 lines 6-25), and the spec never says whether the owned tree is a new worktree, a ref or the current checkout.
PROPOSAL: Add references/authority.md and its guard test to covers. Define the docs-drift assessment and resolve location roles: creation, recorded owner/base/ref, non-forced cleanup and preservation. Add action-table rows for each docs-drift local and remote effect, including superseded-PR closure, together with the authority each one needs.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: blocking
WHERE: Design, Entry and consumer contract; Migration and rollback; AC-02, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The scan result must distinguish "complete collection from findings and failures" (00081 line 3), and skipped inputs must leave the assessment incomplete (line 5). Context says the Stackweaver scanner skips a document on a read failure and has no completeness field (00001 lines 51, 57-58). The contract does not require the scanner to list the documents it examined. It also requires no independent comparison against the configured scope at the bound commit, so completeness still rests on the scanner reporting about itself. The spec keeps the scanner local, with its bytes preserved unless "a separately reviewed migration correction is necessary" (00081 line 35). It never says whether that correction, or an adapter, is part of this feature. Yet AC-06 needs scanner completeness verified (Test Plan AC-06).
PROPOSAL: Define a versioned scan-result schema with an examined-document inventory. ### R-2

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (publication lease); Scheduled worker and staged adoption; Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The lease serializes "the scheduled worker and a local resolve publisher for the same consumer" (00081 line 80). Under the owner's staged choice, "Interactive adoption may precede that trial" (00081 line 14). The local scheduled worker stays active until the trial passes, and the spec says to "Preserve the existing worker bytes as a rollback target" (00161 lines 6, 8). It also says to "Do not change schedules" (00081 line 6). So the scheduled publisher running during the whole staged interval is the unchanged legacy worker, which never acquires the new lease. During that interval a shared local resolve run can push and close superseded proposals while the legacy worker publishes a new advisory PR, with nothing serializing them. delivery.md 00161 lines 8-9 requires serializing publication "where competing runs could conflict". The AC-05 lease race test only covers the shared scheduled and local publishers.
PROPOSAL: Define how the staged interval serializes publication. Options are: give the legacy worker a reviewed minimal lease acquisition as part of the routing migration; have shared resolve publication and closure detect an active or recent legacy run and refuse; or defer shared resolve publication until cutover. Add a race test for the legacy worker against a shared resolve publisher to AC-06.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption; Migration and rollback; AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: The real unattended trial must have "completed assessment/preparation, obtained the required independent candidate review and exercised its bounded authorized advisory handoff" in Stackweaver (00161 line 6). Migration and rollback says "Use bounded synthetic source/docs/PR/feedback scenarios for actual model trials; do not resolve real drift PRs or publish real findings during acceptance" (00161 line 14). AC-06 requires the observed trial before the feature is accepted as complete. The spec never says whether the trial runs on the real corpus and publishes a real advisory PR (which contradicts line 14) or runs on a synthetic fixture or destination (which may not be a "real" trial of the consumer route). It also never says how the trial worker definition reaches a runnable unattended state, such as a dispatch-only workflow on the default branch, before the separate cutover change. Nor does it say how the adoption helper records interactive-adopted but scheduled-pending state.
PROPOSAL: Name the trial's input corpus, publication destination and what counts as the advisory handoff, and state explicitly whether that is an exception to the no-real-findings rule. Specify the consumer change order (interactive adoption, then trial worker without schedule, then cutover) and make the adoption evidence record scheduled adoption as pending until the trial receipt exists.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scan-result protocol and local correction; Entry and consumer contract; Test Plan AC-02
EVIDENCE: The design states "Missing required detector execution is a gap even if all document reads succeeded" (00081 line 38), and AC-02 tests "missing requested detectors" (00161 line 33). The v1 schema lists only declared "detector options", the document inventory, collection failures, findings, suppressed findings and stale suppressions (00081 line 36). It has no per-detector execution or outcome record. The consumer settings list scanner argv, scope, feedback files, procedure and commands, but no required detector set (00081 line 28). So the shared helper has no defined source for which detectors are required, and no observation proving they ran. A scanner that aborts or skips a detector after reading every document would still produce a complete inventory with fewer findings, which is the "lower finding count" case the spec forbids treating as proof (00081 line 32).
PROPOSAL: Add a required detector set to the committed settings, or derive it from the bound scanner options, and add a schema field recording each detector's execution outcome. Have the helper mark the result incomplete when a required detector is absent or failed, and require the Stackweaver correction to emit that field.

### R-4

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Scope; Design, Authority and delivery (staged interval and publication lease); Scheduled worker and staged adoption; Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: Context says `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` selects the local resolver (00001 line 60). Scope says to preserve Stackweaver's current command "through thin forwarding instructions" (00081 line 6). Before cutover, shared interactive adoption is "report/local-prepare only" and the spec says "Keep the legacy publishing routes explicitly available under their existing consumer procedure" (00161 line 8). Stage one adopts interactive preparation "while retaining all legacy real-corpus publishing routes" (00161 line 16). The spec never says whether the existing command forwards to the shared operation at stage one or only at cutover. If it forwards at stage one, it does not name the route an owner uses for the legacy resolver's push and superseded-PR closure. Cutover is described only as "change the scheduled route and enable shared real-corpus publication" (00161 line 16). It does not require the legacy interactive resolver's publication and closure route to be retired or disabled in the same change. After cutover, the lease covers only "the shared scheduled worker and a local resolve publisher" (00161 line 10), and the legacy resolver does not take it. The AC-05 race tests cover shared against shared after cutover and shared against a legacy run before cutover, but not a legacy interactive resolver against the shared scheduled worker after cutover. That leaves the unserialized publication race from R-2 F-01 in the post-cutover state.
PROPOSAL: For each stage, state which entry point the existing command selects and which legacy resolve and closure route stays reachable. FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (trial); Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The publication design allows "A final record-only commit ... when its diff contains evidence alone", made after the review, and says evidence goes into "the published artifact/PR through the authorized wrapper" (00081 line 80). The trial requires "The publisher pushes that same reviewed checkpoint without rewriting, transplanting, generating or remapping anything afterward; assert both published head and tree equal the reviewed consumer objects" (00161 line 20). It also requires "The candidate-review and publication steps themselves are unchanged" (00161 line 22). If the production publisher adds a record-only commit, either the trial's head-equality assertion fails or the trial publisher skips that step. Skipping it is a publisher difference that invalidates the trial under its own rule. The spec also never defines "evidence alone": which paths qualify, how the publisher checks that the post-review diff touches only those paths, and how the AC-05 "mismatched review" denial treats a head that differs only by that commit.
PROPOSAL: Choose one rule for both production and the trial. Either evidence goes only in the PR body or in an external artifact, and the published head always equals the reviewed commit. KIND: factual

EVIDENCE: The review is invalidated by "Changed source/diff, settings, scanner/options/scope, feedback, selected PR heads/bodies or owner decision payloads" (00161 line 2). Resolve must "Revalidate source, proposals and approved decisions after material changes" (00081 line 62). None of these says what happens when the consumer default branch has moved since the reviewed checkpoint. It is unclear whether any new default commit is "changed source" that forces a new scan and review, whether only changes to in-scope documents, settings, scanner or feedback count, or whether a moved base is allowed for an advisory PR. "Material" is undefined. On an active repository, the unattended worker would then either never publish or publish against an unchecked base, and the AC-05 invalidation tests have no defined pass condition for a moved base.
PROPOSAL: Define the base-freshness rule. For example, the publisher may proceed only when the current default commit has no changes since the reviewed base to the bound document scope, settings, scanner, feedback or generator inputs. EVIDENCE: Resolve "reads complete proposal bodies and diffs" (00081 line 60). PR discovery reuses `github-read.ts` and "preserves its completeness/error observations" (00081 line 20). EVIDENCE: The detector input digest "uses the existing stable-key `digest` primitive over source commit, settings/scope digests, feedback digests, detector ID/options and the sorted applicable `[path, content_sha256]` entries" (00081 line 42). FINDING: F-01

WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication (base freshness, publication gate); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (real unattended trial); Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The trial assesses "a committed bounded synthetic documentation/code/feedback fixture in an isolated scratch Git repository" (00161 line 28). It then maps output "into an owned consumer branch based on the selected default commit" (line 30). The trial says "The candidate-review and publication steps themselves are unchanged" and treats any different "publisher behavior" as invalidating (line 32). The production freshness rule must "Bind the assessment to its exact base commit and input read set" and "read the current consumer default and compare its full delta from the assessed base". Under that rule, "A non-descendant base, unavailable comparison or any bound input change requires a fresh scan" (line 10). For the trial, the assessed base is a scratch-repository commit, so the consumer default cannot descend from it. Its read set also spans two repositories: fixture documents in scratch, and the scanner, worker and procedure in the consumer. An unchanged publisher would therefore always refuse the trial. Separately, staged configuration "denies shared real-corpus push, PR and closure effects" (line 12), but the spec never names the bound input the unchanged publisher uses to classify a run as the trial namespace rather than real corpus. The R-1 F-06 staged-cutover decision depends on this trial being passable without changing the publisher.
PROPOSAL: Define the trial's assessed base and read set for the publisher. For example, the freshness comparison uses the consumer base commit and the manifest's recorded scratch commit plus fixture digests. Name the committed trial-scope binding that lets the gate allow the trial namespace while it denies real-corpus effects. FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual

EVIDENCE: The cutover commit sets `publication.mode: adopted`, and the publication block names "the committed adoption-record path/digest" (00161 lines 12, 16). The adoption helper validates that record's "passing trial, exact component and worker/reviewer/publisher bindings, paired acceptance and recorded cutover route" (line 14). Paired review "binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate" (line 42). If the record is committed in the cutover change, it cannot contain the paired acceptance of the commit that contains it, because that commit's ID depends on the record bytes. Adoption evidence `adopted` also "requires a passing exact trial receipt, paired review and observed post-cutover worker route" (line 34). A post-cutover observation cannot exist in the record committed by the cutover itself. The first post-cutover scheduled run is also the publisher that needs the gate to pass. The spec does not say whether the gate requires evidence state `adopted` (which is circular) or accepts `trial-passed` plus a recorded route (which means publication runs before the state `adopted` describes).
PROPOSAL: Say exactly which consumer candidate the paired acceptance binds and how the committed record refers to it without self-reference. For example, bind the reviewed candidate tree minus the record path, or the parent commit with a mechanically checked record-only diff. State which evidence state the gate requires for the first post-cutover publication, and when and through which authorized change the observed post-cutover route is recorded.

### R-6

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scan-result protocol and local correction (per-detector execution accounting); Entry and consumer contract (consumer settings); Test Plan AC-02
EVIDENCE: Each required detector unit records a "bound input digest", and the shared helper treats "differently bound required units" as incomplete (00081 line 40). That digest includes "`documents` is the sorted applicable `[path, content_sha256]` array" (00081 line 42). To recompute it, the helper must know which documents apply to each unit. Consumer settings name only "required detector-unit IDs and options" (00081 line 28). The scope object has `roots`, `exclusions`, `suffixes` and `required_detectors` (line 42), and none of these defines per-unit applicability. Two outcomes are possible. The helper might accept whatever applicable subset the scanner declares. In that case, a detector that skips or swallows an error on one document still reports `complete` with a self-consistent digest, even though every document read succeeded. That is the same silent-omission failure the owner's completeness decision was meant to expose. Otherwise the helper cannot recompute the digest at all. The AC-02 cases cover a unit that is absent or throws, but not a unit that runs over fewer documents than it should.
PROPOSAL: Define where each required unit's applicable document set comes from. One option is a per-unit selector (roots/suffixes or "all inventory") in committed settings, which the helper evaluates against the independent inventory. The alternative is to state that per-unit applicability is scanner-attested and record that as a visible limitation. Add an AC-02 case where a unit completes over a strict subset of its applicable documents.

SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback; Authority and delivery; Test Plan AC-04, AC-05
EVIDENCE: Resolve classifies each hunk as "apply, apply-modified, already fixed, wrong or defer" and must "Preserve uncertain proposals in the decision report" (00081 line 64). Superseded-PR closure appears only as an authorized effect (00081 line 80; 00161 lines 2, 20, 22). The only eligibility rule is negative: incomplete proposals "cannot be closed" (00081 line 68). The spec never says when a complete proposal counts as superseded. It does not address a proposal that still has `defer` hunks or undecided owner decisions. It does not say whether closure must wait until the consolidated PR is actually published, or whether the closure must link to that PR and to each hunk's disposition. As written, a resolve run could close a stale audit PR whose deferred hunks exist only in a locally saved report. Those deferred hunks would then drop out of the visible backlog, which works against preserving uncertain proposals. The AC-04 and AC-05 proofs test that incomplete proposals cannot be closed, but they have no positive closure-eligibility case.
PROPOSAL: Define superseded as follows. Every hunk in the proposal has a terminal disposition (applied, already fixed or wrong, with feedback recorded) or an explicitly carried deferral in the published consolidated PR's decision list. Closure happens only after that PR exists and posts a link to it. A proposal with uncarried deferred or undecided hunks stays open. Add AC-04 cases for a fully superseded proposal, a proposal with a deferred hunk, and a closure attempted before the consolidated PR publishes.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (publication lease); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (trial definition)
EVIDENCE: The cooperative publication lease is defined only "After scheduled cutover" (00161 line 22), and its ref namespace is named by the adoption procedure and the `publication` block (lines 14, 22). The pre-cutover trial nonetheless relies on "The trial publication lease and namespace are disjoint from legacy real-corpus proposals" (line 30). The committed trial definition binds "exact output-path and branch namespace, worker entry and allowed draft-only effects" (line 34), but it names no trial lease ref, and no acquire/release authority covers it. As a result, the "same publisher" trial either takes a lease the staged policy does not define or skips the lease step that production runs.
PROPOSAL: Add the trial lease ref namespace and its acquire/release effect to the committed trial definition. State that the unchanged publisher's lease step uses that ref in trial mode. Add an AC-06 assertion that the trial lease cannot overlap the real-corpus lease ref.

### R-7

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (worker pin, first post-cutover gate); Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The scheduled worker pins the published vh-adlc source at an exact commit. That pin stays visible to the dependency updater, but "Updater proposals do not authorize automatic route changes or waive adoption/review checks" (00161 line 28). The post-cutover gate requires "matching current protected route/component/policy bindings", and "current protected publication/worker inputs must continue to match the adopted record" (00161 line 44). The adopted record is immutable and bound to the trial's exact component (00161 lines 18, 42). Cutover also retires every legacy publication route (00161 line 20). After cutover, the first updater bump of the shared source SHA or plugin version therefore makes the gate deny all scheduled and interactive real-corpus publication, with no legacy fallback. The spec never defines how a new component is re-adopted. It does not say whether that needs another real unattended trial, another C/D assembly and paired review, or a lighter procedure. AC-06 tests a mismatched component only as a refusal.
PROPOSAL: Define the post-cutover upgrade route for a changed component or worker binding. Present the owner trade-off: either require a new paid unattended trial plus C/D record for every upgrade, or allow a reviewed re-adoption record without a new trial for specified compatible changes. Add an AC-06 case where an updater bump is denied until that route completes.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication; Scheduled worker and staged adoption; Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: Publication is blocked by "A missing reviewer, model mismatch or blocking/should-fix finding" (00161 line 12). Review uses "the existing model-identity/isolation/returned-record protocol" (00161 line 8). Under that protocol, a model mismatch exists only against an owner-requested model. With no requested model, the host default is inherited and `MODEL: unknown` is acceptable (review-protocol.md lines 29-40, 46-60). Under unattended scheduled runs there is no owner in session to name a model. Neither the consumer settings (00081 line 28), the `publication` block (00161 line 16) nor the trial definition (00161 line 36) names the expected candidate-reviewer model. The trial only records "provider/model identities" (00161 line 40). The AC-05 "mismatched review" denial therefore has no reference value. An unattended run could either publish with an unattested reviewer identity or never be able to show a match.
PROPOSAL: Add a committed expected candidate-reviewer model to the adopted policy, or explicitly declare that the host default with unknown identity is allowed. Bind that choice in the trial definition and adoption record, and add AC-05 and AC-06 cases for a matching, a different and an `unknown` reviewer identity in unattended mode.

EVIDENCE: The trial runs from a separate file such as `.github/workflows/docs-drift-shared-trial.yml` with no cron trigger, and "does not replace the existing job" (00161 line 30). Expected differences are limited to trigger and namespace, while any different "permissions, invocation, reviewer or publisher behavior invalidates the trial" (00161 line 40). The adoption helper validates "exact component/worker/reviewer/publisher bindings" (00161 line 18). Only "exact workflow/job blobs" are recorded, so the two files' blobs necessarily differ. The spec defines no mechanical comparison that proves the cutover scheduled job matches the trial job apart from its trigger. That includes job permissions, environment, secrets exposure, runner and step invocation. AC-06 "changed-worker invalidation" has no pass condition for this comparison.

### R-8

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Upgrades after adoption; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (reusable worker, trial block, caller comparison); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: Some upgrades need a new trial: "A changed worker execution path, scanner/protocol/decision behavior, provider/reviewer identity, permissions, secret exposure, lease or publication/closure behavior requires another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version" (00161 line 56). The spec also says "Keep the approved source/plugin pin and adoption-record selection active while an updater proposes its replacement" and "Do not merge a pin bump first and leave the default branch pointing at an unapproved component" (00161 line 50). After cutover, the trial and the scheduled trigger are "thin callers of one committed reusable worker workflow". That worker "owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps", and "both callers use that same definition". The adoption helper allows the two callers to differ only in `on` and declared synthetic `with` values (00161 line 40). The gate classifies a trial only through the `trial` block and trial definition in "current-default `.vh-adlc/docs-drift.yaml`" (00161 line 36). An upgrade trial needs the new component, or new permissions or execution, inside the shared worker or caller. Putting it there on the default branch also moves the scheduled worker to the unapproved component and breaks the caller-equality rule. Dispatching from a candidate ref instead leaves the current-default trial definition and worker blob mismatched, so the gate refuses the run. The spec therefore defines when an upgrade trial is required but gives no way to run one without breaking either the approved-pin rule or the trial-eligibility rule. AC-06 tests only the trigger classification and the denial of a stale successor.
PROPOSAL: Define how an upgrade trial runs. For example, a separately committed and bound candidate worker definition, or an exact candidate-commit ref, named by a successor trial definition on the default branch, while the scheduled caller keeps the approved worker. Alternatively, have the gate read the trial definition from a reviewed candidate commit that current-default policy names. State how the caller comparison applies during that interval. Add an AC-06 case where an execution- or permission-changing upgrade trial passes while the scheduled worker still runs the approved pin.

KIND: factual

EVIDENCE: The recorded owner decision sends "changed execution, provider/reviewer identity, permissions or publication behavior" and uncertainty to a new trial (00081 line 14). AC-06 lists "execution/provider/reviewer/permissions/scan decisions/publication" (00241 line 5). D assembly freezes "every frozen policy/worker/scanner/component binding" (00161 line 42). Afterwards, "current protected publication/worker inputs must continue to match the adopted record" (00161 line 44), but the protected set is never listed. Upgrades are classified from "the full old-to-new shared/consumer diff" (00161 line 52). FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (superseded closure); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: Closure is allowed once every hunk is "applied/apply-modified in the published consolidated PR" or carried "in that published PR's decision list". It "happens only after the consolidated PR exists at the reviewed head" (00081 line 72). Publication opens an advisory or resolve PR and "never authorizes merge" (00161 line 4). If the owner later rejects, closes or reworks the consolidated PR without merging, the original proposals are already closed. Their applied and carried hunks then survive only in an unmerged PR, which defeats "Preserve uncertain proposals" (00081 line 64) and the requirement that incomplete members "cannot disappear from the selected scope" (00241 line 3). Add an AC-04 case for a consolidated PR that is closed without merging.

KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix

EVIDENCE: The reusable worker "owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps", and setup must "bind its exact blob" (00161 line 40). The protected manifest contains the "reusable-worker blob and effective caller contract" (00161 line 50). A compatible upgrade prepares C "with the new component pin", and the gate accepts it "only when the preserved behavior/policy bindings match and the current component equals the newly reviewed/tested component" (00161 line 62). A "changed worker execution path" is a retrial trigger (00161 line 64). The spec never says where the pin lives. If the pin is inside the bound reusable-worker file, as the install ownership implies, every compatible bump changes a protected exact blob. Under the stated rules, that change either fails "preserved bindings" or counts as an execution change that needs a trial. The owner-selected compatible route then has no possible passing case.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual

The trial and review evidence are "immutable artifacts" and "separately retained external artifacts" outside the tree (00161 lines 10, 42). The new helper follows the existing artifact-hash pattern (00081 line 22). That pattern rehashes files under a private, owner-only local `artifact_root` (`scripts/audit/adoption.ts` lines 27-36; `scripts/maintenance/migration.ts` lines 21-29). FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual

"Unexpected source/proposal changes ... or conflicting later edits stop that closure plan" (00081 line 72). Base freshness has a mechanical read-set rule, but that rule covers publication (00161 line 14). Closure gets only the undefined word "unexpected". The spec does not say how "contains" is checked: ancestry of the reviewed head, the merged PR's head SHA, or applying the reviewed diff against the merge tree. Depending on the reading, closure is impossible on an active or squash-merging repository, or it can close proposals after a later revert.

FINDING: F-04

### R-10

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (superseded closure after merge); Entry and consumer contract (lifecycle route); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: Under the owner's settled policy, closure "happens only after the consolidated PR is observed merged from its reviewed head" (00081 line 72). The owner merges later, so the resolve run that published the PR has already ended. Closure is therefore always a separate later run. To perform it, that run must know the consolidation's "head SHA equal to the independently reviewed published checkpoint", "the approved consolidation-body digest", each file's "expected postimage raw-blob SHA" and "the same bounded assessment read set used for publication" (00081 line 72). The spec never names the durable, trusted record that supplies those bindings to the later run. Review evidence stays "in the PR body and separately retained external artifacts" (00161 line 10). The PR body is mutable data that cannot authorize itself (00081 line 28). An external artifact may be unavailable to another machine or run. The spec also gives no entry for this follow-up. The lifecycle route names only "recheck and consolidate these docs-audit PRs" for resolve (00081 line 24), and "closure-only assessment/review" is defined only for the changed-input case (00081 line 72). AC-04 tests closure states, but no test starts closure in a fresh run that holds only the merged PR.
PROPOSAL: Define the post-merge closure entry: its lifecycle request example, the intent it selects (a resolve sub-mode or resume) and whether a scheduled run can perform it under an adopted grant. Name the authoritative source of the reviewed checkpoint, body digest, postimages and read set, for example a retained resolve record bound by digest in the published PR and verified against the reviewed head tree. Add an AC-04 case where closure runs in a new session or on another machine.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (C/D assembly, first post-cutover gate); Upgrades after adoption; Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: For upgrades, the spec says the unattended advisory trial alone "cannot verify a changed resolver" and "A missing affected-surface trial leaves the upgrade unverified". An upgrade that changes resolve publication or closure must also pass "an additional bounded actual model resolve trial ... using the exact installed candidate" that observes "consolidation publication, denial before merge, verified post-merge explanation/closure, and abandonment/revert refusal" (00161 line 70). First adoption turns on shared resolve publication and superseded closure for the first time (00161 lines 20, 22). Yet its prerequisites are only "the observed trial, paired review and normal checks" (00161 line 30), and the attestation records only "trial-passed and paired source-candidate acceptance" (00161 line 44). The first-adoption trial covers only the advisory draft-PR handoff (00161 line 32). Migration requires synthetic actual model trials only in general terms (00161 line 76), and AC-06 names the collector trial only for "affected resolver/closure changes" in an upgrade (00241 line 19). As a result, a first cutover could enable real-corpus closure that no installed-model trial has exercised, a weaker standard than the rule for an upgrade that changes the same surface.
PROPOSAL: Make the installed-model resolve/closure collector trial an explicit first-adoption prerequisite. Bind its result in the pre-cutover attestation and check it at the C/D assembly. Add that requirement to AC-06 and AC-07.
