### R-11

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (routing at every stage); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (cutover); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The legacy publisher "does not participate in the new lease" (00161 line 27). Cutover switches both routes and disables every legacy publication and closure entry "in the same reviewed change" as `publication.mode: adopted` (00161 lines 25, 35). After cutover, "A current entry must refresh its committed policy/routing before effects; an obsolete cached instruction is not a supported alternative publisher" (00161 line 25). The spec calls such an entry unsupported but never prevents it from running. A legacy scheduled run, or a legacy interactive resolver session, that started before the cutover merge keeps executing the pre-cutover legacy instructions. It never rereads the new policy and never takes the lease. Rollback explicitly says "first quiesce shared publication under the same lease" (00161 line 23), but cutover has no matching step to quiesce or drain legacy runs. The AC-06 race test covers only "the old interactive entry against the shared scheduled publisher after cutover" (00241 line 24), not a legacy run already in flight when cutover merges. The unserialized legacy-versus-shared race from R-2 F-01 therefore stays open for the first post-cutover shared publication.
PROPOSAL: Require cutover delivery to first quiesce legacy publication and then confirm it. For example, disable or cancel the legacy scheduled workflow and observe that no legacy run is queued or in progress, and confirm no legacy interactive resolver session is active. Only then enable shared publication. Alternatively, make the first shared publication wait until the cutover is older than a bounded legacy-run window. Add an AC-06 case with a legacy run that starts before cutover and is still running when the first shared publish attempt starts.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (post-merge closure and fresh-session entry); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: Closure must GET-observe "the consolidation's unchanged head SHA equal to the independently reviewed published checkpoint" (00081 line 73). The resolve record deliberately contains neither "its own future checkpoint ID" nor review results (00081 line 75). Review records and native evidence live "in the PR body and separately retained external artifacts" (00161 line 15). A fresh post-merge session starts with "only the consolidation PR ID", treats the PR-body locator as "an untrusted hint", and needs no "historical private cache" (00081 line 77). It also states that the fresh review "can verify the old checkpoint now without claiming to recover or prove its original pre-publication review" (00081 line 77). That session has no trusted source for the reviewed checkpoint SHA. Comparing the observed head with itself is a tautology, and the only other value is the mutable PR body. Suppose an owner or another writer pushed commits onto the consolidation PR before merge. The fresh session cannot tell whether the merged head is the reviewed checkpoint. Only the touched-file postimage hashes in the record give partial coverage, and unlisted extra changes to other files go unseen. The rule that a "changed original/consolidation head/body pauses closure" (00081 line 73) therefore cannot be evaluated in the AC-04 fresh-session case (00241 line 22).
PROPOSAL: Name a trusted, fresh-session-readable binding for the reviewed checkpoint. One option is to identify the commit that added the resolve record and require the merged head to equal it, with the PR's full GET commit list showing no later commits. Another is for the closure review to compare the full merged consolidation diff against the record's declared touched-file set and refuse any extra path. Alternatively, drop the equality claim and say that closure relies on that full-diff comparison. Add an AC-04 case where a commit is pushed to the consolidation PR after review and the PR is then merged.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (component pin); Upgrades after adoption; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: Each component pin is "a separately committed immutable YAML pin under `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-components/<id>.yaml`" that holds the source repository, commit, release, plugin version and component SHA-256. The source SHA and release annotation must stay "visible to the project's existing dependency updater, with an extraction fixture for that pin" (00161 line 33). A compatible upgrade "adds the new immutable component pin" and switches the caller's selection (00161 line 69). Prior pins are kept for rollback, which "selects the prior approved pin" (00161 line 73). A Renovate or Dependabot custom manager rewrites the matched SHA and version in place in whatever file it matches (delivery.md 00081 lines 30-33, 59-66). An updater PR would therefore modify an adopted pin file that the spec calls immutable. It would also leave the component SHA-256 and plugin version stale, because the updater cannot compute them. Every retained historical pin also matches, so each rollback target gets its own bump PR. The spec never says which pin files the updater should match, how an in-place updater proposal becomes the new-file successor procedure, or that updater PRs must never merge as-is. An accidental merge would corrupt the active pin or the rollback pin. The gate would then deny publication, and rollback to that pin would be refused.
PROPOSAL: Name the single updater-tracked location. Examples are the active pin only, or a separate non-executing upgrade-signal line that a custom manager matches. Exclude historical pins from updater matching. State that an updater PR is a signal that is never merged as-is, and that the successor procedure creates the new pin file with a recomputed component digest. Add AC-06 cases where an updater edit to an active or historical pin is detected and denied.
FINDING: F-04
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (authority table); Resolution and feedback
EVIDENCE: The authority table lists "read-only assessment/report output; scoped prepare/resolve edits and generators; local commits; pushes and advisory/resolve PRs; issue creation; superseded-PR closure; merged-branch cleanup; and merge/release effects" (00161 line 7). Closure requires a separate tracker write: "Post an authorized explanation linking that merged PR and the per-hunk dispositions before closing" (00081 line 73). The lease acquire and release are remote ref writes (00161 line 29). Neither appears as a row. The existing authority table also lists each remote write explicitly (authority.md 00001 lines 10-32).
PROPOSAL: Add rows for the PR explanation comment and for lease-ref acquire and release, each with the authority it requires. Alternatively, state that each one is included in the closure or publication grant it serves.

### R-12

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (fresh post-merge closure review); Independent candidate review and publication (base freshness); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: The publisher pushes the reviewed checkpoint on its assessed base. A disjoint default advance "does not invalidate the original reviewed checkpoint; do not rebase, regenerate or amend that checkpoint to follow it" (00161 line 19). The closure review "compares the complete Git diff from the recorded publication base to the actual merged head against the record's exact changed-path set plus the resolve-record path itself". It also says "Unlisted paths, extra hunks that change a declared postimage ... deny closure" and "A later commit added to the consolidation after publication cannot pass merely because it carries the old record" (00081 line 77). Suppose the consumer requires branches to be up to date before merge, or the owner uses GitHub "Update branch". The merged head then contains a merge from default. A two-point diff from the old publication base to that head includes every interim default change as unlisted paths. Closure is then always denied for a mechanical branch update, even though the spec explicitly allows unrelated default advances. The closure-only recovery path covers only changes after the merge result, so it does not help here. The spec says neither whether such update merges are allowed nor how they are excluded, and AC-04 has no case for them.
PROPOSAL: Define the closure diff so that changes merged in from default are excluded, while conflict resolution and authored branch commits remain visible. For example, compare the declared changes with the diff from the merge base of the merged head and the merge-result parent on default, or reject such updates explicitly as a stated consumer constraint. Add an AC-04 case for a consolidation PR updated from an advanced default before merge.

KIND: factual

The Design requires unattended publication to have "a committed nonempty `candidate_reviewer.requested_model` in consumer policy, with the provider selecting that model without fallback" (00161 line 17). ### R-13

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication (update-merge constraint); Authority and delivery (authority table, publication lease); Test Plan AC-04, AC-05
EVIDENCE: The spec does not support an update merge from default into a consolidation. It requires `git rev-list --merges <recorded-base>..<head>` to be empty. A branch refresh "needs a fresh clean preparation against the new default, updated resolve record, complete assessment and independent review, followed only by an authorized PR update". It also says "If the consumer requires an up-to-date branch, perform that refresh before merge" (00161 line 24). The new candidate is based on the new default, so the already-published PR head is not its ancestor. Updating the existing PR to it is therefore a non-fast-forward push that replaces published history. The other option is to open a new consolidation PR and close the old one. The authority table lists only "pushes and advisory/resolve PRs", "superseded-PR closure" (of original proposals) and lease effects (00161 line 12). Shared authority requires separate approval for "history rewrites" (authority.md 00001 line 30). The lease section bars force only for a foreign lease (00161 line 36). No row, lease recheck or test says whether the refresh force-updates the owned consolidation branch, what authority that needs or how the replaced head is checked, or whether the refresh opens a new PR and closes the old consolidation. Neither route is defined, and neither is in the original-proposal closure rules. AC-04 tests only "the supported fresh-clean-candidate/review route", not the remote effect that delivers it.
PROPOSAL: Name the refresh delivery effect. Option one is an exact-owned force-with-lease update of the consolidation branch, conditioned on the previously published head, under the publication lease. Option two is a new PR followed by closing the replaced consolidation PR. Add an authority-table row with its required grant, and AC-04/AC-05 cases for an authorized refresh, a refused refresh when the branch head changed and a refusal without that grant.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication (reviewer identity, runtime gate); Resolution and feedback (fresh closure review); Protected adoption bindings; Owner decisions; Test Plan AC-04, AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The settled upgrade policy sends a changed "provider/reviewer" binding to a new live trial (00081 line 20). The protected manifest includes "provider/requested-reviewer identities" (00161 line 66). The runtime gate checks "exact current component/worker/reviewer/publisher bindings" (00161 line 30). Only unattended publication must match the committed `candidate_reviewer.requested_model`. Interactive candidate review allows "the configured host default and its honestly reported identity", which can be `MODEL: unknown` under review-protocol.md lines 31-38 (00161 line 22). After cutover, an interactive local resolve run can perform real-corpus resolve publication and superseded closure (00161 lines 32, 36). Each post-merge closure also requires "a fresh independent closure review" (00161 line 2), but the spec never says which reviewer identity that review must have, or whether the "unattended publication" model rule covers unattended closure. As written, a real-corpus publication or closure can rely on a reviewer model that is unbound, unattested or recently changed. That makes the settled "changed reviewer requires a trial" rule and the gate's reviewer binding meaningless for those routes. The AC-05 identity tests cover only unattended publication.
PROPOSAL: State whether interactive real-corpus publication and every closure review (interactive and unattended) must match the committed expected reviewer model, or are intentionally excluded from the reviewer binding with that limit recorded. Add AC-05/AC-06 cases for interactive resolve publication and post-merge closure with matching, different and `unknown` reviewer identities.

The reused module's import graph goes further. `scripts/audit/inventory.ts` line 1 imports `./runtime`, and `scripts/audit/settings.ts` line 3 imports `../triage/settings`, which imports `./types` (triage/settings.ts lines 3-4). `scripts/audit/runtime.ts`, `scripts/triage/settings.ts` and `scripts/triage/types.ts` exist in the shared tree but are not in covers.
PROPOSAL: Add `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/audit/runtime.ts`, `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/triage/settings.ts` and `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/triage/types.ts` to covers. Alternatively, move the reused Git/hash primitives into a neutral module so these imports no longer affect docs-drift.

KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (scoped docs-drift locations); Resolution and feedback (owned assessment object store); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (scratch fixture repository)
EVIDENCE: Authority adds exactly "two scoped docs-drift locations": an assessment checkout and an owned prepare/resolve branch or worktree (00161 line 10). Resolve also needs "a recorded owned assessment object store" into which it read-only-fetches selected PR head and base objects (00081 line 72). The trial creates "an isolated scratch Git repository" for the fixture (00161 line 44). The spec does not say whether these are the assessment role, which may be "an existing owned checkout" whose object store and FETCH_HEAD a fetch would write to, or separate resources. If they are separate, it does not give their creation, ownership recording or cleanup authority.
PROPOSAL: State that the PR object store and the trial scratch repository either belong to a named docs-drift role or are separate temporary resources outside any caller checkout. Give their recording and non-forced cleanup rules, and require fetches that leave caller refs and FETCH_HEAD unchanged.

### R-14

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Owner decisions; Protected adoption bindings and ordinary scan inputs; Upgrades after adoption; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: Upgrades defines a compatible change as one that "preserves assessment/scan contracts, selected inputs and decisions, worker execution, provider/reviewer identity, permissions, secret exposure, lease behavior and publication/closure semantics" (00241 line 3). A new trial is required only for changes to "the protected execution/protocol, provider/reviewer, permission/secret, lease or publication/closure behavior" and for uncertainty (00241 line 9; Owner decisions 00081 line 23; AC-06 00241 line 40). The protected-inputs section says a change to the "worker invocation, decision or effect-authority contract" is classified "through re-adoption" (00161 line 75), but it never says which route that is. The resolve collector trial runs only "when resolve publication or superseded closure changes" (00241 line 11). That leaves a known change to shared assessment or resolve decision behavior without a route. Examples are new rules for historical documents or a changed hunk-classification or supersession rule. Such a change is not compatible, because it does not preserve decisions. It also matches no trial trigger and is not uncertain. No trial is required to exercise it, so it could take a path with neither the compatible-route checks nor a trial.
PROPOSAL: State that every shared change that fails the compatible definition takes the trial route. This includes a known assessment, resolve-decision or scan-contract change. Also require the installed-model resolve/closure collector trial when resolve classification or supersession decisions change. Use the same wording in Owner decisions, the protected-inputs section, Upgrades and AC-06, and add an AC-06 case for a decision-only procedure change.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (real unattended trial, trial definition, caller comparison); Independent candidate review and publication; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The trial handoff is "a draft PR" with "allowed draft-only effects" (00161 lines 51, 55). The trial is valid only when "Expected differences are limited to trigger, the explicitly bound synthetic input/output namespace and that fixture materialization", and "The candidate-review and publication steps themselves are unchanged". Any different "publisher behavior invalidates the trial" (00161 line 59). The caller comparison allows differences only in `on` and "the specifically declared literal synthetic-input/output values under `with`" (00161 line 59). The spec never says whether production scheduled advisory or resolve PRs are drafts (00161 lines 19, 25; legacy PR publication at 00001 line 69). If production opens ready-for-review PRs, the draft flag is an undeclared publisher difference. Under the trial's own rule, that difference invalidates the trial; otherwise production runs a PR-creation path the trial never exercised.
PROPOSAL: State the production advisory and resolve PR draft state. Either make production match the trial, or declare draft state as an explicitly bound trial input in the trial definition and caller `with` comparison. Add an AC-06 assertion that the scheduled caller's PR-state input matches the declared production value.

Each of these is said to need separate explicit authority, but none has a table row. The shared authority table lists each remote write explicitly (authority.md 00001 lines 10-32).
PROPOSAL: Add authority-table rows for workflow dispatch, workflow disable/enable, run cancellation, trial draft-PR creation and trial branch/PR cleanup, each with the exact grant it requires.

### R-15

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (authority table, shared defaults); Independent candidate review and publication (publication gate, staged interval); Test Plan AC-05
EVIDENCE: The authority table lists "issue creation" as a docs-drift effect (00161 line 15). Shared defaults also say that "commits, pushes, advisory PRs, issue creation, superseded-PR closure and branch cleanup each retain their actual consumer/current permissions" (00161 line 19). No Design section says when audit or resolve creates an issue, what the issue contains, or which finding scope it covers. Independent review is required only for "every prepared advisory or consolidated resolve change" (00161 line 23). The staged gate denies only "shared real-corpus push, PR and closure effects" (00161 line 37), and the staged interval lists "push, advisory/resolve PR publication and superseded-proposal closure" (00161 line 43). The lease serializes "pushing or closing superseded proposals" (00161 line 45). Under these rules, a pre-cutover shared run with an issue grant could publish real-corpus findings as issues. That issue would bypass the staged denial, the exact-candidate review the owner chose and the lease, and could run alongside the legacy worker. AC-05 checks issue creation only as an unauthorized-action denial.
PROPOSAL: Either remove issue creation from docs-drift's supported effects, or define the issue payload and its source finding scope. In the second case, require independent review of the exact payload and the adopted reviewer identity, put issue creation under the staged gate and the lease, and add AC-05 cases for a pre-cutover issue request and an issue request without review.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scan-result protocol and local correction; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (three stages); Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The trial must use "the consumer scanner" (00161 line 53), and the shared helper accepts only protocol v1. So the Stackweaver scanner correction must land before the trial, while "the legacy scheduled worker remains active" (00081 line 23). The correction "emits the bound protocol directly or through its local adapter" (00081 line 55). The spec says only that "advisory/strict exit semantics and detector findings remain unchanged" and that findings and suppressions are compared before and after (00081 line 55). It never requires the existing report shape that the legacy worker and legacy resolver read to stay unchanged. It also adds no test that the unchanged legacy worker works on the corrected scanner's output. Rollback restores routing but "preserves scanner" bytes (00241 line 21), so the "old worker as the scoped rollback target" (00161 line 69) also runs on the corrected scanner. The three-stage order (00161 line 51) does not say when the correction is delivered.
PROPOSAL: Place delivery of the scanner correction in the stage order before the trial. Require the legacy report fields the legacy worker reads to stay byte- or schema-compatible, for example by emitting protocol v1 only through the adapter or a separate output. Add AC-06 cases that run the legacy scheduled and interactive routes, and the rollback target, against the corrected scanner.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (quiescence and cutover, thin callers); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: Cutover disables the legacy scheduled workflow in the control plane. Then "Only after merge, current shared routing/gate verification and no legacy publishers may the selected shared schedule be enabled and the first shared publication proceed" (00161 line 63). AC-06 says "First shared publication and schedule enable must wait for that verified quiescence and delivered routing/gate" (00241 line 42). The spec never says whether D's scheduled caller is the existing legacy workflow file, whose disabled state persists, or a new caller file. A new workflow with a cron trigger is active as soon as D merges, and the runtime gate allows "the first adopted publication" without any post-cutover observation (00161 lines 39, 67). As written, the required post-merge verification before enabling the schedule has no mechanism and may never happen. The rollback text, "re-enabling legacy publication" (00161 line 63), also assumes a particular file identity.
PROPOSAL: State that D changes the existing scheduled workflow in place, so the control-plane disable stays in effect until an authorized enable, or that D delivers the scheduled caller without a cron trigger and a later reviewed change adds it. Name the workflow identity that the pause, enable and rollback act on, and add an AC-06 case where D merges and no scheduled shared run starts before the authorized enable.

### R-16

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication (reviewer identity); Protected adoption bindings; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (trial record); Test Plan AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: Under the settled upgrade policy, a changed provider or reviewer needs a new live trial (00081 line 23; 00241 line 13). Real-corpus effects require a committed `candidate_reviewer.requested_model`. The returned `MODEL:` is compared "using the protocol's family/full-ID rule" (00161 line 29). Under that rule, "An alias matches any ID of its family" (review-protocol.md lines 52-54). The protected manifest binds only "provider/requested-reviewer identities" (00161 line 77). The trial records the observed "provider/model identities" (00161 line 63), but the runtime gate compares each run only with the committed requested value. A policy such as `requested_model: opus` would therefore accept a later family member that the host silently substitutes, with no trial and no denial. The same gap applies to the scanning worker's model. Scope preserves the consumer's provider and model (00081 line 15), but the worker's per-run returned model is never compared with the model the trial observed. A host-default upgrade changes the provider/model identity without any detection.
PROPOSAL: For adopted policy, require an exact full model ID with no alias, for both the candidate reviewer and the worker invocation. Alternatively, require each run's returned IDs to equal the full IDs recorded by the trial or adoption. Treat a difference as the settled new-trial trigger, and add AC-05/AC-06 cases for an alias policy and a same-family model substitution.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Entry and consumer contract; Independent candidate review and publication; Test Plan AC-01, AC-07
EVIDENCE: The parent child contract requires "Harness integrations remain thin adapters with an explicit tested capability matrix" and "Unsupported operations and owner-approved test deferrals stay visible" (shared-workflow-migration.md 00081 lines 7-10). The draft has no capability matrix. A search of the spec for "capability matrix" or a per-host support table finds nothing. The matrix matters here because every interactive real-corpus publication, explanation and closure must use one committed `requested_model` "selected without fallback" (00161 line 29). Under review-protocol.md lines 48-50, a host that cannot pass that model leaves the review pending. So whether interactive resolve publication and post-merge closure work depends on the host, for example on Codex compared with Claude Code or Copilot. Meanwhile AC-01 claims one harness-agnostic workflow, and AC-07 mixes Codex/Claude installs with "actual Copilot trials" (00241 lines 27, 45). Nothing states which host is proven for which mode.
PROPOSAL: Add a tested capability matrix with one row per host (Codex, Claude Code, Copilot, hosted unattended runner) and columns for report/prepare, independent reviewer dispatch with the committed model, interactive resolve publication, post-merge closure and unattended worker. Mark each cell tested, unsupported or unclaimed. State that a host which cannot select the committed reviewer model is limited to report/prepare with a pending-review handoff, and map each cell to an AC-01 or AC-07 proof.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Owner decisions; Upgrades after adoption; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The owner's recorded upgrade choice is: independent review and installed checks for compatible updates, and a new live trial for changed execution, provider/reviewer, permissions or publication, or for unknown compatibility. The spec defines a compatible change only as "a reviewed display-only change or unrelated vh-adlc change" (00241 line 7). Owner decisions then lists further trial triggers that were not in that choice: "known assessment, resolve-classification/supersession, scan-contract ... lease" (00081 line 23; 00241 line 13). Taken together, a behavior-preserving refactor or a narrow bug fix in docs-drift helpers is neither display-only nor unrelated. Every such change would need a separately authorized paid unattended trial, and some would also need the resolve/closure collector trial (00241 line 15). The risk-based compatible route then covers only changes that do not touch docs-drift. That may be the intended conservative reading, but the paragraph presents it as the owner's selection, and Open Questions says "None" (00241 line 53).
PROPOSAL: Ask the owner to confirm the trade-off. Option A: the compatible route covers only display-only or unrelated changes, so almost every docs-drift code change pays for a trial. Option B: behavior-preserving docs-drift changes, shown by independent diff review plus regression and installed checks, also count as compatible. Under B, known decision or contract changes still take the trial route. Until the owner answers, keep the expanded trigger list out of the owner-attributed sentence.

### R-17

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Owner decisions; Upgrades after adoption (compatible definition and trial route); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The restored compatible definition says it "includes behavior-preserving docs-drift refactors/fixes". It also says such a change "preserves assessment/scan contracts, selected inputs and decisions" (shared-docs-drift.md 00241 line 23). Every change that fails this definition, "including known assessment, resolve-classification/supersession, scan-contract" changes, takes the trial route (00081 line 23; 00241 line 29). A changed resolve decision also triggers the collector trial (00241 line 31). AC-06 says to "Test a decision-only procedure change" on the trial route, and says a "behavior-preserving docs-drift refactor/fix uses the compatible route ... a decision or contract change cannot" (00241 line 60). A bug fix changes some output by definition, for example a hunk the old code misclassified or a document it wrongly marked accurate. The spec never says what "preserved" is measured against: the accepted documented contract, or the previous bytes' observed decisions. Under the second reading, almost no real fix qualifies as compatible, and the owner's restored compatible route for fixes becomes nearly empty. Under the first reading, the AC-06 "decision-only procedure change" case and the collector-trial trigger need a rule that separates a fix restoring the contract from a change to the contract. The shared lifecycle already draws that line for bounded maintenance: a regression fix "restores" the published contract, while changed promised semantics use the feature route (lifecycle.md 00081 lines 26-33).
PROPOSAL: Define behavior preservation against the accepted docs-drift contract. A fix whose independent review and failing-then-passing regression show that it restores documented assessment, classification or publication behavior is compatible. A change to the documented rules themselves takes the trial route, including the collector trial when resolve decisions change. Add AC-06 cases for a contract-restoring classification fix on the compatible route and a classification-rule change on the trial route.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (issue creation); Capability evidence by host; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (first-adoption trials); Upgrades after adoption; Test Plan AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: Issue creation is a supported real-corpus publication. It uses the staged/adopted gate and the lease, and the gate denies it only before cutover (00161 lines 21, 29, 61, 63). After cutover it is therefore enabled. First adoption requires two trials: the unattended advisory trial, whose handoff is "a draft PR" (00161 line 71), and the collector trial, which observes "consolidation publication, denial before merge, verified post-merge explanation/closure, and abandonment/revert refusal" (00241 line 31). Neither trial exercises issue creation. The capability matrix has no issue column (00161 lines 41-47). The spec's own rule is that "A missing affected-surface trial leaves the upgrade unverified even when its other trial passes", and that first adoption "must exercise both supported surfaces" (00241 line 31). Yet a later change to issue publication, which counts as "publication ... behavior", maps to no trial that can observe it. That is the same gap R-10 F-02 fixed for resolve publication and closure.
PROPOSAL: Choose one rule. Either add issue creation to the collector trial's synthetic scenarios and to the capability matrix, and require that observation for first adoption and for issue-affecting upgrades, or keep shared real-corpus issue creation unclaimed and denied after cutover until a separately trialed adoption enables it. Add the matching AC-06/AC-07 proof.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication (full-ID role binding); Capability evidence by host; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (trial record); Test Plan AC-05
EVIDENCE: Before real-corpus effects, the scanning/preparing worker must have a "current native returned identity" equal to the adopted full ID. That includes a host that "performs the role itself" (00161 line 35). A host that cannot "select/attest the adopted full worker/reviewer identity" is limited to report/local preparation (00161 line 39). The trial records "provider/model identities" (00161 line 79). The only identity-return mechanism the spec reuses is the reviewer's `MODEL:` header, and the protocol forbids inferring identity "from the main session's model picker" (review-protocol.md lines 34-36). The spec never defines where a worker's native identity comes from: provider machine-readable result metadata, a required header in the worker's returned manifest, or a session self-report. It also does not say which of these counts as attestation for an interactive host that performs the worker role itself. Without that, the AC-05 tests for "absent/unknown scanning-worker identities" have no defined input, and a host cannot know whether it qualifies for real-corpus effects.
PROPOSAL: Specify the accepted worker-identity sources for each role and route. Examples are a native provider result field for hosted runs and a required `MODEL:` line in a returned worker manifest for delegated runs. State that a self-performing interactive host without such a native source is limited to report/local preparation. Bind the chosen source in the trial definition and test each source and its absence in AC-05.

### R-18

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Independent candidate review and publication; Audit and preparation (report output); Scheduled worker and staged adoption; Test Plan AC-02, AC-05, AC-06
EVIDENCE: After cutover, the unattended worker "publishes only after a clean matching result" (shared-docs-drift.md:187). Publication is also prevented by "A missing reviewer, model mismatch or blocking/should-fix finding" (line 189). An incomplete scan "prevents clean/ready claims" (lines 121, 300). The current-default gate can also deny a run (lines 217-219). Report mode saves "a report only at an explicitly selected output location" (line 147). Scope keeps the legacy notification behavior and rules out "an additional notification mechanism" (line 95). The legacy advisory PR is the owner's visible signal. The spec never defines what a non-publishing unattended run leaves behind: its job status, where its report and gaps are retained on the hosted runner, or whether an incomplete assessment may still publish an advisory PR that discloses its gaps. Only the passing trial path is observed (lines 233, 241). AC-05 checks only that a failure has no "clean-success fallback" (line 303). With the new read-failure accounting, one persistently unreadable document or one should-fix review finding could stop every scheduled run with nothing visible to the owner, so accumulated drift would stop surfacing.
PROPOSAL: Define the unattended non-publication outcome for incomplete assessments, blocked or mismatched reviews and gate denials. For example, the run ends with a non-success job status and retains a bounded report artifact naming the gap, using GitHub's existing run notification. State whether an incomplete assessment may publish a disclosed partial advisory PR. Add AC-05/AC-06 cases for a scheduled run with an incomplete scan and one with a should-fix review result.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Upgrades after adoption; Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The AC-06 proof requires running "a compatible upgrade with independent diff/dependency review, actual new-version installed checks and a successor record". It also requires running "a separately authorized default-branch dormant candidate worker/trial definition; its new-version execution/permission trial can pass without changing the approved scheduled worker" (shared-docs-drift.md:304). The Design says "Test a permission- or execution-changing candidate trial passing while the old approved scheduled worker continues to pass its production gate" (line 273). A trial-route upgrade means "another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version" on "an actual hosted runner and selected provider/model" (lines 273, 233). At first adoption there is no successor version. Read literally, AC-06, the criterion that completes this feature, needs all of these before it can be met: a second published release, new-version marketplace installs, and a second paid live trial that adds dormant candidate files to Stackweaver's default branch. Neither the stage order (line 231) nor Migration and rollback (lines 279-283) mentions that work or its authorization. The proofs could also be meant as hermetic adoption-helper/gate tests with synthetic pins, workflows and receipts. The spec does not say which.
PROPOSAL: State that the AC-06 upgrade-route proofs are hermetic adoption-helper and runtime-gate tests using synthetic pins, caller workflows, records and receipts, and that actual installed checks and live trials apply only at real later upgrades. If a real successor exercise is intended, add it to the staged order with its release, trial and default-branch authorizations.
FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery (issue creation); Test Plan AC-05
EVIDENCE: The issue payload carries "the exact repository/title/body/labels, source finding/PR scope, current evidence, disposition and unknowns". An issue-only run keeps "its payload/review artifacts external and digest-bound" without a local checkpoint (shared-docs-drift.md:181). The pinned documentation convention, which this operation lists as a required page (line 115), says "Findings live in documents. An investigation, measurement or root cause goes into the repository's analysis or feature document with its evidence. An issue states the work in a few lines and links that document" (documentation.md lines 52-57). Under the current design, the evidence and reasoning go into the issue itself. An issue-only run also has no committed or reviewable document for the issue to link.
PROPOSAL: Make the issue body a short statement of the work that links a committed or published report or document holding the evidence, dispositions and unknowns. Alternatively, record why docs-drift issues are an exception to the pinned convention. Add an AC-05 assertion on the issue body shape.

After a trial-route upgrade, the signal could stay at the old release, so the updater keeps proposing a version that is already adopted. ### R-19

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Owner decisions; Upgrades after adoption (compatible definition, compatible pin bump, trial route); Protected adoption bindings; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The settled owner policy sends "changed execution/provider/reviewer/permissions/publication" to another live trial. The draft repeats this rule in three places: "execution/provider/reviewer/permission/publication changes still require a new trial. Static YAML validation and a local interactive model call cannot satisfy this condition" (00241 line 1), and the trial route covers changes to "execution" (00241 line 35). The protected manifest separately binds the "reusable-worker blob and effective caller contract", the "install/worker/reviewer/publisher invocations" and the "runner, execution environment, job permissions and secret-name mappings" (00241 line 17). That reusable worker "owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps, runner, environment and permission requirements" (00241 line 1). The compatible route, however, also allows "A component/worker refactor ... only when independent review and the affected regression/installed checks demonstrate preserved execution", and says "raw byte changes are not automatically behavior changes" (00241 line 31). The gate then accepts "changed component/worker hashes" that have a compatibility mapping and installed/regression evidence (00241 line 33). The only installed checks the draft defines are "actual fresh/upgrade installed-payload checks for the new exact version" (00241 line 33), meaning Codex/Claude marketplace installs (00241 line 39; AC-07). None of these checks runs the hosted reusable GitHub workflow. The draft never says whether "worker" here means the shared docs-drift procedure inside the component or the bound reusable-worker workflow blob. Under the second reading, a refactor of the unattended workflow's install, invocation or publisher steps could start real-corpus publication on the compatible route without being executed even once. That is the same static-only evidence the first-adoption rule rejects, and it contradicts the settled rule that execution changes take the trial route. AC-06 tests "a behavior-preserving worker/component refactor" on the compatible route (00241 line 68) without separating these two cases.
PROPOSAL: Define the boundary. A change to the reusable-worker workflow blob or to any protected caller/execution field (install, runner, environment, permissions, secret mapping, or the worker/reviewer/publisher invocation and steps) is an execution change and takes the trial route. The compatible "worker refactor" exception covers only the shared component's procedure and helper code, which installed checks can actually exercise. Add an AC-06 case where a behavior-preserving edit to the reusable-worker workflow is denied on the compatible route, and a separate case where a component-internal worker-procedure refactor passes.

### R-21

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Entry and consumer contract (new helpers); Independent candidate review and publication; Authority and delivery (publication lease); Resolution and feedback (closure sequence); Test Plan AC-05
EVIDENCE: The listed new helpers are `scripts/docs-drift/settings.ts`, `snapshot.ts`, `scan.ts`, `report.ts`, `adoption.ts` and `cli.ts`. They provide "validation, input binding, scan checks, report formatting and installed adoption consistency" (00081 line 31). The Design also needs "One common publisher" shared by production and the trial (00161 line 57), an "authorized local wrapper" that checkpoints, reviews and publishes (00161 line 27), a lease with acquire and release (00161 line 67), force-with-lease refresh (00161 line 33), issue creation, and a comment-then-close sequence for each original proposal (00161 line 1). The interactive Copilot and Claude entries and the hosted worker must all reach that same publisher (00161 line 51). No helper, entry point or transport seam is named for any of these effects. Recovery is defined only for an interrupted lease (00161 line 67) and an interrupted force-with-lease (00161 line 33). Nothing covers a lost response after PR, issue or comment creation, or a run that stops between the explanation comment and the closure. A search of the Design for lost-response, idempotency, journal or retry text finds nothing. The existing shared audit already has this mechanism: durable intents for `issue`, `push` and `merge` (`scripts/audit/journal.ts` line 10), and `recoverEffect`, which observes actual state before it replays (`scripts/audit/recovery.ts` lines 5-25). The parent contract requires each child to identify the shared behavior it reuses (shared-workflow-migration.md 00081 lines 3-4). The Design neither reuses nor excludes this mechanism. As written, a scheduled run that times out after PR creation can open a duplicate advisory PR on the next run. An interrupted closure batch can also post duplicate explanations.
PROPOSAL: Name the shared publisher/effect module and its CLI entry, used by the hosted worker and both interactive entries. Name its transport seam for the hermetic collector and say what stops production from selecting that collector. Define durable intent and observe-before-replay recovery for each push, PR, issue, comment and closure effect, by reusing the audit journal/recovery pattern or stating why it is excluded. Add AC-05 cases for a lost PR-creation response and a closure batch interrupted after its comment.

Before cutover, the existing `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` still selects the legacy resolver (00161 line 63), so this check must run against candidate forwarding bytes that are not yet on default. The full-ID rule binds every protected role or entry to IDs "observed in the applicable passing adoption trial" (00161 line 35), but the attestation has no Claude-entry record to supply those IDs. So nothing mechanically ties the required Claude check to C's exact command blob or to its bound external role IDs. A missing or stale Claude result is therefore not detected by operator verification or by the gate.
PROPOSAL: Add the Claude forwarded-entry result to the required attested artifacts. Bind it to C's exact `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` blob, the forwarded arguments, and the native worker and reviewer IDs it dispatched. KIND: factual

EVIDENCE: Consumer settings give the scanner only as "an argument vector" (00081 line 37). The production argv the spec cites is `node scripts/docs-audit.js --churn --prune --protocol-json <owned-output>` (00161 line 77), and the spec never says what `--prune` does. Report mode must leave "consumer docs, feedback, indexes, Git index and refs unchanged" (00081 line 67). The scanner reports stale suppressions (00001 line 68). The protocol has no side-effect contract for the scan invocation. It does not require the scan to be write-free, and it does not require the helper to compare the tree and index after the scan. ### R-23

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Entry and consumer contract (helper reuse); Scan-result protocol (raw blob digests); Authority and delivery (PR object store); Test Plan AC-02, AC-03
EVIDENCE: The spec reuses "`hash` and the Git-read helpers exported by `scripts/audit/snapshot.ts`" and requires "byte-exact `gitBytes`" for paths, inventories and blobs (shared-docs-drift.md 00081 line 35). Protocol v1 requires document entries to "match the bytes of their blob at `source_commit`" (00081 line 57). The scan helper must snapshot and compare "index bytes, HEAD/refs and FETCH_HEAD" (00081 line 45). The PR store must make "no caller refs or FETCH_HEAD writes" and must never fetch "into an existing caller `.git`" (00161 line 23). `snapshot.ts` `gitBytes` runs `git -C root` with `{ ...process.env, GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS: "0" }` and nothing else (snapshot.ts lines 16-22). So it inherits `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` and related variables, follows `refs/replace` substitutions, and allows promisor lazy fetches. The shared docs-impact collector already handles this risk. It strips those variables because "Git exports these when calling hooks", sets `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, and runs Git with `--literal-pathspecs -c core.fsmonitor=false` (docs-impact/collection.ts lines 26-31, 87). The spec does not mention any of these controls. With the named helper, a hook-invoked or partial-clone run could hash replaced or wrong-repository bytes, snapshot another index, or write objects during a "read". That breaks the raw-blob agreement between scanner and helper and the caller-preservation guarantees.
PROPOSAL: Require every docs-drift Git read, fetch and snapshot to use the hardened docs-impact Git environment and arguments. One way is to move that environment into a neutral exported module that both features use, keeping only `hash` from audit. Add AC-02/AC-03 cases for an inherited `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE`, a replace ref on a document blob and a promisor lazy fetch. Each must leave the assessment incomplete or produce the unaltered bytes, with no caller-state change.

The normative Upgrades text also sends "permission/secret, lease or publication/closure rules/behavior" and "resolve-classification/supersession" changes to the trial route. It requires the non-pin "secret" caller fields to stay exact (00241 lines 49, 53). The protected manifest lists "secret-name mappings" and "lease policy" as separate bindings (00241 line 35). The summary sentence that cites the owner therefore names neither secret-name mappings nor lease behavior. A reader could take a lease or secret-mapping change to be compatible, even though the Upgrades section and AC-06 route it to a trial.
PROPOSAL: Add secret-name mappings, lease behavior and supersession/closure rules to the Owner decisions trial-route sentence, or have that sentence point to the Upgrades trial-route list without restating it.

### R-25

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Upgrades after adoption; Scheduled worker and staged adoption (component pin); Capability evidence by host (post-cutover interactive entries); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The settled policy keeps the current approved pin active until a verified successor is approved. The spec defines that pin only for the hosted worker. "Its future shared worker obtains the published vh-adlc source at an exact full commit" and "The active caller selects the approved pin" (00241 line 2). The required post-cutover interactive publishing entries are "the installed `vh-adlc-docs-drift` operation invoked through Copilot CLI, and Stackweaver's existing Claude Code `/docs-drift` command forwarding to that operation" (00161 line 62). Cutover "disables/replaces every legacy real-corpus publication and closure entry" (00161 line 74). The runtime gate checks the "exact current component/worker/reviewer/publisher bindings" (00161 line 72). The Upgrades section says "Interactive runs using an unadopted upgraded component may assess/prepare, but current-default binding checks deny their real-corpus publication. The approved pinned worker continues to operate during that review. No legacy fallback is silently re-enabled" (00241 line 42). The compatible definition includes "unrelated vh-adlc changes" (00241 line 46), so later vh-adlc releases are expected to change the installed payload. The pin records a "plugin version and component SHA-256" (00241 line 2), but the spec never says whether that digest covers only the docs-drift files and their transitive dependencies or the whole plugin payload and version. The spec also defines no way for an interactive Copilot or Claude host to install, load or verify the approved pinned component instead of the host's current marketplace install. Post-merge closure is interactive unless an unattended resume is separately adopted (00161 line 8). Once a host's installed plugin moves to any later release, including one unrelated to docs-drift, every interactive resolve publication and superseded closure is denied until a full C/D re-adoption completes, and no legacy route is available. For those entries, the approved pin is not actually "active", which conflicts with the settled policy. AC-06 tests only the hosted approved worker staying usable.
PROPOSAL: Define what the component digest and version binding cover, and whether a release that does not touch docs-drift files changes them. Then specify how an interactive Copilot or Claude publishing entry runs or verifies the approved pinned component, for example by having the helper verify or load the pinned source in an isolated profile. If that is not supported, state as a visible limitation that interactive real-corpus publication and closure pause after a host upgrade until re-adoption. Add an AC-06 case where an interactive host is upgraded to a newer release while the approved pin is still selected.

EVIDENCE: Every real-corpus interactive effect now runs through "the consumer's thin pinned launcher", which resolves the approved pin, provisions the isolated profile and loads the snapshot. The spec says "The launcher and its protocol form part of the consumer's protected entry contract" (00241 line 44). For Copilot, that contract also includes the loader arguments and profile controls: `--plugin-dir`, `COPILOT_HOME` and disabled automatic updates (00241 line 46). The closed protected manifest does not name the launcher, its path or these profile and loader controls (00241 line 34). Instead it lists component, reusable worker and callers, invocations and identities, runner/permissions/secrets, the protocol validator, routing and lease policy, and it states "missing/unknown protected fields ... cannot establish compatibility". The C/D attestation binds an entry by blob only for Claude, through C's exact `.claude/commands/docs-drift.md` hash (00241 line 22). The runtime gate likewise checks only the "current Claude command blob/forwarding contract and bound role IDs" (00241 line 24). Nothing binds the launcher blob or the Copilot entry's loader and profile configuration to the passing collector trial, to C or to the runtime gate. The AC-06 entry checks also cover only "changed command blob/forwarded contract" (00321 line 13). As a result, a launcher edit between the trial and C, or after adoption, goes undetected. The spec also never says whether such an edit is ordinary assessment freshness, compatible re-adoption or a trial-route execution change, even though the launcher selects which code publishes.
PROPOSAL: Name the launcher path and add its blob, together with the Copilot loader and profile invocation fields, to the closed protected manifest. Bind them to the collector-trial result and the C/D attestation, as the Claude command blob already is, and have the runtime gate check them. Classify a launcher or loader change as an execution change on the trial route, and add AC-06 cases where a changed launcher or profile flag denies the gate.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Upgrades after adoption (hermetic upgrade proofs); Approved interactive runtime; Test Plan AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: The Design says the AC-06 upgrade, re-adoption and rollback proofs are "hermetic helper/runtime-gate scenarios using synthetic version pins ... They need no second published release, second paid hosted trial" (00241 line 64). The AC-06 proof nevertheless requires: "Upgrade the front-door Copilot/Claude host plugin to a later release, including an unrelated release, while retaining the old pin: require actual pinned-loader operation and synthetic resolve/closure through the old verified profile" (00321 line 13). That is an actual model operation on two hosts, and it assumes a later release exists. At first adoption the adopted release is normally the newest one, so this proof cannot run until another vh-adlc release ships, and the criterion that completes the feature would wait on it. The spec does not say whether the "later release" may be a locally built synthetic plugin directory with a higher version, or whether this belongs under AC-07's actual-operation evidence instead of the hermetic AC-06 set.
PROPOSAL: State that the front-door upgrade case uses a locally built, clearly synthetic later-version plugin payload installed in the disposable host profile, with the actual pinned loader. Alternatively, move the actual-operation part to AC-07 and keep AC-06's version of this case hermetic. Either way, state explicitly that no second published release is needed before AC-06 can be met.

### R-27

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Protected adoption bindings and ordinary scan inputs; Approved interactive runtime; Upgrades after adoption; Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: Line 274 says the protected manifest is closed: "missing/unknown protected fields or uncertain dependency influence cannot establish compatibility". The manifest binds the component, the reusable-worker blob, invocations, "provider/requested-reviewer identities", the runner, the environment, the launcher blob and the `runtime_loader` contract. It does not bind the provider client that executes the worker and reviewer roles: the Copilot CLI (or other provider CLI) build used on the hosted runner and on each interactive host. Line 286 disables only the client's automatic self-update inside the dedicated profile. It says to "verify duplicate/ambient plugin precedence rather than assuming it" and records the "front-door host version" only as reported provenance. Line 292 classifies upgrades from "the full old-to-new shared/consumer diff". A provider-client upgrade on a host, or a floating client install in the worker, changes plugin loading, precedence and native identity-event behavior without any consumer or shared diff. The upgrade routes therefore never classify it, and the gate cannot detect it. That runs against the settled trial trigger for changed "execution/provider" and against the spec's own uncertain-influence rule. provider-loading-evidence.md:7,15 also says loading behavior "must be tested, not inferred".
PROPOSAL: Bind the provider client identity, either an exact version or a verified checksum, for each protected role in the trial and adoption record and in the hosted install step, and have the runtime gate and launcher preflight compare it. It adds a persistent, separately owned runtime profile and source cache on every participating host, with credentials provisioned into it (line 286). PROPOSAL: Extend the Scope list of explicit migration changes with the launcher and `runtime_loader`, the per-host runtime profile and credential provisioning, the Claude command's move to bound external roles, and the CI signal and updater configuration. Keep the statement that none of these authorizes credential rotation or a different scanning model.

The reviewer agent says "your records are stored exactly as you return them" (vh-adlc-reviewer.agent.md:55-56), and `review check` compares the stored text (review-protocol.md:73-74). Evidence lines and Context citations are dated by design: "a `file:line` ... gathered when the claim was written" (feature-documents.md 00001 lines 75-76). A whole-corpus link/file-reference scanner will flag many of these in complete and planned feature documents. Such an edit corrupts the review evidence that gates rely on. AC-03 tests only the normative and claimed cases.

### R-28

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Approved interactive runtime (provider client identity); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (upgrade signal); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The R-27 F-01 resolution makes each provider client a protected pinned dependency. It says to "Bind an exact native release version and, for custom/development builds or non-unique versions, a verified code-distribution checksum". It also says "the hosted install step selects that fixed build rather than latest" and "Provision the approved client in an owned retained runtime-client cache/profile" (shared-docs-drift.md 00241 line 49). The pinned delivery convention has two requirements here. First: "Download executable tools at an exact version and verify an independently pinned checksum before executing them." Second: "Every dependency must be discoverable by the repository's update tool, or it silently stops being updated ... A version and checksum hardcoded in a script are invisible to Dependabot and Renovate" (delivery.md 00081 lines 55-66). The spec requires a checksum only for custom or non-unique builds, so a normal release download of the hosted or interactive client runs after a version check alone. The spec defines updater discovery only for the vh-adlc component, and says "The only updater-matched location is a separate non-executing `.vh-adlc/docs-drift-upgrade-signal.yaml`" (00241 line 3). It names no updater-visible location for the new client pins. It also does not say whether an updater proposal for a client pin is intake only, the way a component signal is. As written, the adopted client builds either stop receiving update proposals, or an updater bump to the hosted install step or the `runtime_loader` reaches the execution path without the intake-only and no-automerge protections the component pin has.
PROPOSAL: Require every protected client download to verify an independently pinned checksum, as delivery.md requires. Name an updater-visible, non-executing signal for each protected client pin, and state that a client updater PR is intake only, with automerge false, for the trial route. Add AC-06 cases for a missing checksum and for detection of a client signal merged as-is.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Approved interactive runtime (provider client identity); Upgrades after adoption (trial route, affected-surface trials); Test Plan AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: The spec says "Choosing a different protected client build is an execution change on the live-trial route", and "Compare each platform/entry's tested identity" (00241 line 49). The trial route is "another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version" for "Every shared change that fails the compatible-upgrade definition" (00241 line 63). The extra installed-model collector trial is required only when "resolve-classification/supersession rules, PR/issue publication or closure behavior changes". Also, "A missing affected-surface trial leaves the upgrade unverified" (00241 line 65). For launcher and loader changes, the spec does name the entry observation: "new entry observation, including a matching Copilot entry result" (00241 line 45). A carried Claude entry result needs "unchanged command/dispatch/role bindings" (00241 line 25). No rule names the observation needed when only an interactive client changes, such as a new Copilot CLI build or a new dispatched Claude worker/reviewer client. That change is not a "shared change", the hosted unattended trial never runs the interactive client, and it is not a collector-trial trigger. AC-06 tests the client case only as a hermetic denial: "selecting a new build requires its trial route" (00321 line 16). So it is unclear whether a hosted trial alone, which never runs the changed client, would wrongly pass this upgrade, or whether a new installed entry or collector trial on that host is required. The reverse case is also open: does a hosted-client-only change need the hosted trial alone?
PROPOSAL: Map each protected client change to its affected-surface observation. A hosted worker or reviewer client change needs the hosted unattended trial. An interactive Copilot or Claude client change needs a new actual installed entry/collector result on that platform and entry, bound in C/D, with the hosted trial needed only when hosted execution also changes. Add matching AC-06/AC-07 cases.

### R-29

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Upgrades after adoption (trial-route paragraph and resolve collector trigger); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (caller comparison); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The restored compatible boundary measures behavior preservation "against the accepted docs-drift contract at the adopted revision". A fix is compatible "when independent review identifies the unchanged documented rule and a failing-then-passing regression demonstrates restoration of that rule" (shared-docs-drift.md 00241 line 59). "A compatible change includes such contract-preserving docs-drift refactors/fixes" (00241 line 61). The compatible exception "covers only the shared component's internal worker procedure and helper code" (00241 line 63). AC-06 requires "a contract-restoring classification fix with an old-fail/new-pass regression on the compatible route" (00321 line 20). Two trigger sentences say otherwise. The trial-route paragraph says, without a non-compatible qualifier: "A change to shared rules/helpers used by the hosted worker requires another separately authorized live unattended trial at the proposed version" (00241 line 67). Every compatible internal helper refactor or fix is a change to helpers that the hosted worker uses. The collector-trial rule requires an installed-model resolve trial "again when accepted resolve-classification/supersession rules, PR/issue publication or closure behavior changes" (00241 line 69). A contract-restoring fix changes observed behavior by definition, so this reads as sending the AC-06 compatible classification fix to the collector trial. The reusable-worker section similarly says "execution/provider/reviewer/permission/publication changes still require a new trial" (00241 line 19) without saying whether a publication fix that restores the contract counts. Under the literal reading of these sentences, the owner's accepted compatible route for behavior-preserving fixes is empty for any helper the hosted worker or resolver uses. The adoption helper's classification cases would then have no consistent pass condition.
PROPOSAL: Qualify each trigger sentence with the contract boundary. For example: "A shared change to hosted-worker rules/helpers that fails the compatible definition requires another live unattended trial", and "when the accepted resolve-classification/supersession, PR/issue publication or closure contract changes". Then state once that a contract-restoring fix with independent review, an old-fail/new-pass regression and installed checks needs neither the unattended trial nor the collector trial.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Acceptance Criteria AC-04, AC-06; Test Plan AC-04, AC-06; Design, Owner decisions
EVIDENCE: The owner settled two end states. First, superseded originals close only after the consolidated replacement merges: "Merely opening/publishing the replacement is insufficient", and "An abandoned or closed-unmerged replacement leaves every original proposal open" (00161 line 5). Second, the approved pin stays active until a verified successor is approved through the compatible or trial route (00241 lines 57, 65, 67). Neither end state appears in any criterion. AC-04 covers only feedback and that stale proposals "cannot silently reintroduce rejected or unsupported changes". AC-05 covers reports, review before publication and effect scope. AC-06 covers first adoption: "staged scheduled-worker cutover after an observed unattended trial, retained local scanner/feedback/site rules and a conflict-safe rollback path" (00321 lines 6-8). Both decisions are tested only inside the AC-04 and AC-06 proof cells (00321 lines 18, 20). The standard requires a criterion to state "The end state, observable without reading the code" (feature-documents.md 00081 line 1). An implementation review returns a verdict against the exact criterion text (feature-documents.md 00161 lines 11-25). As written, the settled closure-after-merge and post-adoption upgrade outcomes are mentioned in Design and the Test Plan, but they are not criteria that a review must find met.
PROPOSAL: Extend AC-04 so that an original proposal closes only after its merged consolidation is verified to carry every hunk's disposition, and otherwise stays open. Extend AC-06, or add a new criterion, so that after adoption the approved pin and record stay active until a successor is approved: compatible changes through independent review and installed checks, all other changes through the affected-surface trial route.

### R-30

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Common publisher and effect recovery; Upgrades after adoption (installed-model collector trial); Capability evidence by host; Test Plan AC-05, AC-06, AC-07
EVIDENCE: The common publisher says "The production CLI always constructs its native transport and accepts no report/settings/environment switch to a collector. A separate hermetic test driver may inject a collector into the same publisher API" (shared-docs-drift.md 00161 line 29). It also says "Both interactive entries and the hosted wrapper invoke the same implementation" (same line). The first-adoption collector trial requires "a bounded actual model resolve trial using the exact installed candidate ... through a hermetic remote collector". In that trial, "The actual model executes the installed resolve procedure; the tracker transport and merge event are fixtures" (00241 line 69). The Copilot entry trial and the Claude `/docs-drift` check must exercise "the actual loader", the retained launcher and "synthetic publication and closure through the collector" (00161 line 63; 00241 lines 23, 47). Resolve input is read through the GET-only `github-read.ts` reader and a fetch from "the verified consumer origin and its fixed PR ref" (00081 lines 29, 79). That reader always spawns the real `gh api` (github-read.ts 00001 lines 54-58). The installed procedure reaches effects only through `cli.ts publish`, and that CLI cannot select a collector. So the spec never says how an unmodified installed procedure, run by a real model through the real launcher, gets synthetic PR reads, synthetic merge events and a collector for its effects. If the trial calls the separate test driver or patches the procedure, it no longer runs the installed entry the trial is meant to prove. If it uses a PATH, environment or endpoint override, that is the switch the production CLI forbids. The required collector cases in AC-05, AC-06 and AC-07 therefore have no defined way to run that still meets both rules.
PROPOSAL: Define the trial seam explicitly. One option is a separately invoked, test-only installed entry that loads the same procedure and publisher, verifies a committed synthetic trial context and refuses any real repository. Another option is to make the transport boundary a fixture GitHub/Git endpoint that only the bound trial definition can select, with production refusing it. State how synthetic reads, object fetches and merge observations reach the procedure. Add an AC-05 case showing that the production entry cannot reach that seam.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Capability evidence by host; Approved interactive runtime; Migration and rollback; Test Plan AC-01, AC-07
EVIDENCE: After cutover, one required interactive publishing entry is "the installed `vh-adlc-docs-drift` operation invoked through Copilot CLI" (00161 line 63). The design says "The marketplace skill is intake" and that it forwards to the retained launcher (00241 line 45). Reports must state the "front-door host version" (00241 line 47). Migration requires only "actual native Codex and Claude marketplace fresh installs/upgrades, discovery and installed execution" (00241 line 77). AC-07 likewise asks only to "Observe actual Codex/Claude marketplace fresh installs/upgrades" (00321 line 21). The Copilot matrix cell asks for "a bounded actual installed-model trial", but names no install source and no discovery check (00161 line 59). The parent contract requires each capability to be "installed from its published marketplace source and exercised through its declared supported entry points before the consumer's legacy route is retired" (shared-workflow-migration.md 00081 line 29). Its adoption steps are to "install it from the actual marketplace in an isolated profile, verify its payload and native discovery" (00081 lines 14-16). The spec never says how the Copilot front-door skill is installed or discovered. It could come from a marketplace, a `--plugin-dir` checkout or a local copy. So the Copilot entry could retire the legacy route without the install and discovery evidence the parent contract requires, and the Copilot trials could run against a front door that differs from what users install.
PROPOSAL: State how the Copilot front door is installed, for example a published marketplace install or a documented supported plugin source, and require an observed isolated install and native discovery of `vh-adlc-docs-drift` there before cutover. Add that observation to Migration, the Copilot matrix cell and AC-07, or record an owner-approved deferral that leaves the Copilot publishing entry unclaimed.

EVIDENCE: Consumer settings define "the scanner invocation as an argument vector, intended documentation scope, optional `read_set.source_roots` and `read_set.reference_roots`, required detector-unit IDs, per-unit document selectors and options, feedback files, project procedure and allowed verification/generation commands" (shared-docs-drift.md:117). After an authorized edit the worker must "Apply the consumer's generators and source/visual checks" (line 173). That happens inside the reusable worker, which "owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps, runner, environment and permission requirements" (line 267). The ordinary input set lists "corpus/source content and history, local detector implementation/rules/options, detector-unit/input selectors, documentation roots/exclusions/suffixes and suppression/correction history" (line 285). Neither list names the allowed verification/generation commands, the scanner argv or `read_set`. The allowed commands run with the hosted job's write permissions and mapped secrets. Under the settled policy, changed execution, permissions or secret exposure takes the trial route, but the owner decision sends only "Local detector rules, ordinary scan scope/options and feedback" to normal freshness (line 103). `read_set` limits the publication freshness comparison (line 235), so narrowing it weakens a publication gate. Add AC-06 cases where changed verification/generation commands, scanner argv and `read_set` each follow their declared route.

### R-32

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Protected adoption bindings and ordinary scan inputs (settings leaf classification); Scheduled worker and staged adoption (committed trial block, first post-cutover gate); Upgrades after adoption (dormant candidate trial route); Test Plan AC-06
EVIDENCE: The settings classification has only two classes. It says "The ordinary-settings allowlist is documentation roots/exclusions/suffixes, required detector IDs and declarative input selectors, typed detector-option values and feedback file/data selection. Every other recognized setting is protected" and "A schema test requires every field to have exactly one classification" (shared-docs-drift.md chunk 00241 line 43). Trial eligibility depends on "A committed `trial` block in current-default `.vh-adlc/docs-drift.yaml` [that] names the trial-definition path and raw-blob digest" (00241 line 23). That block is not on the ordinary allowlist, so it is protected. After adoption, "current protected publication/worker inputs must continue to match the adopted record" (00241 line 33). The upgrade design says something different. It says "Dormant upgrade-trial definitions and candidate worker files may be added through separately authorized trial setup without changing the active production manifest", that "additions to this trial setup are therefore not a reason to invalidate the approved production adoption record", and that "The active manifest excludes ... unselected dormant candidates" (00241 line 47). A trial-route upgrade needs "the current-default trial definition [to bind] their exact blobs" while "The active scheduled caller still references the old approved worker and record" (00241 line 75). Pointing the default-branch `trial` block at a successor definition changes a protected settings field. By the classification and the gate rule, that change denies every production real-corpus effect for as long as the upgrade trial runs. Removing or changing the first-adoption `trial` block after cutover has the same effect. The AC-06 proof "exercise a dormant candidate worker/trial definition ... without changing the approved worker gate" (00321 line 28) therefore has no consistent pass condition, and the settled rule that the approved pin stays active until a verified successor is approved does not hold during an upgrade trial.
PROPOSAL: Give the `trial` block its own explicit classification, for example trial-setup: excluded from the active protected manifest and runtime gate, but strictly bound by each trial and by the trial eligibility check. Alternatively, move trial selection into a separate committed file outside the protected settings. Add an AC-06 case where adding, changing or removing the default-branch `trial` block leaves the approved production gate passing, while a change to any other protected setting still denies it.

### R-33
