Two readings are therefore possible for a contract-restoring fix to the reviewer instructions, such as restoring a documented check of feedback files that the reviewer skipped. Under the general definition it is compatible. Under the "covers only" sentence it is not, so it falls to the trial route without any listed trigger. The AC-06 proof repeats the narrower wording, "a component-internal worker-procedure/helper refactor can pass" (00321 line 34). The owner-accepted route covers compatible updates in general, not a list limited to worker and helper code. This sentence brings back the narrowing that the current draft says it removed.
PROPOSAL: Rephrase the sentence so that the refactor/fix exception covers the whole shared docs-drift component payload, including procedures, the reviewer and routing references, and helpers, when the contract-based evidence is present. Keep the exclusion for the consumer reusable-workflow blob and protected caller/execution fields. Make the AC-06 proof match, and add one case with a contract-restoring fix to the reviewer instructions.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Acceptance Criteria AC-05, AC-06; Design, Owner decisions; Independent candidate review and publication
EVIDENCE: The owner chose independent review of the exact candidate before publication. The Design makes this exact: review binds the "exact candidate commit", "Both production and trial publish the exact independently reviewed candidate commit and tree", and "A changed head requires fresh candidate review" (00161 line 41). AC-05 says only "independent review precedes publication" (00321 line 21). A review of an earlier or different candidate, or one that does not cover issues, explanations and closures, satisfies that text. The owner also chose to keep the legacy scheduled worker "until a real unattended trial of the installed shared worker succeeds" (00081 line 25). AC-06 says "staged scheduled-worker cutover after an observed unattended trial" (00321 line 22), and a failed trial is also observed. The standard requires a criterion to state "The end state, observable without reading the code" (feature-documents.md 00081 line 1). An implementation review then decides its verdict against the exact criterion text (feature-documents.md 00161 lines 11-25). The stricter conditions appear only in the Design and the Test Plan cells. As written, both settled conditions are mentioned but are not part of the criteria that a review must find met.
PROPOSAL: Reword AC-05 so that every real-corpus PR, issue, explanation and closure is published only as the exact commit, tree and payload that a current independent review covered with the adopted reviewer identity. Reword AC-06 so that the legacy scheduled worker stays active and cutover happens only after an observed passing unattended shared trial.

### R-35

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (superseded closure after merge; fresh post-merge closure entry); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: Two closure rules give different outcomes for the same inputs. The first is in the closure-sequence paragraph (shared-docs-drift.md:167). It says closure "happens only after the consolidated PR is observed merged from its reviewed head". It then says "A bound-input change, unavailable comparison or changed original/consolidation head/body pauses closure for a fresh closure-only assessment/review at current default; it does not require inventing another docs PR. Reverify the selected dispositions and expected touched-file bytes there, and obtain fresh approval for any materially changed disposition/explanation." The second is in the fresh-session paragraph (line 171). It says the closure basis is a fresh full-diff review that "does not claim that this actual head equals an unavailable historical pre-publication review target". It also says "Unlisted paths, extra hunks that change a declared postimage, changed original proposal bindings, or an incomplete diff deny closure", with a recovery handoff and no closure. The AC-04 proof follows only the deny rule: "unlisted paths or changed postimages deny fresh-session closure" (line 352). The two rules conflict in two common cases. In the first, an original audit PR's head or body changes after resolve. In the second, the consolidation gains a commit after review, for example an owner edit before merge. Line 167 sends both cases to a closure-only reassessment that can approve the changed disposition again. Line 171 denies both outright. Line 167 also still requires the merge to come "from its reviewed head", which line 171 says cannot be established. An implementer of the post-merge closure entry cannot tell whether these cases should pause and reassess or deny with a handoff. The AC-04 test also leaves the line 167 path without a pass condition.
PROPOSAL: Choose one outcome for a changed original proposal and for a consolidation diff that differs from the record, then state it in both paragraphs and in AC-04. For example, limit the closure-only reassessment to later changes on the default branch and changes to proposal bodies, and deny with a recovery handoff when the consolidation diff or an original head differs from the record. Also replace "observed merged from its reviewed head" with the full-diff closure basis that line 171 defines.

### R-36

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (superseded closure, resolve record); Test Plan AC-04
EVIDENCE: A selected proposal can be superseded when a hunk is "already fixed with current evidence" (chunk 00161 line 7). Closure, however, "happens only after the consolidated PR is observed merged", and "Merely opening/publishing the replacement is insufficient" (same line). The resolve record is created "in that same candidate tree" before the publication checkpoint (00161 line 9). The fresh post-merge entry finds that record only through the merged consolidation head's full diff (00161 line 11). The spec never says what resolve does when no selected hunk produces a documentation or feedback change. That happens when every hunk is already fixed, or every hunk is wrong and its feedback entry already exists. In that case it is unclear whether resolve publishes a consolidation PR that contains only the record, or publishes nothing. If it publishes nothing, those stale originals can never satisfy the merged-replacement condition, so they stay open indefinitely, which is a common outcome for old drift PRs. The only defined no-change outcome is for unattended advisory runs (00241 line 41). AC-04 tests already-fixed hunks only inside overlapping proposals that also produce changes (00321 line 32).
PROPOSAL: Define the zero-correction resolve outcome so it stays consistent with the settled closure-after-merge rule. For example, require a reviewed consolidation PR that contains only the resolve record and carries the dispositions. Otherwise, state explicitly that such originals stay open with a handoff. Add an AC-04 case where every selected hunk is already fixed.
FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Entry and consumer contract (scanner invocation and Git controls); Protected adoption bindings and ordinary scan inputs; Scheduled worker (reusable worker); Test Plan AC-02, AC-06
EVIDENCE: The ordinary input set includes "local detector implementation/rules/options". Changes to it need a fresh scan but "no new adoption trial" (00241 line 47). The protected manifest separately binds "job permissions and secret-name mappings". It makes verification and generation command vectors protected because of what they execute (00241 line 45). The reusable worker "owns the install, scanner/worker, independent reviewer and publisher steps, runner, environment and permission requirements" (00241 line 29). The only environment controls the spec defines for the scanner are Git controls: "The scanner subprocess and its Git children receive those same trusted controls" (00081 line 33). The write-free check compares only the local worktree, index, HEAD/refs and FETCH_HEAD after the run (00081 line 43). It cannot observe a remote effect or credential use. The spec never requires the scanner child to run without the job's provider credentials, mapped secrets, GitHub token or persisted checkout credentials. So an ordinary detector-code change, which needs no trial, can run with the worker's secret exposure and write permissions. That contradicts the settled rule that changed permissions or secret exposure take the trial route.
PROPOSAL: Require the scanner and any other ordinary-input executable to run with an allowlisted environment that has no provider credentials, mapped secrets, GitHub token or Git credential helpers/extraheaders. Run it before secret-bearing reviewer or publisher steps receive those secrets. Add AC-02/AC-06 cases where a scanner tries to read a token or push, and require denial or an incomplete assessment.

### R-37

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Resolution and feedback (superseded closure: closure freshness and the fresh post-merge entry); Independent candidate review and publication (base freshness); Test Plan AC-04; AC-04
EVIDENCE: Publication-time freshness compares the assessed base with the default branch as observed under the lease when the PR is published. "A disjoint default advance may still permit publication of the unchanged candidate" (chunk 00161 line 49; chunk 00241 line 1). The PR then stays open until the owner merges it, and the spec allows squash, rebase and merge commits without an update merge (00161 lines 9, 49). At closure, the mechanical checks are narrower. "Verify these postimages at the observed merge-result tree. Then read current default, require the merge-result commit to be reachable there and compare its full later delta against the same bounded assessment read set used for publication" (00161 line 9). The fresh-session review "compares the complete Git diff from the recorded publication base to the actual merged head against the record's exact changed-path set" (00161 line 15). That is the consolidation branch's own diff. Neither check covers default-branch commits that land after publication and before the merge result, that is, between the published default and the merge result's default-side parent or rebase base. Postimages are verified only for declared touched files. "Already fixed with current evidence" dispositions, and the source evidence behind applied hunks, can depend on undeclared paths in the read set. Example: after publication, a default commit reintroduces the drift that an "already fixed" hunk relied on, and the consolidation is then squash-merged without an update. Every closure check passes and the original proposal closes. This contradicts AC-04, which says an original closes only after the merged consolidation "is freshly verified to carry every hunk or its terminal disposition" (00321 line 24). The AC-04 proof covers only "a later unrelated in-scope edit" and "later default input" changes (00321 line 36). That wording does not separate pre-merge default commits from post-merge ones.
PROPOSAL: Compare the closure delta from the recorded publication base, or the default commit observed at publication, through to current default against the bounded read set. Treat only the consolidation's declared paths whose blobs equal the recorded postimages as the expected transition. Any other bound-input change goes to the existing closure-only reassessment, or to a denial when record or postimage mismatches apply. Add AC-04 cases for squash, rebase and merge-commit merges where an in-scope default commit lands between publication and merge and changes evidence behind an already-fixed disposition.

### R-38

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Acceptance Criteria AC-02, AC-06; Design, Owner decisions; Scan-result protocol and local correction
EVIDENCE: The owner selected "the bounded scanner-completeness correction" (shared-docs-drift.md chunk 00081 line 25). The Design makes completeness depend on a check that does not trust the scanner: "The shared scan helper independently enumerates the expected documents from the exact Git tree" (00081 line 53), and "an echoed digest alone never establishes collection" (00081 line 59). It also requires delivering the Stackweaver correction "Before trial/interactive shared adoption" while legacy output stays unchanged (00081 line 63). None of the criteria states this end state. AC-02 says only "Assessments expose incomplete collection, unknown findings and stale inputs without claiming a clean or fully verified corpus" (00321 line 22). AC-06 says only that Stackweaver keeps "retained local scanner/feedback/site rules" (00321 line 26). AC-02 can therefore be met in two ways the owner did not choose. In the first, completeness rests on the scanner's own failure report. In the second, the correction is never delivered and every Stackweaver assessment stays permanently "incomplete", which still counts as exposing incomplete collection. The independent inventory comparison and the delivered Stackweaver correction appear only in the Design and the Test Plan cells. R-29 F-02 and R-34 F-02 fixed the same gap for the closure, upgrade, exact-review and trial decisions, but this decision was never added to a criterion.

Extend AC-06 so that Stackweaver's scanner reports skipped or failed reads and required-unit execution, while its legacy report, detector findings and exits stay unchanged, before trial and shared interactive adoption.

### R-39

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix

For those documents, prepare/resolve must never edit Review Log rows, Evidence/Residual blocks, Task completion history or dated Context/Sources provenance. For local-format repositories, the spec says only "Local-format consumers keep their project governance, documented matching dialect, plan rules and ownership gates". It does not say what happens when that retained local procedure does not name a document's protected history ranges or editable ranges. The standard rules do not apply in a local-format repository. If the procedure does not classify the document or range, report the proposed correction without editing, as docs-impact does for unknown applicability. Add an AC-03 case: a local-format plan with a stale path inside its recorded review or evidence text, and a procedure that does not classify that range. The case passes only if the plan stays byte-identical and the correction is reported.

FINDING: F-02

EVIDENCE: A run succeeds without publishing only when it has "no selected correction, unresolved owner decision or provisional/unknown verification". ### R-42

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix

It also says "Resolve prior reports only through consumer-configured report roots or recorded owned run artifacts" (shared-docs-drift.md chunk 00241 line 47). A run with no verified correction publishes no advisory (00241 lines 45, 47), so in steady state the prior report is the main way to carry attention. The PR path checks only that the "exact carried entry" is observed, not that it is still the independently reviewed body bound in the lease plan (00161 line 37). PROPOSAL: Name the source of earlier reports for the hosted and interactive routes. Examples are a committed report root in settings, classified like other settings, or earlier run artifacts of the recorded scheduled workflow ID on default-branch scheduled events. Bind the digest and provenance to the adopted worker run, and check PR-carried entries against the reviewed body recorded in the lease plan. Add AC-05/AC-06 cases where a planted artifact from another workflow or a pull_request run, or an edited PR body, cannot suppress new attention. Also require the hosted trial, or a recorded hermetic substitute, to read an earlier report.

EVIDENCE: The outcome rules give three results. "A complete assessment with no selected correction or attention may succeed with an explicit no-change report." ### R-44

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Scheduled worker and staged adoption (attention definition and unattended outcomes); Test Plan AC-05
EVIDENCE: A newly confirmed correction is not attention. A run that delivers one "succeeds as `published`, recording `attention: none|carried`" (shared-docs-drift.md chunk 00241 line 49). The only outcomes that publish nothing are `no-change`, which requires "no selected correction or attention", and `carried-attention`, which applies when the "only remaining" items are unchanged previously surfaced decisions or unknowns (00241 line 51). Unknowns and decisions are usually carried by "a reviewed advisory containing verified selected corrections" (00241 line 53). Under the current outcome rules, that run either publishes a second advisory as `published` or fits no stated outcome. The AC-05 proof requires, for unchanged attention "carried by an open owned PR", a green result with "no duplicate advisory" (00321 line 63). The Design never defines how to meet that pass condition.

Alternatively, keep the legacy behavior of proposing it again, record that as an intentional difference, and make the AC-05 assertion match. Add an AC-05 case where an open advisory carries both corrections and attention.

The lease is a "named ephemeral lease ref" that is released once effects are terminal (00241 line 15; 00161 line 41). The same `incomplete` outcome also comes from docs-impact-only problems: a malformed or duplicate `Documentation impact roots:` declaration, a declared root missing from HEAD, a tracked symlink in or above a root, or an undecodable filename under a root (chunk 00081 line 78; chunk 00161 lines 1-8 and 14-15). ### R-49

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md lines 239-241 ("Delivery acknowledgement during recovery", eligibility paragraph); AC-05 Test Plan acknowledgement cases (line 452); T78 (line 575)
EVIDENCE: The eligibility paragraph applies one rule to every effect: "Eligibility requires one unique native match for the original operation/effect marker, expected repository and actor, exact target and applicable head/branch bindings". The next sentence asks the spec to "Bind the pre-dispatch inventory or source payload so a marker that predated the selected effect cannot establish its application". The same paragraph then treats closures as eligible: "A closure requires direct terminal evidence for the closure itself in addition to its explanation". Only creation effects have a marker that this rule can test. In the current plan shape, `pr-close` carries only `number` and `expected_head`, with no marker (recovery.test.ts fixtures). `push` and `branch-refresh` are ref effects with no marker; transport.ts observes them only by ref object or PR head. `pr-update` edits an existing PR whose prior body is its source payload. Native observe() covers it through `mutablePull`/`updateTransitions`, not through a unique new marker. For pr-update, no direct per-mutation evidence is named, and none is available through the existing GET-only body comparison. As written, the marker requirement makes closure acknowledgement impossible, but the closure sentence makes it eligible. The pr-update, push and branch-refresh cases are left undetermined. "Account for every remaining effect through ... eligible acknowledgement" therefore has no defined meaning for those kinds. The AC-05 proof names only a "unique PR/issue/comment delivery" positive case, plus an "unproved closure" refusal. T78 says "effect-specific native proof" without the effect set. Implementers and reviewers cannot decide from the spec whether a reopened closed PR, an edited pr-update target or a force-moved pushed branch may be acknowledged or must stay pending.
PROPOSAL: In the acknowledgement subsection, list the eligible effect kinds and the proof shape for each. For pr-create, issue-create and comment-create, use a unique effect marker that is absent from the bound pre-review inventory. For pr-close, require direct native closure evidence for that PR number by the expected actor at the bound expected head, plus the already-proved explanation, with no marker requirement. For pr-update, either name the direct native evidence and source-transition binding, or state that pr-update is ineligible. State explicitly that push, branch-refresh, lease-acquire, receipt-retain and lease-release are ineligible, or define their proof. Limit the marker sentence to creation effects. In the AC-05 Test Plan, add a positive case for each eligible kind beyond creation, and a refusal case for each ineligible kind showing that the lease stays pending. Name the same effect set in T78.

### R-50

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, "Delivery acknowledgement during recovery" proof table rows `pr-create` and `pr-update` (chunks 00228, 00249); AC-05 Test Plan acknowledgement cases (chunk 00467); T78 (chunk 00585)
EVIDENCE: Native transport can partly apply one effect.
- **`pr-update`:** it can send up to three owned requests: `PATCH pulls/{n}` for title and body, `PUT issues/{n}/labels`, and an optional `gh pr ready`/`--undo` (transport.ts 00481 lines 14-24). `updateTransitions` already treats the intermediate states as valid: source, source+title/body, and source+title/body+labels (transport.ts 00061 lines 26-35).
- **`pr-create`:** the observer already reports a created PR whose labels are missing as `native-PR-labels-incomplete` (transport.ts 00361 lines 46-49).
- **Quiescence rule:** it counts "native evidence of non-dispatch, definitive rejection or terminal application" for each owned request (spec 00249 line 15). So a `pr-update` whose PATCH applied but whose labels request was never sent or was definitively rejected is fully settled.
- **Eligibility is undefined for that case.** The `pr-update` row asks for evidence of "applied field values" and binding to the "intended transition". The acknowledgement event means "the observed applied delivery and current mismatch" and "supplies no exact-completion or non-application verdict". The spec does not say whether a partly applied update or creation can be acknowledged as applied delivery. That matters because acknowledgement then permanently stops replay and allows lease release (00249 line 17).
- **No test or task covers it.** The AC-05 plan names whole-effect positive cases only: "the original source-to-approved-field transition followed by a later edit". It has no partial-application case, and T78 does not name one.
- **Two readings remain.** An implementer could acknowledge and release a half-applied effect, so its unapplied parts can never be replayed. Or they could keep it pending. A reviewer cannot tell which is correct.
PROPOSAL:
- **Proof table:** in the `pr-create` and `pr-update` rows (and in the general rule after the table), state that eligibility requires terminal application evidence for every native request the selected effect makes, covering its full approved payload. For `pr-update` that means title/body, labels and any draft-state change. For `pr-create` it means the created PR plus every requested label.
- **Partial application:** a settled but partly applied effect stays pending and is not acknowledged. This is the conservative default and matches the existing "leave that effect pending" rule. If acknowledging partial application should instead be allowed, it needs its own owner decision and its own report wording.
- **AC-05 Test Plan:** add refusal cases with the lease left pending:
  - a `pr-update` whose PATCH applied and whose labels request was definitively rejected or never dispatched, then edited later;
  - a `pr-create` whose label application is incomplete.
- **T78:** name these partial-application refusals.

### R-51

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Delivery acknowledgement during recovery": the `issue-create` proof-table row and the full-application paragraph after the table; Test Plan AC-05 acknowledgement cases; Tasks T78.
EVIDENCE: The `pr-create` and `pr-update` rows require proof that the full approved payload was applied, including labels. The `issue-create` row only requires "One unique native issue with the operation/effect marker and expected creator/target" plus "Direct native application evidence binds the created resource to that effect and attempt". It never mentions the issue's requested labels. The general rule after the table names only "every requested PR label and any draft-state change". Native issue creation sends the labels in the same request as the title and body (transport.ts 00481 line 27: `mutation(plan, "POST", `${prefix}/issues`, { title: p.title, body: p.body, labels: p.labels }, delivery)`). Native observation reports an issue whose labels differ from the requested set as `conflicted("native-issue-payload-changed")` (transport.ts 00421 line 23). That conflict is the changed-delivery state this acknowledgement path is meant to resolve. A created issue whose requested labels were never applied is therefore a partly applied effect. The current wording doesn't clearly classify it as one, and an implementer could accept the marker and creation evidence alone as eligible. The AC-05 refusals cover only PR cases: "a PR update whose text PATCH applied but whose labels request was never dispatched or definitively rejected", "PR creation with incomplete label application" and "an update with an unapplied requested draft-state change". T78 likewise names only "incomplete PR creation labels" and the PR update cases. No planned test refuses an issue created without its requested labels.
PROPOSAL: Add to the `issue-create` row: "Prove terminal application of the full approved payload, including title, body and every requested label." Change "every requested PR label" in the general rule to "every requested PR or issue label". Add an AC-05 refusal case and a matching T78 case for an issue created with incomplete or absent requested labels. In that case the lease stays pending, no acknowledgement is recorded and abandoning the missing labels is not authorized.

### R-52

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Delivery acknowledgement during recovery": the `pr-create`, `issue-create`, `comment-create`, `pr-update` and `pr-close` proof table rows and the native evidence binding paragraph (line 260); Test Plan AC-05 acknowledgement cases (line 469); T78 (line 592).
EVIDENCE: The Design requires every eligible effect to have native evidence bound "to the original request through the retained exact response or an independently retrievable native mutation record that establishes the listed resource, actor, applied transition and attempt bindings". It also says "If the native service supplies no such proof, leave that effect pending." The evidence has to bind the selected attempt, and that attempt ID is local. GitHub timeline and issue event records do not carry it, so in practice only a response that the executor retains at dispatch can supply the attempt binding.

The current source keeps no such response:
- `PublicationTransport.execute` returns `Promise<void>` (transport.ts 00001 line 36).
- Native `mutation()` runs `gh api ... --include --jq empty` (transport.ts 00301 line 6), which drops the response body on success.
- `pr-create` runs `gh pr create ... --label=...` (transport.ts 00481 lines 9 to 11), and its stdout identifies only the created PR. It does not show which labels were applied.
- `pr-update` is three separate commands: PATCH, then PUT labels, then `gh pr ready` (transport.ts 00481 lines 14 to 24).
- The `attempt-ended` payload holds only `attempt_id`, `sent`, `reaped` and optional `rejection` (journal.ts 00241 lines 20 to 24). It has nowhere to record success evidence.

The spec's own Task evidence records this suppression as deliberate. T43 (line 518) says "mutation response bodies suppressed". T54 (line 545) says "successful response bodies remain suppressed". Neither the Design nor T78 says that the executor must now capture and retain success evidence for each owned command, what bounded or hashed form it takes given that earlier sensitive-projection decision, or where the journal links it. They also don't say how journals and in-flight runs written before capture existed should be treated.

T78 lists transport.ts and attempts.ts but only asks to "Require effect-specific native proof". Every AC-05 positive case assumes "direct native mutation evidence" already exists. As written, an implementer could either:
- treat ambiguous timeline events as the "independently retrievable native mutation record", which the Design forbids, or
- find no admissible proof source for the owner-approved `pr-update`, `pr-close` and label-bearing creations, so acknowledgement never applies to them.
PROPOSAL: Add an evidence-capture rule to the acknowledgement Design.
- The native executor retains a bounded private artifact for each owned command, linked to the run, effect, attempt and command. For a successful mutation it records the HTTP status and the native resource ID, mutating actor, applied field digests, label IDs and draft state.
- Bodies are stored only as digests, consistent with the T43/T54 suppression.
- The `execute` contract returns or links this artifact, and the journal binds it, for example through a success-evidence field on `attempt-ended`.
- A multi-command effect needs evidence from every command. Where a CLI step cannot supply applied-state evidence, that component counts as unprovable and the effect stays pending.
- Journals and attempts recorded before this capture existed lack the binding, so they stay pending unless a native record establishes every listed binding.

Name the executor and `execute` contract change in T78. In AC-05, add these cases:
- refusal when the success artifact is missing, tampered or unlinked;
- refusal for a pre-capture legacy attempt;
- a positive case driven only by captured evidence.

### R-53

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md:262-266 (executor success-evidence capture and the retained-artifact exclusion), :598 (T78 file list)
EVIDENCE: The spec makes several demands of the new executor capture. It says to "Exclude raw response bodies, credentials, headers containing secrets and unrestricted stdout/stderr from retained artifacts". It says "The executor may parse bounded native response bytes transiently to construct the projection". It says to "Preserve the existing rejection evidence and settlement distinctions", and to keep evidence safe "across crashes and retries". The current executor conflicts with these demands in four ways:
1. Native command output streams straight into persistent files. `owned-process.ts:28` spawns with `stdout: Bun.file(stdout), stderr: Bun.file(stderr)`. Those paths are created in the retained `native-command-<id>` directory under the artifact root (`transport.ts:275-290`).
2. Response bodies are currently suppressed only by `--jq empty` (`transport.ts:306`). Capturing applied-field responses for the success projection requires removing that suppression. Once removed, raw response bodies and `--include` headers would land in those persisted stdout files.
3. Existing rejection proof depends on that persisted stdout. `rejections.ts:59-62` reads the stdout file and digests it. `rejections.ts:91` reads the command directory again whenever the proof is later verified.
Satisfying the exclusion, the transient parsing, crash safety and preserved rejection evidence together therefore requires changing `owned-process.ts` and `rejections.ts`, plus their tests. T78 names only `journal.ts`, `observations.ts`, `transport.ts`, `attempts.ts` and `delivery-ack.test.ts`. No task names those two files.
PROPOSAL: Add `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/owned-process.ts` and `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/rejections.ts`, with their existing tests, to T78. State in the capture paragraph how mutation-response bytes are captured: through a bounded in-memory or ephemeral path that never persists raw bodies or secret-bearing headers, including after a crash. Also state how existing rejection evidence stays verifiable and readable for already-serialized journals. Add a Test Plan case: a crash between command completion and projection persistence leaves no raw response body or header canary in the artifact root, and existing rejection proofs still verify.

### R-54

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md: the Design "Authority and delivery" paragraph after the authority table (chunk 00212). It conflicts with the "Delivery acknowledgement during recovery" capture and approval paragraph (chunk 00248) and the ownership and settlement text (chunk 00270). Related places are the owner summary (chunk 00061), AC-05 (chunk 00463) and Tasks T23, T25 and T79 (chunk 00598).
EVIDENCE: The authority paragraph puts "delivery acknowledgement in recovery" in the same list as the other recovery effects. It then says each one "requires the actual current request or an adopted consumer procedure that names that effect and scope." The approved acknowledgement contract is narrower. Chunk 00248 requires a fresh bounded lookup whose exact scope is shown to the owner. The approval is bound to the complete immutable scope and its evidence digests, and any changed binding needs a new lookup and a new approval. The owner summary says "This acknowledgement has its own approval scope." AC-05 says "An explicitly approved delivery acknowledgement." Chunk 00270 says "The owner's approval supplies permission." A standing adopted consumer procedure is written before the run, so it cannot name evidence digests that only exist after the lookup. The generic rule therefore lets an unattended adopted grant authorize acknowledgement, which contradicts per-instance owner approval. This matters in practice. Current recovery.ts `reconcileAbsent` uses one `authorize(scope)` callback, with the comment that the actual request or adopted-procedure controller names this cleanup. The reconciliation and native-transport tests drive cleanup and retirement through that same callback with synthetic request digests. T23 only says "Route an exact owner-approved acknowledgement request." Neither T25 (authority and lifecycle wording) nor T79 says that an adopted procedure cannot supply this approval. No planned test refuses an acknowledgement authorized only by an adopted procedure.
PROPOSAL: Change the authority paragraph so delivery acknowledgement is an exception to the "current request or adopted consumer procedure" rule. It should require the owner's actual current approval of the exact presented lookup scope and evidence digests. No standing adopted procedure, scheduled or unattended context, or publication, closure, retirement or cleanup grant can supply that approval. Carry the same sentence into T25's authority.md and lifecycle.md updates. Add a T79 and AC-05 Test Plan case that refuses an acknowledgement authorized only by an adopted procedure, or by a cleanup or retirement grant through the existing reconciliation authorize path. That case must record no acknowledgement, stop no replay and release no lease.

### R-55

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, the Delivery acknowledgement during recovery proof table and the success-evidence paragraphs (chunks 00247 and 00268), the end of the AC-05 Test Plan row (chunk 00475), the AC-07 Test Plan row (chunk 00477), and the T78 and T22 task entries (chunk 00598)
EVIDENCE: The acknowledgement Design only allows direct native evidence. Each eligible row needs "Direct native application evidence" or "Direct native mutation/closure evidence". The proof must come from "the retained bounded native response projection or an independently retrievable native mutation record". The capture contract extends "the native executor" and owned-process.ts so they parse gh and HTTP responses and record "the verified authenticated mutating actor". AC-05 and AC-07 also require an installed resolve collector case that proves "the expected non-success acknowledgement plus exact cleanup", along with "verified native-fixture resource preservation". The collector transport is a separate PublicationTransport implementation: transport.ts line 27 has kind "native" | "collector", and publication-fixture.ts line 70 has its own execute. Collector plans are kept apart from native production evidence (journal.ts line 77, journal.test.ts lines 61 to 67). The spec never says what success projection the collector's execute returns, which actor and request bindings it attests, or whether a collector-labelled projection can satisfy the proof table in a trial/collector plan. T78 changes the PublicationTransport.execute contract but lists only native files. T22 asks for acknowledgement outcomes from trial-entry but names no success-evidence source. As written, either the required collector acknowledgement case cannot pass, or an implementer must invent unreviewed collector proof semantics.
PROPOSAL: Add a sentence to the success-evidence Design. The collector transport should return a transport-labelled projection with the same schema, request, attempt and command bindings and the same integrity and permission rules. It would be produced by the collector's own execute against its owned fixture objects. This proof would be accepted for acknowledgement only in plans with context trial and transport collector, and could never satisfy a native or production acknowledgement. Add tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/publication-fixture.ts and plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/trial-entry.ts (or the collector module) to T78 or T22. Add AC-05 cases showing that a collector projection is refused in a production native plan, and that the installed collector acknowledgement passes only with executor-captured collector evidence.

### R-56

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Delivery acknowledgement during recovery", capture paragraph (chunk 00247 line 18); Tasks T78 (chunk 00600 line 1); AC-05 Test Plan acknowledgement cases (chunk 00477 line 1)
EVIDENCE: The capture paragraph has two rules that conflict. First, it says mutation capture must "extend `scripts/docs-drift/owned-process.ts` to drain stdout/stderr through bounded in-memory pipes" and "Never redirect these raw streams to retained files or temporary disk paths." Second, it says "Git rejection parsing retains its existing bounded ref-status proof." The only new format it defines is "versioned projected HTTP rejection evidence", and "New HTTP captures use the projected format." The existing Git ref-status proof depends on retained stdout files. Git pushes are owned native mutation commands: transport.ts:308-314 `pushRef` calls `run(..., { protocol: "git", updates })`. transport.ts:280 writes `stdout`/`stderr` files into the native command directory, and owned-process.ts:28 spawns with `stdout: Bun.file(stdout)`. rejections.ts:46-59 `observedCommand` then reads `join(directory, "stdout")` from disk again on every verification. attempts.ts `pendingAttempt` uses that proof to settle rejected lease-acquire, receipt-retain, push, branch-refresh and lease-release attempts, and acknowledgement cleanup depends on those attempts settling. The current tests also rely on rewriting that file-backed Git proof. native-transport.test.ts expects "rejection-proof-unavailable" after `writeFileSync(ended.payload.rejection.path, "{}")` and covers real Git atomic rejection. If Git pushes follow the memory-only rule, the spec defines no projected Git ref-status record, so newly rejected pushes cannot keep a definitive rejection proof. The spec gives no other way to settle them, so they would stay pending forever. If Git pushes keep the file-backed proof, that breaks "Never redirect these raw streams" for every owned mutation command. T78 plans "memory-only mutation capture" and "projected HTTP rejection evidence" but has no test for a new Git rejection format. The Test Plan only says "Preserve Git rejection verification".
PROPOSAL: State the Git rule explicitly in the capture paragraph. Option A: `git push` also drains through bounded memory pipes and retains a versioned projected porcelain ref-status record (per-ref status, ref names, command/attempt linkage, digest, limits). It uses the same hash-linked journal path as the HTTP projection, and the reader for legacy file-backed Git proofs stays in place. Add T78 and AC-05 cases for a new projected Git rejection, tampered or incomplete projected Git proof, and legacy file-backed Git proof compatibility. Option B: limit memory-only capture to HTTP/gh mutation responses, say that the bounded Git porcelain stdout file stays as retained rejection evidence, and explain why it holds no raw response body, credential or secret-bearing header.

## Sources

- [Git revision traversal](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-list)
- [GitHub workflow runs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs) and [GitHub CLI run listing](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_run_list) identify native run status and conclusion values used in quiescence records.

- [curl manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) documents transfer/connection limits, disabled retries and `-q`. The installer makes one bounded attempt per route and excludes user curl configuration.

- [Earlier pinned feature-document standard](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/feature-documents.md) and [template](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/feature-document-template.md): one child document, owner decisions, review and status gates.
- [Earlier pinned documentation conventions](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/documentation.md), [delivery rules](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/delivery.md) and [prose guide](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/humanize-prose.md): publication boundaries, authoritative local rules and concise owner-facing prose.
- [Earlier pinned agent entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/AGENTS.md): source-grounded task routing and retained consumer instructions.
- Stackweaver files cited in Context were inspected locally at `d77259358c87aebc93ad76231ae245fd790c043a`; private report bodies, application source and secrets are not copied into this draft.

- [GitHub local plugin directories](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-sdk/features/plugin-directories), [CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference) and [programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference), consulted 2026-11-05: explicit plugin loading, isolated configuration and update controls. Local CLI help confirms `--plugin-dir`, `COPILOT_HOME` and automatic-update controls; actual new-operation loading remains a required test.
- [GitHub Actions artifacts REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/artifacts): artifact IDs, expiry/digest/run metadata, archive retrieval and Actions read permission for private resources used by the proposed history reader.
- [GitHub Actions workflow runs REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs): native workflow/event/branch/head/attempt metadata used to distinguish adopted scheduled producer runs from other workflows and pull-request runs.
- [yauzl 3.4.0 source](https://github.com/thejoshwolfe/yauzl/tree/f5798e15204ffbb9a428b00217e4659684a41b0f): exact tagged ZIP-library source selected for bundling; its documented buffer/lazy-entry/filename/size-validation APIs support the proposed bounded wrapper. Package installation and integrity remain implementation verification.

- [Official yauzl 3.4.0 npm metadata](https://registry.npmjs.org/yauzl/3.4.0) and [Bun 1.4.4 release](https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/releases/tag/bun-v1.4.4). T10 records registry integrity, exact upstream-source comparison and verified Linux build-tool execution.

- [GitHub workflow jobs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs). T12 uses the fixed run-attempt jobs endpoint and native step outcomes under the existing Actions read permission. The run API examples also document branch-qualified workflow paths, which are checked against the recorded default branch or exact native head.

- [Current engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/AGENTS.md) and [owner-summary guidance](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/conventions/feature-documents.md). T40 integrates the published upstream pin and adds a summary and glossary of the accepted behavior.

GitHub documents that the actual `merge_commit_sha` after a merge identifies the resulting merge commit, squashed commit or rebased default tip. T51 reads that identity separately from a hypothetical pre-merge test commit. [GitHub pull-request REST documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/pulls#get-a-pull-request)

Git documents all-or-none ref updates for an atomic push and failure when the server lacks that capability. T52 verifies those outcomes against an owned local receive-pack server. [Git push documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push)

- [GitHub labels REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/labels#get-a-label) provides the repository label-identity lookup used to compare native canonical spelling. Read-only GETs for `cli/cli` labels `bug` and `BUG` returned the same label ID and canonical name during implementation.

- [Git reference reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/refs#get-a-reference) and [Git commit object reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/commits#get-a-commit-object) use repository Contents read permission. Native ref absence rechecks the current publication repository identity, default ref and native commit, retaining those requests before classifying visible absence.

- Bun's archive API provides in-memory regular-file reads and tar extraction. [Bun archive documentation](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive).
- Copilot exposes explicit configuration/cache locations and update suppression for scoped probes. [Copilot CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference), [Copilot programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference).
- Claude exposes separate configuration and update controls for the version-only probe. [Claude Code environment variables](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars).

The bounded client-distribution reader uses Bun for regular tar files and the runtime zlib API for capped gzip expansion. It validates ustar metadata separately so omitted entries cannot become an accepted package. Extended archive formats require an explicitly supported setup path. [Bun archive reference](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive), [Node zlib options](https://nodejs.org/api/zlib.html#class-options)

Non-executing client upgrade signals use the documented provider npm package names as intake identifiers. Runtime selection continues to use independently pinned native distributions. [Copilot CLI installation](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/get-started/cli-quickstart), [Claude Code setup](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup), [Codex source installation guide](https://github.com/openai/codex#installation)
